OFFICIAL PART

ACTS OF THE GOVERNMENT OF THE TOGOLESE REPUBLIC

LAWS, ORDINANCES, DECREES, ORDERS AND DECISIONS

LAW

LAW NO. 2015-010 OF 24 NOVEMBER 2015
enacting new penal code

The National Assembly has deliberated and adopted;

The President of the Republic promulgates the law the content of which follows:

### TITLE I: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Chapter I: Of the offence

##### Section 1: Of criminal law

###### Article premier

Criminal offences are classified, according to their gravity, into crimes, délits and contraventions.

1) crimes are punished by criminal penalties;

2) délits are punished by correctional penalties;

3) contraventions are punished by police penalties.

###### Art. 2

The law determines crimes and délits and fixes the penalties applicable to their perpetrators.

Regulations determine contraventions and fix, within the limits and according to the distinctions established by law, the penalties applicable to offenders.

###### Art. 3

No one may be prosecuted or punished for a crime or for a délit whose elements are not defined by law, or for a contravention whose elements are not defined by regulation.

No one may be punished by a penalty that is not provided for by law, if the offence is a crime or a délit, or by regulation, if the offence is a contravention.

No one may be subjected to security measures that are not defined by law.

###### Art. 4: Criminal provisions are to be strictly interpreted

When a criminal provision is ambiguous, its interpretation shall be made in favour of the person who is the subject of prosecution.

###### Art. 5

Criminal courts are competent to interpret administrative acts, regulatory or individual, and to assess their legality when the solution of the criminal proceedings before them depends on it. This interpretation is valid only for the case at hand.

##### Section 2: Of the application of criminal law in space

###### Art. 6

Togolese criminal law is applicable to any offence committed on Togolese territory, including:

1) the land area delimited by the borders of Togo;
2) the territorial waters of Togo;
3) the airspace above the land area and territorial waters of Togo;
4) vessels and aircraft registered in accordance with Togolese legislation or fixed platforms located on the continental shelf of Togo.

###### Art. 7

Togolese criminal law is not applicable to offences committed on board foreign military vessels navigating or stationed in Togolese territorial waters.

Togolese criminal law does not apply to an offence committed on board by a crew member or passenger of a foreign vessel or aircraft, to the detriment of another crew member or passenger within territorial waters or Togolese airspace, except in the following cases:

1) the intervention of Togolese authorities has been requested;
2) the offence has disturbed public order;
3) the perpetrator or victim of the offence is Togolese.

###### Art. 8

An offence is deemed to have been committed in Togo if at least part of the acts that constitute it or the facts of complicity in the principal action were carried out in Togo or if damage resulting from this offence was suffered in whole or in part on Togolese territory.

An attempt is deemed to have been committed at the place where the act constituting the commencement of execution within the meaning of article 46 of this code is committed.

Togolese criminal law is applicable to anyone who has been guilty on the territory of the Republic, as an accomplice to a crime or délit committed abroad if, and unless otherwise provided by this code, the crime or délit is punished both by Togolese law and foreign law and if it has been established by a final decision of the foreign court.

###### Art. 9

Togolese criminal law is applicable to any crime committed abroad by a Togolese national.

It is also applicable to any délit committed abroad by a Togolese national if the act is also punished by the law of the country where it was committed, unless otherwise provided by this code.

The same applies if the accused acquired Togolese nationality only after the prosecuted act.

Prosecution may only be initiated by request of the public prosecutor upon complaint by the victim or denunciation of the facts by the authority of the country where they were committed.

###### Art. 10

Togolese criminal law is applicable to any crime as well as to any délit committed by a Togolese national or by a foreigner outside the territory of the Republic when the victim is of Togolese nationality at the time of the offence.

###### Art. 11

Togolese criminal law also applies to foreigners who, outside the territory of the Republic, have been guilty as perpetrators or accomplices of offences against the security of the State, of counterfeiting the seal of the State, of counterfeiting currency, when they have been able to be arrested in Togo or duly extradited.

##### Section 3: Of the application of criminal law in time

###### Art. 12

Any new criminal provision less severe than the old one benefits the perpetrators of prior offences not yet tried on the day of its entry into force.

When the new law is more severe than the old one, offences committed before the entry into force of the new criminal provision continue to be tried in accordance with the old law.

###### Art. 13: Are subject to immediate effect upon their entry into force

However, when the law so provides, there is a délit in case of deliberate endangerment of the person of another.

1) laws of jurisdiction and judicial organization as long as a judgment on the merits has not been rendered at first instance;

2) laws setting the modalities of prosecution and the forms of procedure;

3) laws relating to the regime of execution and application of penalties; however, these laws, when they have the result of making more severe the penalties pronounced by the conviction decision, are applicable only to convictions pronounced for acts committed after their entry into force;

4) when prescriptions have not been acquired, laws relating to the prescription of public prosecution and to the prescription of penalties, except when they have the result of aggravating the situation of the person concerned.

###### Art. 14

Any new penal provision providing for a security measure is immediately applicable to offences that have not been the subject of a final conviction, even in the case where the former legislation provided for the application of a penalty in place of the security measure.

Excluding a conviction resulting from a default judgment, a conviction is final when it results from a decision that is not or is no longer subject on the part of the public prosecutor, the convicted person or the civil party to an ordinary or extraordinary remedy.

Remedies are subject to the formal rules in force on the day they are exercised.

#### Chapitre II : De la responsabilité pénale

##### Section 1: Des dispositions générales

###### Art. 15

One can only be criminally liable for one's own acts, actions or omissions.

###### Art. 16 : Est auteur de l'infraction la personne qui

1) materially commits the incriminated acts or uses a criminally irresponsible person to commit them or knowingly compels another to commit them;
2) attempts to commit a crime or, in cases provided for by law, an offence.

###### Art. 17 : Il n'y a point de crime ou de délit sans intention de le commettre

There is also an offence, when the law so provides, in case of fault of imprudence, negligence or breach of an obligation of prudence or safety provided for by law or regulation, if it is established that the perpetrator of the acts did not exercise normal diligence taking into account, where applicable, the nature of his missions or functions, his competences as well as the power and means at his disposal.

In the case provided for by the preceding paragraph, natural persons who did not directly cause the damage, but who created or contributed to creating the situation that allowed the damage to occur or who did not take measures to avoid it, are criminally liable, if it is established that they either manifestly and deliberately violated a particular obligation of prudence or safety provided for by law or regulation, or committed a characterised fault that exposed others to a risk of particular gravity that they could not ignore.

There is no contravention in case of force majeure.

##### Section 2 : Des faits justificatifs

###### Art. 18 : Les faits justificatifs sont

1) self-defence;

2) order of the law and command of legitimate authority; 3) state of necessity.

###### Paragraphe 1 : De la légitime défense

###### Art. 19

A person is not criminally liable who, faced with an unjustified attack against himself or others, commits, at the same time, an act required by the necessity of self-defence or defence of others, unless there is disproportion between the means of defence employed and the gravity of the attack.

###### Art. 20

A person is not criminally liable who, to interrupt the commission of a crime or offence against property, commits an act of defence, other than voluntary homicide, when this act is strictly necessary for the purpose pursued provided that the means employed are proportionate to the gravity of the offence.

###### Art. 21

Is presumed to have acted in a state of self-defence the person who commits the offence:

1) to repel, by night, entry by climbing, breaking and entering, violence or ruse into an inhabited place, into a shop, into an operating building or into any other dependency of a rented place;

2) to defend himself against the perpetrators of thefts or pillaging carried out with violence.

###### Paragraphe 2 : De l'ordre de la loi et du commandement de l'autorité légitime

###### Art. 22

A person is not criminally liable who commits an act prescribed or authorised by legislative or regulatory provisions.

###### Art. 23

A person is not criminally liable who commits an act ordered by legitimate authority, unless this act is manifestly illegal.

###### Paragraphe 3 : De l'état de nécessité

###### Art. 24

A person is not criminally liable who, faced with a present or imminent danger that threatens him or threatens others or property, commits an act necessary for his safety, that of others or of the property, unless there is disproportion between the means employed and the gravity of the threat.

##### Section 3 : Des causes d'irresponsabilité pénale

###### Art. 25 : Les causes d'irresponsabilité pénale sont

1) impairment of mental faculties;

2) duress;

3) mistake.

###### Paragraphe 1 : De l'altération des facultés mentales

###### Art. 26

A person is not criminally liable who was suffering, at the time of the acts, from a psychic or neuropsychic disorder having abolished his discernment or control of his acts.

A person who was suffering, at the time of the acts, from a psychic or neuropsychic disorder having impaired his discernment or hindered the control of his acts remains punishable; however, the court takes this circumstance into account when it determines the penalty and sets its regime.

###### Paragraphe 2 : De la contrainte

###### Art. 27

There is no criminal liability when the perpetrator of the offence acted under duress or a force to which he could not resist.

###### Paragraphe 3 : De l'erreur

###### Art. 28

There is no criminal liability when the perpetrator of the offence justifies having believed, by a mistake of law that he was not in a position to avoid, that he could lawfully accomplish the act.

##### Section 4 : Des causes d'atténuation de la responsabilité pénale

###### Art. 29 : Les causes d'atténuation de la responsabilité pénale sont

1) absolving excuses;

2) mitigating excuses;

3) minority;

4) mitigating circumstances.

###### Paragraphe 1 : Des excuses absolutoires

###### Art. 30

Constitutes an absolving excuse any cause limitatively provided for and defined by law and whose admission, without causing the offence to disappear, results in dispensation or exemption from penalty.

###### Art. 31 : Les excuses absolutoires sont

1) the excuse of the informer having participated as perpetrator or accomplice in offences against State security, in false testimonies, in counterfeiting of State seals, stamps or banknotes, in criminal groups, in destruction by explosives;

2) the excuse of repentant rebels benefiting those who, having been part of an armed gang without exercising any command therein and without fulfilling any employment or function, withdrew at the first warning from civil or military authorities or surrendered to these authorities;

3) the discretionary excuse for instigators or organisers of an unlawful assembly, as well as for participants in this assembly.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Des excuses atténuantes

###### Art. 32

Constitutes a mitigating excuse any cause limitatively provided for and defined by law and whose admission, without causing the offence to disappear, results in a mandatory mitigation of the penalty incurred.

###### Art. 33 : L'homicide et les violences volontaires sont excusables

1) if they were provoked by serious violence or threats against persons;

2) if they were committed in repelling the climbing and breaking and entering of an inhabited place or its dependencies;

3) if they were committed by a spouse on his spouse and the latter's accomplice at the moment when he surprised them in flagrante delicto of adultery.

###### Art. 34

When a fact of mitigating excuse is established, the principal penalties incurred are reduced as follows:

1) if it is a crime, the penalty cannot exceed five (05) years of imprisonment;

2) if it is an offence, the penalty cannot exceed six (06) months.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Criminal minority

###### Art. 35

Children under the age of fourteen (14) years are not criminally responsible.

The children's judge and the children's court may take, upon requisition of the public prosecutor, measures of protection, assistance and education with respect to them, including:

1) placing the child, for the duration they determine, in an educational, vocational training or care institution;

2) returning the child to his or her parents or to a trustworthy person by placing him or her for the duration determined under a supervised liberty regime;

3) admonishing the child by indicating a reparative act to be performed;

4) imposing a fine in relation to the resources of the child and his or her parents which may not, in any case, exceed half the rate of the fine applicable for the offence prosecuted against an adult accused.

The duration of the measure of protection, assistance and education taken may not extend beyond one (01) year after the child reaches the age of majority.

If the offence is classified as a felony or if the child is in a state of recidivism after having benefited from measures of protection, assistance and education, the children's court may, by a specially reasoned decision, impose a sentence of imprisonment against the child having exceeded the age of fourteen (14) years on the day of judgment, without this sentence being able to exceed half the maximum applicable to adult offenders or exceed a total of ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Paragraphe 4 : Mitigating circumstances

###### Art. 36

In the application of criminal law, the judge takes into account the necessities of public order, the particular circumstances of the case, the personality of the accused and, where applicable, his or her family responsibilities and his or her integration into the social environment.

###### Art. 37

When the judge grants the benefit of mitigating circumstances, except in cases where the law formally excludes them, the principal sentence is reduced as follows:

1) up to half of the sentence of imprisonment incurred for felonies punishable by fixed-term imprisonment, without exceeding twenty (20) years of imprisonment;

2) up to six (06) months of imprisonment if the misdemeanour is punishable by at least three (03) years of imprisonment;

3) up to the minimum of police penalties for other misdemeanours.

###### Art. 38

The benefit of mitigating circumstances allows the substitution of a fine for the sentence of imprisonment and the waiver of the fine when the law provides for its application cumulatively with imprisonment.

##### Section 5 : Cumulation of offences

###### Art. 39 : No one may be punished twice (02) for the same acts

When several felonies and misdemeanours are the subject of the same prosecution and have been committed by the same person who was not in a state of recidivism, the sentences incurred for each of these offences are not cumulated.

Only the strongest sentences and security measures are imposed.

###### Art. 40

When a person is the subject of several prosecutions, the judge hearing the second prosecution may order the merger of the principal sentences. In this case, the principal sentences imposed for the offence that was most severely punished are executed.

If the judge fails to rule on the merger or if, having been petitioned by the convicted person by an application subsequent to the decision rendered, he or she refuses to grant it, the principal sentences are executed cumulatively without being able to exceed in total the maximum sentence incurred for the most severely punished act.

###### Art. 41

Complementary sentences and security measures are executed cumulatively when they are identical and temporary; the actual total may exceed the temporary maximum provided by law for acts classified as felonies.

If the complementary sentences and security measures are incompatible, either with one or the other of the principal sentences imposed, or with each other, they are executed in the order indicated by the judge, unless the law provides otherwise.

For the application of paragraph 1 of this article, account is taken of sentences resulting from commutations by way of pardon, to the exclusion of sentences originally imposed.

###### Art. 42

When the same act is susceptible to several classifications and in the case where the offences committed include distinct constituent elements, this act may be submitted to the judge under its different classifications. However, it may give rise to only a single prosecution and be sanctioned under the highest classification.

##### Section 6 : Recidivism

###### Art. 43

Is in a state of recidivism any individual convicted of a felony or misdemeanour who commits a new felony of the same category as the first within a period of ten (10) years or a new misdemeanour of the same category within a period of three (03) years, the period to be counted from the day the first conviction became final.

###### Art. 44

Offences are classified into categories according to whether they harm:

1) persons; 2) the family; 3) morals; 4) property; 5) the authority of the State; 6) public peace; 7) the public treasury, the public domain or the national economy; 8) the security of the State.

###### Art. 45

In case of recidivism, the maximum of fines and custodial sentences, including the maximum of fixed-term imprisonment, is doubled.

In the case where the law expressly provides for it, any prior conviction, imposed in a foreign State, may be taken into consideration to establish recidivism.

In case of concurrence between recidivism and an aggravating circumstance, the latter takes precedence over recidivism. Recidivism is applicable to contraventions only by virtue of particular provisions of law or regulation.

##### Section 7 : Participation in the offence

###### Paragraphe 1 : Attempt

###### Art. 46

The attempt of a felony or misdemeanour is punishable as the completed offence provided that it has been manifested by a commencement of execution, if it was suspended or failed to have its effect only by circumstances independent of the will of its author.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Co-perpetration and complicity

###### Art. 47

Is a co-perpetrator of an offence one who, without personally accomplishing the incriminated act, participates with the perpetrator and in agreement with him or her in its commission. The absence in an individual of a quality or circumstance personally necessary for the commission of an offence does not prevent the quality of co-perpetrator, when with full knowledge and will, he or she associates himself or herself with its commission.

###### Art. 48 : Are considered accomplices to the offence those who, knowingly, have

1) given information or instructions to commit it or provoked its commission by using gifts, promises, threats, abuse of authority or power, machination or culpable artifices;

2) procured instruments, weapons, vehicles or any other means useful for the preparation, the commission of the offence or to favour the impunity of its perpetrators;

3) aided or assisted knowingly, directly or indirectly, the perpetrators or co-perpetrators of the offence in the acts that prepared, facilitated or completed it.

###### Art. 49

Any co-perpetrator or accomplice to a felony or misdemeanour is equally criminally responsible for any offence the commission of which was a foreseeable consequence of the concerted action or complicity.

###### Art. 50

If several perpetrators act together and in concert, they are each liable to the sentences sanctioning the offence committed. None of them may avail himself or herself of the exceptions, excuses or immunities of the other.

###### Art. 51

The co-perpetrators or accomplices to a felony or misdemeanour are punished with the same sentences and the same security measures as the principal perpetrator of that felony or misdemeanour, except when the law provides otherwise.

###### Art. 52

Any individual who, knowingly and unequivocally, incites a third party by one of the means listed in Article 48 to commit a crime or an offence, is punished as the perpetrator of that crime or offence, even if it has not been attempted or completed.

The same applies to anyone who organizes or directs the commission of the offence.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Des infractions commises par les personnes morales

###### Art. 53

Except for special or derogatory provisions, legal persons excluding the State, are criminally liable for offences provided for by this code, committed on their behalf by their organs or representatives.

The reference to legal persons in this code always means Togolese or foreign legal persons.

However, territorial authorities and their groupings are criminally liable only for offences committed in the exercise of activities that may be the subject of public service delegation agreements, activities relating to the award of public contracts or acts of public finance management.

The criminal liability of legal persons does not exclude that of natural persons who are perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts, subject to the provisions of Article 17 paragraph 4 of this code.

The legal person in question may, by reasoned decision, be declared jointly and severally liable or not with the convicted person(s) for payment of all or part of the fines, costs and expenses to the State as well as civil damages.

###### Art. 54 : Les peines applicables aux personnes morales sont

1) l'amende qui peut être portée au quintuple de celle encourue par les personnes physiques ou à trois cent millions (300.000.000) de francs CFA si aucune amende n'est prévue ;

2) l'exclusion temporaire ou définitive des marchés publics ou du bénéfice des aides publiques octroyées par l'Etat togolais ou des organisations internationales étatiques ou non étatiques, ou la perte et le remboursement des avantages accordés en application des lois et règlements en vigueur lorsque ces avantages ont été obtenus frauduleusement par la commission d'infractions réprimées en application du présent code ;

3) la fermeture temporaire ou définitive des établissements ou de l'un des établissements de la personne morale ayant servi à commettre les faits incriminés, qui se substitue à l'emprisonnement ;

4) la confiscation spéciale prévue à l'article 117 du présent code ou la confiscation générale prévue à l'article 69 du présent code ;

5) la fermeture temporaire ou définitive de la succursale d'une personne morale étrangère, ayant servi à commettre les faits incriminés ;

6) l'interdiction temporaire ou définitive de procéder à une offre au public de titres et financements ou de faire admettre ses titres financiers aux négociations d'un marché réglementé ; 7) la dissolution, lorsque la personne morale a été créée ou détournée de son objet pour commettre les faits incriminés.

Except as otherwise provided by the laws in force, the penalties applicable to legal persons are determined by this code.

###### Art. 55

Members of administrative, management and executive bodies, de jure or de facto, who have been the instruments of the offence committed by the company may be disqualified, for up to five (05) years, from the right to direct, administer or manage a company.

###### Art. 56

Any conviction to a penalty is published in the official gazette, in a legal notices journal and in a daily newspaper with national circulation at the expense of the convicted legal person.

The court seized may also order, at the expense of any convicted legal person, the posting or publication in the written press or by any means of electronic communication to the public of extracts or copies of convictions, when the public prosecutor has specifically requested the conviction due to the exemplary nature of the case.

###### Paragraphe 4 : Des infractions commises en bande organisée

###### Art. 57

An organized gang within the meaning of the law constitutes any grouping or any agreement, de facto or de jure, formed by any means whatsoever, with a view to the preparation or commission, characterized by one or more material or legal acts, of one or more offences.

Offences committed by an organized gang are punished by doubling the penalties incurred for these offences, except for provisions specifically provided for the punishment of these offences.

#### Chapitre III : Des alternatives aux poursuites pénales

###### Art. 58 : Les alternatives aux poursuites pénales sont

1) la médiation pénale ;

2) la composition pénale.

##### Section 1 : De la médiation pénale

###### Art. 59

Mediation is a mechanism that aims to conclude a conciliation between the perpetrator of an offence or their legal representative and the victim or their legal representative or their successors in title. Its objective is to stop the effects of criminal proceedings, to ensure reparation for the damage caused to the victim, to put an end to the disturbance resulting from the offence and to contribute to the rehabilitation of the perpetrator of the offence.

###### Art. 60

The offences, conditions and procedures for recourse to criminal mediation are determined by the code of criminal procedure.

##### Section 2 : De la composition pénale

###### Art. 61

The public prosecutor, as long as the public prosecution has not been initiated, may propose, directly or through an authorized person, a criminal composition to a natural person who acknowledges having committed one or more offences punishable as a principal penalty by a fine or a term of imprisonment, as well as, where applicable, one or more related contraventions.

###### Art. 62

The offences, conditions and procedures for recourse to criminal composition are determined by the code of criminal procedure.

#### Chapitre IV : Des peines

##### Section 1 : Des dispositions générales

###### Art. 63

No one may be sentenced to penalties other than those provided for by law.

The effect of the penalty on the social reintegration of the convicted person is taken into account.

The measures for adjusting criminal and correctional sentences are determined by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Art. 64

In case of concurrence of causes of aggravation and mitigation of the penalty, the maximum and minimum penalties incurred are fixed by taking into account successively:

1) les circonstances aggravantes inférieures à la réalisation de l'infraction ; 2) les circonstances aggravantes inhérentes à la personnalité de l'auteur de l'infraction ; 3) les excuses atténuantes inhérentes à la réalisation de l'infraction ;

4) les excuses atténuantes inhérentes à la personnalité de l'auteur de l'infraction ; 5) l'état de récidive.

###### Art. 65

In case of concurrence between aggravating circumstances and mitigating excuses, priority must be given first to aggravating circumstances, then to the mitigating excuse, and finally to recidivism.

In case of concurrence between mitigating circumstances and aggravating circumstances relating to recidivism from correctional penalty to correctional penalty, the penalty must first be reduced by application of mitigating circumstances and then recidivism from correctional penalty to correctional penalty must be applied.

In case of concurrence between recidivism and an aggravating circumstance, the aggravating circumstance must be applied before recidivism, which excludes the application of mitigating circumstances and even of suspended sentence.

##### Section 2 : Des peines criminelles

###### Art. 66 : Les peines criminelles sont

1) la réclusion à temps ; 2) la confiscation générale ; 3) la déchéance civique.

###### Paragraphe 1 : De la réclusion à temps

###### Art. 67

Fixed-term imprisonment is a criminal penalty of five (05) to fifty (50) years.

###### Art. 68

Imprisonment for life is served in a penitentiary facility designed for long sentences.

A decree of the Council of Ministers determines the working conditions of life prisoners and the disciplinary regime of the facility.

During their detention, life prisoners are subject to civic forfeiture and civil incapacity. Their property is managed by a guardian as that of a minor.

###### Paragraphe 2 : General confiscation

###### Art. 69

General confiscation applies to all or a fraction of the totality of present assets composing the estate of the convicted person, after satisfaction of any rights of his or her spouse, co-heirs or joint heirs, without however affecting the rights of third parties over said property.

###### Art. 70

The reserved portion of heirs, calculated after settlement of liabilities, is exempt from confiscation, as are property declared unseizable by law, personal property of the spouse or children whose administration, management or disposal the convicted person had in fact or by virtue of law.

If the convicted person is married, confiscation applies only to his or her separate property and to his or her share in the division of community property or undivided property between his or her spouse and himself or herself.

###### Art. 71

Sale by auction of confiscated property is carried out if it is indispensable for division among the interested parties or required by the state property administration. Otherwise, land ownership titles are transferred to the state domain.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Civic forfeiture

###### Paragraphe 2 : Fine

###### Art. 75

A fine means the pecuniary penalty consisting in the obligation to pay to the public treasury a sum of money determined by law.

###### Art. 76

The amount of the fine is fixed by law for each offense that it defines and punishes. It is expressed in national currency. Payment is made according to the procedures determined by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

In the event of non-payment of the fine within the prescribed period, imprisonment may be substituted for the fine at the rate of one day of imprisonment for twenty-five thousand (25,000) CFA francs of fine, by order of incarceration of the public prosecutor. The convicted person may obtain his or her release by paying the balance of the fine not covered by the substitute incarceration.

###### Art. 72

Civic forfeiture includes the loss of any public mandate, incapacity to serve in the civil service, to exercise civic and political rights, to wear decorations, to obtain a weapon carrying permit, a hunting permit, to be awarded a public contract, to be a juror, expert, arbitrator or to take an oath as a witness, to teach or to occupy an educational position in a public or private institution.

##### Section 3 : Correctional penalties

###### Paragraphe 3 : Movable property confiscation

###### Art. 77

Movable property confiscation means the seizure of one or more movable items or movable effects belonging to the convicted person, for the benefit of the public treasury.

###### Art. 78

The judge may substitute for the fine or the imprisonment penalty of a duration less than three (03) months the confiscation of one or more movable items or movable effects belonging to the convicted person for the benefit of the public treasury.

###### Art. 73 : Correctional penalties are

###### Paragraphe 4 : Prohibition of rights

1) imprisonment;

2) fine;

3) movable property confiscation;

4) prohibition of rights;

5) community service work;

6) day-fine.

###### Paragraphe 1 : Imprisonment

###### Art. 74

Imprisonment means the detention of an individual inside a penitentiary facility.

Imprisonment is a correctional penalty of one (01) day to five (05) years.

A decree of the Council of Ministers determines the conditions of supervision and employment of convicted persons; the procedures for correspondence and visits by their families, and the control of the management of their property.

###### Art. 79

Prohibition of rights is a judicial measure that temporarily deprives a convicted person of the ability to exercise certain rights, certain activities or certain functions.

###### Art. 80

The judge may as a complementary penalty pronounce against the author of the offense the temporary prohibition of the exercise of certain of his or her civil, civic or professional rights. Unless otherwise provided, this prohibition may not exceed five (05) years from the day the penalty becomes enforceable.

###### Art. 81

The prohibition may in particular apply to the right to exercise a profession, an elective public function, to be a director or manager of a company or association, to be a guardian, substitute guardian of an incapacitated person, to obtain or use a hunting or fishing permit, a weapon carrying permit, to vote in political or union ballots, to be heard under oath in court or before a public officer.

###### Paragraphe 5 : Community service work

###### Art. 82

Community service work is a correctional penalty requiring the convicted person to work, for a determined duration without receiving remuneration, for the benefit of either a legal person under public law, or a legal person under private law charged with a public service mission or an authorized association to implement community service work.

###### Art. 83

The penalty of community service work may only be pronounced if the accused is present and consents to it, and when the offense he or she has committed is punishable by a penalty of imprisonment. Its duration is between sixty-four (64) and two thousand four hundred ninety-six (2496) hours.

The judge, before pronouncing the judgment, informs the accused of his or her right to refuse to perform community service work and receives his or her response.

The procedures for organizing and implementing community service work are defined by decree of the Council of Ministers.

###### Art. 84

The rights-restrictive penalties listed in articles 79, 80 and 81 of this code may not be pronounced cumulatively with the penalty of community service work.

The penalty of community service work may not be pronounced cumulatively with the fine.

###### Paragraphe 6 : Day-fine

###### Art. 85

The day-fine is the correctional penalty that requires the convicted person to pay to the public treasury a sum whose total amount results from the determination by the judge of a daily contribution, for a certain number of days, and which may be pronounced in place of the imprisonment penalty or cumulatively.

The procedures for execution of the day-fine are defined by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

##### Section 4

Complementary penalties common to criminal and correctional penalties.

###### Art. 86

Complementary penalties are penalties that may be added to the principal penalty when the law has provided for them and the judge pronounces them.

Prohibition of residence, temporary prohibition of the exercise of certain civic, civil or professional rights, closure of establishment, publication of the conviction and special confiscation of the corpus delicti, its proceeds

or things intended to commit it are complementary penalties common to criminal and correctional matters.

In all cases, confiscation of weapons, objects and instruments having served to commit a crime or offense may be pronounced by the court seized.

##### Section 5 : Security Period

###### Art. 87

When the court pronounces a sentence, not accompanied by suspension, the duration of which is equal to or greater than ten (10) years, the convicted person may not benefit during a security period from the provisions concerning the suspension or splitting of the sentence, external placement, leave permits, semi-liberty and conditional release.

###### Art. 88 : The duration of the security period is half of the sentence

The court may however, by special decision, extend this duration up to two thirds (2/3) of the sentence or decide to reduce it.

###### Art. 89

When the court pronounces a sentence of a duration exceeding five (05) years, not accompanied by suspension, it may fix a security period during which the convicted person may not benefit from any of the methods of execution of the sentence mentioned in article 95 of this code.

###### Art. 90

Sentence reductions granted during the security period are only credited against the part of the sentence exceeding this duration.

##### Section 6 : Police Penalties

###### Art. 91 : Police penalties are

1) community service work which may not exceed forty (40) hours or five (05) days at the rate of eight (08) hours per day;

2) a fine which may not exceed one hundred and fifty thousand (150,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 92

In the absence of payment of the police fine within the period of formal notice, community service work may be substituted for the fine at the rate of one day of community service work for ten thousand (10,000) CFA francs of fine.

##### Section 7 : Prior Admission of Guilt

##### Section 9 : Extinction of Sentences and Erasure of Convictions

###### Art. 93

The Public Prosecutor may, on his own initiative or at the request of the accused who acknowledges the facts alleged against him, or of his lawyer, resort to the prior admission of guilt procedure.

###### Art. 94

The conditions, offences, and methods of resorting to the prior admission of guilt procedure are determined by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Art. 101

The causes of extinction of sentences and erasure of convictions are:

1) amnesty; 2) pardon; 3) prescription; 4) rehabilitation; 5) death of the convicted person.

##### Section 8 : Substitutes for Correctional Penalties

###### Paragraphe 1 : Amnesty

###### Art. 95 : Substitutes for correctional penalties are

1) exemption from penalty; 2) suspension; 3) adjournment.

###### Paragraphe 1 : Exemption from Penalty

###### Art. 102

Amnesty extinguishes the public action. It erases all convictions pronounced and puts an end to all sentences and measures except confinement in a health institution and confiscation, a police measure.

Amnesty is not applicable to costs if the conviction is final. It does not entail:

###### Art. 96

When the accused has, before judgment, ensured reparation of the damage caused by the offence, the judge may, considering the guarantees of reform presented, while declaring his guilt, exempt him from any penalty.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Suspension

###### Art. 97

Sentences of imprisonment and fines may be subject, in whole or in part, to a suspension of execution during a period fixed by the judge, which may not exceed three (03) years.

1) neither the restitution of fines and costs already paid, nor that of confiscations already executed;

2) neither reinstatement in public functions or employment, ranks, public or ministerial offices, nor the right to career reconstruction;

3) neither the restitution of decorations, nor reinstatement in national orders.

Amnesty does not prevent applications for review seeking to establish the innocence of the amnestied person. It has no effect on the civil action as well as on disciplinary proceedings and penalties.

###### Art. 98

In granting suspension, the judge may subject the convicted person to particular obligations and controls in accordance with the provisions of the code of criminal procedure.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Pardon

###### Art. 99

Suspension may be revoked by the judge, upon application of the public prosecutor under the conditions determined by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Adjournment

###### Art. 103

Pardon granted by decree of the President of the Republic entails only total or partial, definitive or conditional, dispensation from executing a sentence or security measure that has become final.

###### Art. 100

The judge may adjourn, according to the methods provided in the code of criminal procedure, the pronouncement of the sentence when it appears that the rehabilitation of the accused is in the process of being achieved, that the damage caused is in the process of being repaired and that the disturbance resulting from the offence will cease. In this case, he fixes in his decision the date on which the sentence is ruled upon.

When it is partial or conditional, it may in particular consist of granting the benefit of sentence adjustment measures provided for by the code of criminal procedure.

Pardon may not apply to confinement in a health institution or to special confiscation.

Adjournment may only be pronounced if the accused natural person or the representative of the accused legal person is present.

###### Art. 104

Pardon does not prevent the right, for the victim, to obtain reparation for the damage caused by the offence.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Prescription

###### Art. 105

The limitation period for criminal sentences is twenty-five (25) full years.

###### Art. 106 : The limitation period for other sentences is

1) five (05) full years for correctional sentences;
2) two (02) full years for contraventional sentences.

###### Art. 107 : The period runs from the day

1) when the conviction became final;
2) of the completion of the formalities provided for by the procedural laws in case of conviction by default.

The above rules apply to complementary penalties that are not executed automatically from the day when the conviction from which they result became final.

They also apply to temporary security measures that are not executed automatically from the day when the conviction from which they result is final.

In this case, the limitation period is twenty-five (25) years.

###### Art. 108

Obligations of a civil nature resulting from a final criminal decision are prescribed in accordance with the rules of the civil code.

###### Paragraphe 4 : Rehabilitation

###### Art. 109

Any person convicted by a Togolese court to a criminal, correctional or contraventional sentence may be rehabilitated.

The convicted person may benefit either from automatic rehabilitation under the conditions provided for by law and regulation for the benefit of natural or legal persons, or from judicial rehabilitation under the conditions provided for by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Art. 110

Rehabilitation erases the conviction and terminates for the future all incapacities and disqualifications resulting from it.

###### Paragraphe 5 : Death of the Convicted Person

###### Art. 111

The death of the convicted person does not prevent the action brought against his property, the execution of pecuniary convictions pronounced and confiscations ordered.

#### Chapter V: Security Measures

###### Art. 112: Security measures are

1) prohibition of residence;
2) special confiscation;
3) closure of establishment;
4) internment in a health facility;
5) preventive detention;
6) prohibition from appearing in certain places;
7) prohibition from the territory of the Republic;
8) prohibition from professional activity;
9) confiscation, police measure;
10) supervision and assistance;
11) good conduct bond;
12) house arrest with placement under electronic bracelet, the terms of which are determined by the code of criminal procedure.

##### Section 1: Prohibition of Residence

###### Art. 113

Prohibition of residence consists of the prohibition imposed on the convicted person from appearing in places the list of which is administratively notified to him.

The judgment may, in addition, accompany it with the supervision and assistance measures provided for in article 134 of this code.

###### Art. 114

Persons sentenced to criminal imprisonment or to a term of imprisonment of at least two (02) years may also be subject to a prohibition from residing in certain places determined by the conviction decision. This list may be supplemented by joint order of the Keeper of the Seals, Minister of Justice and the Minister responsible for security, which is notified to the convicted person at least eight (08) days before his release.

###### Art. 115

The prohibition of residence applies from the day the decision from which it results became final.

The duration of the prohibition of residence may not exceed ten (10) years for an act classified as a crime and five (05) years for an act classified as an offence.

###### Art. 116

Any convicted person who knowingly resides in a prohibited place without having obtained a special pass from the public prosecutor's office is punished by a term of imprisonment of two (02) months to one (01) year. In case of recidivism, these penalties are doubled.

##### Section 2: Special Confiscation

###### Art. 117

When the offence was committed with the aid of weapons, ammunition, explosives or any dangerous instrument or object or a regulated item, the judge orders the confiscation of the seized instrument, substance or object so that it may be, as the case may be, destroyed or handed over either to the military authority or to the administration authorized to use it.

###### Art. 118

The confiscation of movable and immovable property belonging to the convicted person is a mandatory additional penalty when they are the proceeds of the offence or when they were used to commit a crime.

It is a discretionary additional penalty in the cases provided for by law when they were used to commit an offence.

###### Art. 119

Property confiscated pursuant to this section is acquired by the State.

The domain administration proceeds with their sale in the forms prescribed for the sale of State property.

They remain encumbered up to the amount of their value by debts prior to the conviction.

##### Section 3: Closure of Establishment

###### Art. 120

The closure of a legal person, an establishment, a commercial, industrial or cultural center or any other space that was used to commit or facilitate the crime or offence may be ordered when the nature or gravity thereof and the interests of public order justify it.

When the closure of an establishment is pronounced, it entails a prohibition for the convicted person or for any tenant, manager or assignee on his behalf, from carrying on the same professional activity in the same premises, even under another name or under another corporate name.

###### Art. 121

The closure of a legal person or an establishment may be ordered for a duration that may not exceed five (05) years in the cases specifically provided for by laws or regulations.

Any activity carried on in defiance of this closure exposes its perpetrator to a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, to a term of imprisonment of two (02) months to one (01) year and to the doubling of the fine.

###### Art. 122

In the case where the closure of an establishment results in the dismissal of a staff member, the latter, except in case of conviction for co-perpetration or complicity, receives compensation equal to his salary increased by all benefits in kind calculated for the duration of the closure,

subject to the application of collective or individual agreements if they provide for higher compensation.

The compensation period is limited to six (06) months if the duration of the closure pronounced exceeds this period.

##### Section 4: Internment in a Health Facility

###### Art. 123

In case of dismissal, no case to answer, acquittal or discharge for one of the causes provided for in article 25 of this code, the judge orders the internment in a health facility of the perpetrator of the crime or offence, when a medical expert assessment establishes that his release is dangerous for himself or for others. This health internment may not be ordered without requisition from the public prosecutor's office.

The competent medical authority must, of its own motion or at the request of the sentence enforcement judge, at least every six (06) months, provide an opinion stating whether the internment measure is still necessary due to the danger that the person concerned presents to himself or to others. In the event that the release would no longer present any danger, the public prosecutor's office of the place of internment terminates it.

##### Section 5: Preventive Detention

###### Art. 124

When a recidivist is convicted as a habitual offender, the court seized, in addition to the application of article 45 of this code, may order against him preventive detention for a period of five (05) to twenty-five (25) years.

###### Art. 125

For the application of the preceding article, any recidivist is deemed a habitual offender who, taking into account the conviction pronounced for the new offence committed, is subject to:

1) two convictions for a crime either to the maximum term of criminal imprisonment, or to another custodial sentence;

2) one of the convictions provided for in the preceding point and two convictions for an offence to a custodial sentence;

3) four (04) convictions for offences to custodial sentences exceeding one (01) year.

###### Art. 126

Persons in preventive detention are held in special establishments. They are required to work. They may benefit from conditional release under the conditions provided for by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Art. 127

The offences that may justify preventive detention must have been committed within a period of ten (10) years, from the date of the last offence likely to result in detention.

No account is taken of convictions erased by amnesty or rehabilitation, nor of those pronounced against persons under eighteen (18) years of age at the time of commission of the acts.

When a prosecution is of a nature to result in preventive detention, preparatory investigation must be initiated. Counsel must be appointed for the accused, on pain of nullity of the proceedings, if he has not chosen one.

###### Art. 128

Security internment may not be ordered against women and individuals over sixty (60) years of age or under twenty-one (21) years of age at the expiration of the principal sentence originally imposed.

It is replaced in their case by a residence ban for a period of five (05) years or by the supervision and assistance regime provided for in Article 134 of this code.

Any person sentenced to security internment who reaches sixty (60) years of age automatically benefits, from that date, from the conversion of this measure into a residence ban for the remaining period, the duration of which may not exceed five (05) years.

###### Art. 129

For the application of this section, account is taken of the sentences originally imposed and not of sentences resulting from commutation measures that have occurred.

##### Section 6 : De l'interdiction de paraître en certains lieux

###### Art. 130

In cases of crime or offence, the court seized may, taking into account the seriousness of the facts and the danger presented, prohibit the convicted person from returning to the locality where his presence would be likely to disturb public order.

This prohibition may not exceed twenty (20) years for an act considered as a crime and ten (10) years for an act considered as an offence. It applies automatically from the day on which the conviction from which it results becomes final or from the day on which the sentence is time-barred.

##### Section 7 : De l'interdiction du territoire de la République

###### Art. 131

When a conviction is pronounced against a foreigner, the court seized may prohibit the convicted person from the entire territory of the Republic. The duration of the prohibition is:

1) five (05) to twenty (20) years for an act considered as a crime;

2) two (02) to five (05) years for an act considered as an offence.

###### Art. 132

The court seized may prohibit the territory of the Republic to any foreigner dangerous to public order, acquitted or beneficiary of a dismissal in accordance with the provisions of Article 26 of this code.

In case of impossibility of execution or until it becomes possible to execute it, the prohibition from the territory of the Republic is replaced by internment in a health facility. In the event that it is terminated in accordance with the provisions of Article 123, the provisions of the preceding article apply automatically.

##### Section 8 : De l'interdiction de l'activité professionnelle

###### Art. 133

The court seized may, in case of crime or offence, prohibit the exercise of a professional activity that enabled or facilitated the commission of the offence when the nature or seriousness thereof justifies it and the continuation of this profession or professional activity may give rise to fear of recidivism by the convicted person.

The duration of this prohibition is fixed by the court seized. It may not exceed ten (10) years in case of crime and five (05) years in case of offence. In case of recidivism, it may be pronounced for a duration of up to twenty (20) years in case of crime and ten (10) years in case of offence.

##### Section 9 : De la surveillance et de l'assistance

###### Art. 134

Any person sentenced to a custodial sentence with or without suspension exceeding two (02) years may, by reasoned decision, be placed for a period of up to five (05) years under a regime of assistance and supervision comprising general obligations and, where appropriate, special obligations.

The general obligations that automatically apply to the convicted person are as follows:

1) comply with summonses from the authority responsible for the assistance and supervision mission;

2) receive visits from this authority and communicate to it information or documents likely to enable verification of his means of subsistence;

3) notify the authority of changes of employment or residence and justify the reasons therefor;

4) notify the authority of any absence exceeding one month; 5) obtain its prior authorization before any travel abroad.

The observance of these obligations by the convicted person is exercised under the supervision of the sentence enforcement judge, in accordance with the provisions set out in the code of criminal procedure.

##### Section 10 : De la confiscation-mesure de police

###### Art. 135

Things whose manufacture, possession, transport, trade or use are unlawful are subject to confiscation for purposes of destruction or delivery to a hospital or research centre even if they do not belong to the convicted person or if the prosecution is not followed by conviction.

This confiscation may be pronounced, in the absence of any prosecution, upon requisition of the public prosecutor, by summary order.

##### Section 11 : De la caution de bonne conduite

###### Art. 136

When there are good reasons to fear that a person may commit a crime or offence, either because he engages in acts simply intended to prepare or make possible the offence, or because he utters serious threats, the court seized may require from him an express undertaking to behave properly and compel him for this purpose to provide sufficient security.

###### Art. 137

The undertaking is made for a period of one (01) to five (05) years. The security is given in the form of a bond or personal surety.

###### Art. 138

The court seized fixes the duration of the undertaking and the amount of security to be provided according to the nature, seriousness and more or less dangerous character of the preparatory acts carried out or threats uttered and the personal and material situation of the perpetrator or his guarantors.

###### Art. 139

The court competent to rule on these measures is that of the place of residence of the perpetrator or that of the place where the acts were carried out or the threats uttered. The public prosecutor seizes the competent court of its own motion or at the request of the threatened party.

###### Art. 140

If the person referred to in Article 136 is unable to provide the requested guarantee and this situation is not attributable to him, the court seized substitutes for said guarantee, for a period equal to that fixed for the undertaking, one or more of the measures provided for in Article 134 of this code.

In case of refusal, or if, in bad faith, he does not provide the promised guarantee within the fixed time limit, the court seized may compel him to do so by pronouncing against him, for a period equal to that fixed for the undertaking, a residence ban accompanied or not by one or more of the measures provided for in Article 134 of this code.

###### Art. 141

When the probationary period provided for by the undertaking has elapsed without the offence feared having been committed, the guarantees are lifted and the sums deposited are returned.

In the contrary case, the sums deposited are forfeited to the State without prejudice to the penalties and security measures for which the perpetrator of the offence is liable.

#### Chapter VI: Publication of Convictions

###### Art. 142

Apart from cases specifically provided for by law, the court seized may order at the expense of the convicted person the publication in the press of extracts or copies of convictions when the public prosecutor specifically requests it by reason of the exemplary nature of the case.

The court seized may furthermore, if it deems it appropriate, order the publication of the conviction in the official gazette or by posting in very conspicuous characters in public places for a period not exceeding two (02) months. Any person who has been the subject of an order of no case to answer, a decision of discharge or acquittal has the right to request the publication of that decision by the media. This publication must be requested from the judge of the decision who assesses its interest and appropriateness having regard to the media treatment previously reserved for the applicant. His refusal must be specially motivated.

### TITLE II: OFFENCES AGAINST PERSONS

#### Chapter I

The crime of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity and the crime of apartheid

##### Section 1: The crime of genocide

###### Article 143

The crime of genocide means any of the following acts, committed in time of peace or in time of war with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnic, racial, political or religious group, such as:

1) killing members of the group;

2) causing serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group;

3) deliberately inflicting on members of the group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction in whole or in part;

4) imposing measures intended to prevent births within the group;

5) forcibly transferring children of the group to another group.

###### Article 144

The offences listed in the preceding article are punished, by reason of the gravity of the offence and the personal situation of their perpetrator, by a sentence of imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 2: War crimes

###### Art. 145

A war crime means any of the following grave breaches, committed during a period of international armed conflict, when they target persons or property protected by the Geneva Conventions:

1) wilful killing;

2) torture or inhuman treatment, including biological experiments;

3) wilfully causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or health;

4) extensive destruction and appropriation of property, not justified by military necessity and carried out unlawfully and wantonly;

5) compelling a prisoner of war or other protected person to serve in the forces of a hostile Power;

6) wilfully depriving a prisoner of war or any other protected person of the rights of fair and regular trial;

7) unlawful deportation or transfer or unlawful confinement; 8) taking of hostages.

###### Art. 146

Also constitute war crimes, other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to international armed conflicts within the established framework of

international law, namely, any of the following acts:

1) compromising through an unjustified act or omission the life, health and physical and mental integrity of persons in the power of the adverse party or interned, detained or deprived of liberty by reason of a situation of armed conflict, in particular subjecting such persons to physical mutilations, medical or scientific experiments, removal of tissue or organs for transplants not justified by their state of health and not in accordance with generally accepted medical standards;

2) intentionally directing attacks against the civilian population as such or against civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

3) launching or directing an indiscriminate attack affecting a civilian population or civilian objects, knowing that such attack will incidentally cause loss of life, injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be manifestly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated;

4) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against property which is not a military objective; 5) launching an attack against works or installations containing dangerous forces, in particular dams, dykes, nuclear power stations, knowing that such attack will cause loss of life, injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects which are excessive;

6) attacking or bombarding, by whatever means, undefended localities and demilitarized zones, towns, villages, dwellings or buildings which are undefended and which therefore do not constitute military objectives;

7) killing or wounding a combatant who has laid down his arms, no longer has means of defence or has surrendered;

8) causing loss of life or serious injury by improper use of the flag of truce, the flag or military insignia and uniform of the enemy,

of the United Nations, of neutral States or of other States not party to the conflict, as well as the distinctive emblems provided for by the Geneva Conventions and their third additional protocol;

9) the transfer, directly or indirectly, by the occupying power of parts of its own civilian population into the territory it occupies, or the deportation or transfer within or outside the occupied territory of all or part of the population of that territory, unless, in the case of a transfer within the occupied territory, the security of civilians or imperative military reasons so require;

10) unjustifiably delaying the repatriation of prisoners of war or civilians;

11) directing attacks against historic monuments, works of art or places of worship which are clearly recognized as constituting the cultural or spiritual heritage of peoples and to which special protection has been given, provided that such property is not located in the immediate vicinity of military objectives, nor used by the adverse party in support of its military effort;

12) outrages upon personal dignity, in particular subjecting persons to inhuman and degrading practices based on racial, ethnic or sexual discrimination;

13) depriving a person protected by the Geneva Conventions and Protocol 1 of his right to a fair and impartial trial;

14) conscripting or enlisting children under the age of 18 years into the national armed forces or into armed groups or using them to participate actively in hostilities;

15) intentionally starving civilians as a method of warfare, by depriving them of objects indispensable to their survival, including intentionally impeding relief supplies as provided for under the Geneva Conventions;

16) committing rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence;

17) utilizing the presence of a civilian or other protected person to render certain points, areas or military forces immune from military operations;

18) employing asphyxiating, poisonous or other gases, and all analogous liquids, materials or devices;

19) employing poison or poisoned weapons;

20) pillaging a town or place, even when taken by assault;

21) declaring abolished, suspended or inadmissible in a court of law the rights and actions of nationals of the adverse party;

22) declaring that no quarter will be given; 23) killing or wounding treacherously individuals

belonging to the hostile nation or army; 24) destroying or seizing the enemy's property, unless such destruction or seizure be imperatively demanded by the necessities of war;

25) compelling the nationals of the adverse party to take part in the operations of war directed against their own country, even if they were in the service of the belligerent before the commencement of the war;

26) employing bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets with a hard envelope which does not entirely cover the core or is pierced with incisions;

27) employing weapons, projectiles and material and methods of warfare of a nature to cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering or to strike indiscriminately in violation of the law of armed conflict, provided that such weapons, projectiles, material and methods of warfare are the subject of a comprehensive prohibition;

28) launching deliberate attacks against buildings, equipment, medical units and means of transport, and personnel using, in conformity with international law, the distinctive emblems provided for by the Geneva Conventions;

29) launching deliberate attacks against buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals and places where the sick and wounded are collected, provided that such buildings are not military objectives;

30) launching deliberate attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles involved in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians and civilian objects under international law of armed conflict.

###### Art. 147

Furthermore, serious violations of Article 3 common to the Geneva Conventions and other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to non-international armed conflicts constitute war crimes, namely any of the following acts:

1) violence to life and person, in particular murder of all kinds, mutilation, cruel, inhuman and degrading treatment, as well as torture;

2) outrages upon personal dignity, in particular humiliating and degrading treatment;

3) taking of hostages;

4) the passing of sentences and the carrying out of executions without previous judgment pronounced by a regularly constituted court, affording all the judicial guarantees which are recognized as indispensable;

5) intentionally directing attacks against the civilian population as such or against civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

6) intentionally directing attacks against buildings, material, medical units and transport, and personnel using, in conformity with international law, the distinctive emblems of the Geneva Conventions;

7) intentionally directing attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles involved in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians and civilian objects under the international law of armed conflict;

8) intentionally directing attacks against buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals and places where the sick and wounded are collected, provided they are not military objectives;

9) pillaging a town or place, even when taken by assault;

10) rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence constituting a serious violation of Article 3 common to the four Geneva Conventions;

11) conscripting or enlisting children under the age of 18 years into armed forces or groups or using them to participate actively in hostilities;

12) ordering the displacement of the civilian population for reasons related to the conflict, unless the security of the civilians involved or imperative military reasons so demand;

13) killing, wounding or capturing an adversary combatant by treachery;

14) declaring that no quarter will be given; 15) subjecting persons who are in the power of another party to the conflict to physical mutilations or to medical or scientific experiments of any kind which are neither justified by the medical, dental or hospital treatment of the person concerned nor carried out in his or her interest, and which cause death to or seriously endanger the health of such person or persons;

16) destroying or seizing the property of an adversary unless such destruction or seizure be imperatively demanded by the necessities of the conflict;

17) employing poison or poisoned weapons; 18) employing asphyxiating, poisonous or other gases, and all analogous liquids, materials or devices;

19) employing bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets with a hard envelope which does not entirely cover the core or is pierced with incisions.

###### Art. 148

War crimes are punished, according to their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, by a term of imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 3: Crimes against humanity

###### Art. 149

A crime against humanity means any of the following acts, committed knowingly as part of a widespread or systematic attack directed against any civilian population or a disarmed population in case of internal conflict, in time of peace or in time of war:

1) murder; 2) extermination; 3) enslavement; 4) deportation or forcible transfer of population; 5) imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law; 6) torture;

7) rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization and any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity;

8) persecution against any identifiable group or collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other grounds that are universally recognized as impermissible under international law, in connection with any act referred to in this article;

9) enforced disappearance of persons;

10) the crime of apartheid;

11) other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or to physical or mental health.

###### Art. 150: For the purposes of the preceding article, the following definitions apply

1) "attack launched against any civilian population or a disarmed population in case of conflict" means conduct involving the multiple commission of acts referred to in the preceding article against any civilian population, pursuant to or in furtherance of a State or organizational policy to commit such attack;

2) "extermination" means the intentional infliction of conditions of life, inter alia the deprivation of access to food and medicine, calculated to bring about the destruction of part of a population;

3) "enslavement" means the exercise of any or all of the powers attaching to the right of ownership over a person, including in the course of trafficking in human beings, in particular women and children;

4) "deportation or forcible transfer of population" means forced displacement of persons by expulsion or other coercive means from the area in which they are lawfully present, without grounds permitted under international law;

5) "sexual slavery" means the use of a person as a sexual object;

6) "torture" means the intentional infliction of severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, upon a person in the custody or under the control of the accused. The term does not extend to pain or suffering arising only from, inherent in or incidental to, lawful sanctions;

7) "forced pregnancy" means the unlawful confinement of a woman forcibly made pregnant, with the intent of affecting the ethnic composition of any population or carrying out other grave violations of international law;

8) "persecution" means the intentional and severe deprivation of fundamental rights contrary to international law by reason of the identity of the group or collectivity targeted;

9) "crime of apartheid" means inhumane acts of a character similar to those referred to in the preceding article, committed in the context of an institutionalized regime of systematic oppression by one racial group over any other racial group or groups and with the intention of maintaining that regime;

10) "enforced disappearance of persons" means cases where persons are arrested, detained or abducted by the State or a political organization, or with the authorization, support or acquiescence of the State or such organization, followed by a refusal to acknowledge that these persons are deprived of liberty or to disclose their fate or whereabouts, with the intention of removing them from the protection of the law for a prolonged period of time.

###### Art. 151

Crimes against humanity are punished, according to their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, by a sentence of imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 4: Crime of apartheid

###### Art. 152

Apartheid is any act committed for the purpose of establishing or maintaining domination by one racial group of human beings over another racial group of human beings for the purpose of systematically oppressing them.

###### Art. 153

The following inhumane acts constitute a crime of apartheid, assimilated to a crime against humanity:

1) depriving members of a racial group or groups of their lives;

2) seriously impairing the physical or mental integrity, liberty or dignity of members of a racial group or groups, or subjecting them to torture or to cruel, inhuman or degrading punishment or treatment;

3) arbitrarily arresting and unlawfully imprisoning members of a racial group or groups;

4) deliberately imposing on a racial group or groups living conditions calculated to cause its or their physical destruction in whole or in part;

5) adopting legislative or other measures designed to prevent a racial group or groups from participating in the political, social, economic and cultural life of the country;

6) deliberately creating conditions preventing the development of the group or groups concerned, in particular by depriving members of a racial group or groups of fundamental human freedoms and rights: the right to work, the right to form recognized trade unions, the right to education, the right to a nationality, the right to freedom of movement and residence, the right to freedom of opinion and expression, and the right to freedom of peaceful assembly and association;

7) adopting measures, including legislative measures, designed to divide the population along racial lines by the creation of separate reserves and ghettos for members of a racial group or groups, by prohibiting marriages between persons belonging to different racial groups, and by expropriating property and land belonging to a racial group or groups or to members thereof;

8) exploiting the labour of members of a racial group or groups, in particular by subjecting them to forced labour;

9) persecuting organizations or persons, by depriving them of fundamental freedoms and rights, because they oppose apartheid.

###### Art. 154

Crimes of apartheid are punished, according to their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, by a sentence of imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 5

Common provisions on the crime of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity and the crime of apartheid

###### Art. 155

The provisions of this chapter apply to crimes committed on national territory or outside it regardless of the nationality of the perpetrator or accomplice and the place where the offence was committed.

###### Art. 156

The provisions of this chapter apply to all equally without any distinction.

The official capacity of a person, in particular as a member of a government or parliament, as an

elected representative or as an agent of the State, does not exempt that person from criminal responsibility under this code. It may not in any case constitute grounds for reduction, suppression or suspension of the sentence.

###### Art. 157

Criminally and individually responsible for the crimes referred to in this chapter and liable to the penalties provided for that purpose are representatives of the State, including military commanders and other superiors, members of organizations and institutions, as well as individuals or groups who participate therein as perpetrators or accomplices, or who are guilty of direct incitement to perpetrate any such crime, or who participate in a conspiracy to commit it.

Any military commander and other superior is criminally responsible for crimes committed by forces or subordinates under their effective authority and control if:

1) they knew or, owing to the circumstances, should have known that the crime was about to be or was being committed or deliberately disregarded information which clearly indicated as much;

2) they had not taken all necessary and reasonable measures within their power to prevent or repress the commission thereof or to refer the matter to the competent authorities for investigation and prosecution;

3) the crime was related to activities falling within their responsibility and effective control.

###### Art. 158

Natural persons who are perpetrators or accomplices of the crimes referred to in this chapter incur, in addition, the following penalties:

1) general confiscation according to the procedures set out in articles 69 and following of this code as well as the confiscation of profits, property and assets derived directly or indirectly from the crime, without prejudice to the rights of third parties in good faith;
