2) civic disqualification according to the procedures set out in article 72 of this code;

3) prohibition of residence according to the procedures set out in articles 113 and following of this code;

4) special confiscation according to the procedures set out in articles 117 and following of this code.

The court shall impose, in addition, a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 159

The penalties incurred by legal persons declared criminally liable for the crimes provided for in this chapter are:

1) the penalties provided for in article 54 of this code;
2) disqualification for up to ten (10) years from the right to administer or manage a company for the directors and managers who were the instigators or perpetrators of the offence committed by the company, by way of derogation from article 55 of this code;

3) publication of the conviction in the official gazette and in the press according to the procedures provided for in article 142 of this code.

The court shall impose, in addition, a fine of one hundred million (100,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 160

A person responsible for one of the crimes referred to in this chapter cannot be relieved of their responsibility solely on the ground that they performed an act prescribed or authorized by legislative and regulatory provisions or an act authorized by lawful authority.

An order from lawful authority does not exonerate a person from their responsibility for the crimes provided for in this chapter except if:

1) that person was under a legal obligation to obey orders of the government or the superior in question;

2) that person did not know that the order was unlawful; and

3) the order was not manifestly unlawful.

These three conditions are cumulative.

For the purposes of this article, an order to commit genocide or a crime against humanity is deemed manifestly unlawful.

###### Art. 161

The following also constitute grounds for exoneration from criminal liability:

1) a disease or mental deficiency depriving a person of the capacity to understand the unlawful character or nature of their conduct or to control such conduct to conform to the requirements of the law;

2) a state of intoxication, except voluntary intoxication in circumstances such that the person knew that, as a result of their intoxication, they risked adopting conduct constituting a crime or disregarded that risk;

3) self-defence or defence of another person, or in the case of war crimes, to defend property essential to their survival or that of another person or essential to the accomplishment of a military mission, against imminent and unlawful use of force, subject to compliance with the principle of proportionality;

4) cases of necessity and duress resulting from a threat of imminent death or serious, continuing or imminent harm to their own physical integrity or that of another person, provided that they act reasonably and did not intend to cause greater harm than the harm to be avoided.

###### Art. 162

The fact that a person participated in a defensive operation conducted by armed forces does not in itself constitute a ground for exoneration from criminal liability within the meaning of the preceding article.

###### Art. 163

In the case of concurrence between several categories of crimes, the sanction corresponding to the most serious offence shall apply.

However, the complementary penalties relating thereto may, in certain cases, be cumulated with the principal penalty if the duration of the latter does not exceed thirty (30) years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 164

The crime of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity and crimes of apartheid are not subject to any statute of limitations.

#### Chapitre II : Des atteintes à la vie de la personne

##### Section 1 : Des homicides volontaires

###### Paragraphe 1 : Du meurtre et de l'assassinat

###### Art. 165 : Le meurtre est le fait de donner volontairement la mort à autrui

It is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 166

Murder is punished by twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment:

1) when it precedes, accompanies or follows another crime;

2) when its purpose is to prepare, facilitate or execute an offence;

3) when its purpose is to facilitate the flight or to attempt to ensure the impunity of the perpetrator or accomplice;

4) when it was committed for ritual, mystical or cannibalistic purposes;

5) when it was committed in reaction to conduct perceived as having harmed the honour of the family.

###### Art. 167 : L'assassinat est le meurtre commis avec préméditation ou guet-apens

Premeditation is the deliberate intention, formed before the action, to commit a specific crime or offence.

Lying in wait consists of waiting in a place for an individual either to kill them or to inflict acts of violence upon them.

###### Art. 168

Any person who commits murder is punished by twenty (20) to fifty (50) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 169

Parricide is murder committed by a descendant upon the person of their biological or adoptive father or mother or the ascendants of the latter.

###### Art. 170

Any person who commits parricide is punished by twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 171

Infanticide is the murder of a child under the age of eighteen (18) years.

###### Art. 172

Infanticide is punished by ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Paragraphe 2

De l'empoisonnement et de l'administration de substances nuisibles à la santé

###### Art. 173

Poisoning is the act of intentionally attempting to take the life of a person by the use or administration of substances likely to cause death more or less promptly, in whatever manner those substances were used or administered, and whatever the consequences.

###### Art. 174

Any person who commits poisoning is punished by the maximum term of criminal imprisonment.

Attempted poisoning is punished by the same penalty.

###### Art. 175

Any person who causes another person a disease or infirmity by administering to them against their will or by machination, abuse of power or deception, substances harmful to health is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) CFA francs to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

If the administration of substances harmful to health causes serious disability, its perpetrator is liable to

ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 176

Is guilty of an act of intentional transmission of the human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), the person who:

1) knowing themselves to be infected, intentionally, by any means whatsoever, transmits HIV to another person;

2) knowing that the objects they use are contaminated with HIV, uses said objects on natural persons;

3) intentionally injects substances infected with HIV into another person;

4) knowing that the blood offered for transfusion, the tissue or organ donated to be transplanted is infected with HIV, permits its use or proceeds with a blood transfusion or a tissue or organ transplant on a person.

The person infected with HIV may benefit from mitigating circumstances for an act of intentional transmission under this chapter if:

1) they have taken all known useful measures to substantially reduce the risk of contamination, particularly by wearing a condom; and

2) has informed their sexual partner of their seropositivity and obtained the free and informed consent of the latter prior to any sexual relations.

###### Art. 177

Is punished by criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years, anyone found guilty of an act of HIV transmission as defined in the preceding article.

In case of recidivism, the maximum term of criminal imprisonment is applied.

##### Section 2: Involuntary homicides

###### Art. 178

Involuntary homicide is the act of causing the death of a human being through negligence, lack of skill or precaution, violation of safety regulations.

Any person who commits involuntary homicide is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 179

The term of imprisonment is increased to five (05) years and the fine to double that provided for in the preceding article if the involuntary homicide was caused:

1) during a hunting party without a permit; 2) while driving a vehicle without a license; 3) under the influence of alcohol; 4) under the effect of narcotics or psychotropic substances; 5) by the driver of a vehicle used for public transport of persons or goods.

The court seized shall also order the suspension or withdrawal of the driving license or weapon permit.

###### Art. 180

Any person who, through negligence or imprudence, causes HIV infection of another person, is punished by the penalties for involuntary homicide provided for in this section.

When this offense is committed by a health professional, the penalties provided for in article 178 are increased to five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and the fine from five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) without prejudice to applicable disciplinary sanctions.

#### Chapitre III: Endangering the person

##### Section 1: Exposing others to risks

###### Art. 181

Any person who directly exposes another person to an immediate risk of death or injury likely to result in mutilation or permanent disability through the manifestly deliberate violation of a particular obligation of safety or prudence imposed by law or regulation is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Is assimilated to the exposing of others to risks sanctioned in the preceding paragraph and punished by the same penalties, the marketing, direct or indirect, of products containing elements likely to harm human health.

When a child under eighteen (18) years of age is a victim of the acts referred to in this article, the term of imprisonment is increased to three (03) to five (05) years and the fine from five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 182

Any legal person declared criminally responsible for the offense provided for in the preceding article is punished by the penalties provided for in article 54 of this code.

The court seized may, in addition, if it deems it necessary, prohibit the activity in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offense was committed.

##### Section 2: Offenses committed by health personnel

###### Art. 183

Any health professional who, in the exercise of their functions, commits negligence, clumsiness, imprudence or breach of their oath, is punished according to the case:

1) by the penalties for involuntary homicide if their action or omission resulted in the death of the patient; 2) by the penalties for involuntary injuries in case of disability or serious infirmity of the patient.

###### Art. 184

Any health professional who omits or refuses to honor the obligation of care to which they are bound towards a person infected with HIV, is liable to a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

The same penalty is applicable to any individual who prevents the person infected with HIV from accessing care, refuses or obstructs access to care for the infected person.

###### Art. 185

The provisions of the preceding article apply without prejudice to administrative or disciplinary sanctions incurred by the offender.

##### Section 3: Abandonment of a person unable to protect themselves

###### Art. 186

Any person who abandons, in any place, another person who is not able to protect themselves due to their age or physical or psychological condition is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The maximum penalty is pronounced when the victim is a child.

If the abandonment has resulted in an illness or incapacity for personal work of more than six (06) weeks, it is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the abandonment has resulted in mutilation or permanent disability of the person, the penalty is an imprisonment

of three (03) to five (05) years of imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

If the abandonment has resulted in the death of the person, the penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

##### Section 4: Obstruction of assistance measures

###### Art. 187

Constitutes an obstruction of assistance measures, the act of intentionally preventing the arrival of rescue intended to help a person escape imminent danger or to combat a disaster presenting a danger to the safety of persons.

###### Art. 188

Any person guilty of obstruction of assistance measures is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 5: Failure to render assistance

###### Art. 189

Constitutes a failure to render assistance the act of not providing assistance to a person in danger, when the person who could act by themselves or by summoning help without danger to themselves or to third parties, intentionally refrains from doing so.

###### Art. 190

Any person guilty of failure to render assistance is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Any person who, in the event of a calamity or public danger, has, without valid reason, refused or neglected to respond to a request for assistance or to a requisition for aid made by the qualified public authority, is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph.

##### Section 6 : De l'expérimentation illégale sur la personne humaine

###### Art. 191

Illegal experimentation on the human person consists of practicing or having practiced on a person biomedical research without having obtained the free, informed and express consent of the person concerned, of the holders of parental authority or of the guardian or of other persons, authorities or bodies designated to consent to the research or to authorize it.

###### Art. 192

Any person guilty of illegal experimentation on the human person is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The same penalties are applicable when the biomedical research is practiced after the consent has been withdrawn.

The provisions of this article are not applicable to the examination of the genetic characteristics of a person or to their identification by their genetic fingerprints carried out for scientific research purposes.

###### Art. 193

Any legal person declared criminally responsible for the offense provided for in the preceding article is punished by the penalties provided for by article 54 of this code. The court seized may also, if it deems it necessary, prohibit the activity in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offense was committed.

##### Section 7 : De l'abus frauduleux de l'état d'ignorance ou de faiblesse

###### Art. 194

Fraudulent abuse of a state of ignorance or weakness consists of a person exerting serious or repeated pressure on another or using techniques likely to alter their judgment with a view to leading them to an act or an abstention that is seriously prejudicial to them.

###### Art. 195

Any person guilty of fraudulent abuse of a state of ignorance or weakness is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 196

When the offense provided for in the preceding article is committed by the de facto or de jure leader of a group that pursues activities having the purpose or effect of creating, maintaining or exploiting the psychological or physical subjection of persons who participate in these activities, the guilty parties are punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

The same penalties are applicable when the victim is either a person in a state of psychological or physical subjection, or a person whose particular vulnerability, due in particular to their minority, their advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or psychological deficiency, is apparent or known to its perpetrator.

###### Art. 197

Any natural person guilty of the offense of fraudulent abuse of a state of ignorance or weakness also incurs the complementary penalties provided for in this code.

Any legal person declared criminally responsible for the offense of fraudulent abuse of a state of ignorance or weakness is punished by the penalties provided for by article 54 of this code.

The court seized may also, if it deems it necessary, prohibit the activity in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offense was committed.

#### Chapitre IV : Des atteintes à l'intégrité physique de la personne

##### Section 1 : De la torture et des autres mauvais traitements

###### Art. 198

The term "torture" means any act by which severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, is intentionally inflicted on a person for purposes including obtaining from them or from a third person information or confessions, punishing them for an act that they or a third person has committed or is suspected of having committed, intimidating or coercing them or intimidating or coercing a third person, or for any reason based on discrimination of any kind.

This term does not extend to pain or suffering arising only from, inherent in or incidental to lawful sanctions.

###### Art. 199

Any person guilty of torture is punished by a sentence of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 200

If the facts of torture are established, the statements or confessions obtained by this means and the convictions based on these statements or confessions are null and void.

###### Art. 201

The expression "cruel and inhuman punishment or treatment" means any treatment or suffering intentionally inflicted by any person that causes serious mental or physical suffering, which cannot be justified.

###### Art. 202

Any person who subjects an individual to cruel and inhuman punishment or treatment is punished by a sentence of ten (10) years to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 203

The expression "degrading treatment" means any treatment that causes serious humiliation or debasement to the person subjected to it.

###### Art. 204

Any person who subjects an individual to degrading treatment is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 205 : Les peines prévues aux articles 199, 202 et 204 sont portées

1) au maximum de la réclusion criminelle à temps en cas de meurtre ;

2) jusqu'à trente (30) ans de réclusion criminelle :

a) s'il est fait usage d'arme ou de menace ;

b) si les faits sont commis sur un magistrat, un juré, un avocat, un agent ou officier des forces de sécurité ou de défense, toute personne dépositaire de l'autorité publique ou chargé d'une mission de service public dans l'exercice de ses fonctions ou de sa mission, un chef traditionnel lorsque la qualité apparente de la victime est connue de l'auteur ;

c) si les faits sont commis sur une personne dont la situation d'une particulière vulnérabilité due notamment à sa minorité, son âge avancé, un état de grossesse, une maladie, une infirmité ou à une déficience physique ou psychique, est connue de son auteur ;

d) sur un témoin, une victime ou une partie civile soit pour l'empêcher de dénoncer les faits, de porter plainte, ou de déposer en justice, soit en raison de sa dénonciation, de sa plainte ou de sa déposition ;

e) lorsque les faits sont accompagnés d'agression sexuelle autre que le viol ;

f) lorsque les faits ont entrainé une mutilation ou une infirmité permanente.

###### Art. 206

Ne constituent pas une cause d'exonération le fait d'avoir obéi aux ordres d'un supérieur ou d'une autorité publique en commettant les actes de torture et les autres mauvais traitements, ni le fait que ceux-ci aient été justifiés par des circonstances exceptionnelles, notamment un état de guerre, une menace de guerre, l'instabilité politique intérieure ou toute autre situation exceptionnelle.

###### Art. 207

In addition to articles 6 and following of this code, Togolese courts have full jurisdiction to hear offences referred to in articles 198, 201 and 203:

1) when they have been committed on board vessels or aircraft registered in accordance with Togolese legislation;

2) when the victim is a Togolese national and the offences have been committed abroad regardless of the nationality of the alleged perpetrator;

3) regardless of the nationality of the alleged perpetrator, that of the victim, the place of perpetration of the offences; or when the alleged perpetrator is extradited from another State to be handed over to another State or to an international criminal court.

###### Art. 208

No one shall be expelled, returned or extradited to a State where there are serious grounds for believing that he or she is subject to acts of torture, other inhuman and degrading treatment or the death penalty.

The determination of serious grounds provided for in this article shall take into account all relevant considerations including, where appropriate, the existence in the State concerned of a pattern of systematic, flagrant or mass violations of human rights.

In the event that Togo does not extradite the alleged perpetrator of the offences referred to in articles 198, 201 and 203, the case shall be submitted to the competent national court which shall rule in accordance with the provisions of this code.

##### Section 2 : Des actes de barbarie

###### Art. 209

An act of barbarity is any act of cruelty consisting of intentionally inflicting physical and/or mental suffering on another person that is inhuman and odious.

###### Art. 210

Any person guilty of acts of barbarity shall be punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 3 : Des infractions à caractère sexuel

###### Paragraphe 1 : Du viol

###### Art. 211

Rape consists of imposing sexual relations or penetration on another person by fraud, threat, coercion or violence.

###### Art. 212

Any person who commits rape shall be punished by a sentence of criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

With regard to sexual relations imposed by violence, coercion or threat by one spouse on another, it shall be punished by a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or seven hundred and twenty (720) hours of community service.

In the event of a repeat offence, the sentence shall be ten (10) to twelve (12) months' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 213

The penalties provided for in the preceding article shall be a sentence of criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs if:

1) sexual relations have been imposed by several perpetrators on the same victim;

2) the rape has resulted in a pregnancy;

3) the violence inflicted has caused an illness or incapacity for work exceeding six (06) weeks;

4) the rape is committed by a person acting in a state of manifest intoxication or under the manifest influence of narcotic drugs.

5) by a person who has abused the authority conferred by his or her functions.

###### Art. 214

The penalty provided for in the preceding article shall also apply when the rape has been committed:

1) under threat of a weapon;

2) by a legitimate, natural or adoptive ascendant of the victim or the direct ascendants thereof;

3) by a person having authority over the victim;

4) by a person who has abused the authority conferred by his or her functions.

###### Art. 215

When rape is committed on a particularly vulnerable person, due in particular to his or her minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or psychological deficiency, the perpetrator shall be punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 216

Any person guilty of rape shall be punished by the maximum sentence of fixed-term criminal imprisonment when the rape is preceded, accompanied or followed by acts of torture or barbarity or has resulted in the death of the victim.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Des mutilations génitales féminines

###### Art. 217

All forms of female genital mutilation practised by any person, regardless of his or her capacity, are prohibited.

###### Art. 218

Female genital mutilation means any partial or total removal of the external genital organs of girls, young girls or women, and/or any other operations concerning these organs.

Excluded from this category are surgical operations on the genital organs performed on medical prescription.

###### Art. 219

Whoever by traditional or modern methods practises or promotes female genital mutilation or participates therein is guilty of intentional violence against the person of the excised woman.

coercion or violence, against the person of a child under fifteen (15) years of age, or any exposure or exploitation for commercial or tourist purposes of photographs, images and sounds obtained by any technical process, of films or drawings of a pornographic nature featuring one or more children under fifteen (15) years of age.

###### Art. 220

Any person who is guilty of intentional violence within the meaning of the preceding article shall be punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Any act of paedophilia shall be punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five (25,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 4 : Des violences

The penalty shall be doubled in the event of a repeat offence.

###### Sous-section 1 : Des violences volontaires

###### Art. 221

If the mutilation has resulted in the death of the victim, the perpetrators shall be punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of imprisonment and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000).

###### Art. 222

Any person who, having knowledge of an excision already planned, attempted or practised, does not immediately notify the public authorities shall be punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) or a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Paragraphe 3 : De l'inceste

###### Art. 223

Incest consists of all sexual relations and touching, of whatever nature, committed by:

1) a legitimate, natural or adoptive ascendant of the victim or the direct ascendants thereof, without limitation of degree;

###### Paragraphe 1 : Dispositions générales

###### Art. 225

Intentional violence consists of acts of aggression likely to undermine the physical or psychological integrity of the person against whom they are directed.

###### Art. 226

Minor violence, in particular violence that has not caused an incapacity for personal work exceeding nine (09) days, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If such minor violence has been committed with a weapon, jointly by several persons against a single victim, the perpetrator or perpetrators are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s).

2) a brother, a sister, a half-brother or a half-sister.

Incest is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who, having knowledge of an attempted or completed incest against a minor, has not immediately informed the authorities is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

When the victim is a child under fifteen (15) years of age, the maximum penalty is imposed.

###### Paragraphe 4 : De la pédophilie

###### Art. 224

Pedophilia consists of any sexual intercourse or touching, of any nature whatsoever, committed by an adult person, without fraud, threat,

###### Art. 227

Any person guilty of other intentional violence against another is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if such violence has resulted in an incapacity for personal work for the victim of between ten (10) days and three (03) months.

###### Art. 228

The penalty is increased to five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment if:

1) the violence committed has resulted in mutilation or serious disability or an incapacity for personal work exceeding three (03) months;

2) the violence was committed with weapons or sharp or blunt objects used as weapons;

3) the violence was committed jointly by several persons against a single victim;

4) the violence was committed against a person whose particular vulnerability, due in particular to minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or psychological deficiency, is known to its perpetrator;

5) the violence was committed against a person by reason of their membership or non-membership, real or supposed, in a particular ethnic group, race, or religion;

6) the violence was committed against a person by reason of their opinion, their membership or non-membership, real or supposed, in a political organization.

###### Art. 229

If intentional violence committed without homicidal intent has nevertheless resulted in death, the penalty is increased to five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

The penalty is increased to ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment if the fatal blows were inflicted with weapons or were inflicted jointly by several persons against a single victim.

###### Art. 230

Any person who enters a public or peaceful gathering or demonstration and incites other persons to commit violence is liable to a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 231

Any person who, outside the public security forces on duty, in uniform or not, during a public gathering or demonstration, is found carrying a weapon or an object dangerous to public safety or to the physical integrity of persons and property, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In case of recidivism, the penalty is doubled.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Des violences faites aux femmes

###### Art. 232

Violence against women consists of all acts of violence directed against persons of the female sex that cause or may cause them physical, sexual, psychological or economic harm or suffering.

This includes in particular:

1) violence against women in situations of armed conflict or internal disturbances; 2) violence against a pregnant woman;

3) violence related to all forms of forced marriage; 4) inhuman and degrading widowhood rites; 5) economic violence.

###### Art. 233

Any person guilty of violence against women in situations of armed conflict or internal disturbances is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 234

Any person guilty of physical or psychological violence against a pregnant woman is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties. The penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment if such violence has resulted in the death of the victim.

###### Art. 235

Any person who, through violence against a pregnant woman or against the unborn child, causes, even unintentionally, the permanent incapacity of the child, is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment if such violence has caused the death of the child.

###### Art. 236

Inhuman and degrading rites consist of all ceremonial or funeral acts imposed on a woman that cause or are likely to cause her serious humiliation or degradation.

The perpetrators or accomplices of inhuman and degrading rites are punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 237

Economic violence consists of constraints imposed on a woman to deprive her of her financial independence or to restrict it.

Any person who commits economic violence against a woman is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Des violences faites aux enfants

###### Art. 238

All minor violence committed against the person of a child is punished by one (01) to six (06) months of imprisonment and a fine of fifty thousand (50,000) to two hundred thousand (200,000) or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 239

If the minor violence referred to in the preceding article is of a repeated nature, the penalties incurred are one (01) to three (03) years of imprisonment or a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 240

All other intentional violence such as physical and psychological abuse, intentional and repeated deprivation of care or food committed against the person of a child is punished by two (02) to five (05) years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 241

If intentional violence without homicidal intent has nevertheless resulted in death, the penalty is increased to five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Sous-section 2 : Des blessures involontaires

###### Art. 242

Involuntary injuries consist of those that have been caused to another through negligence, clumsiness, inattention, failure to comply with an obligation of safety or prudence imposed by law or regulations.

###### Art. 243

Any person guilty of involuntary injuries or who is the cause thereof is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 244

The penalties provided for in the preceding article may be doubled if the involuntary injuries were caused:

1) during the practice of hunting without a hunting permit;

2) while driving a vehicle without a driver's license; 3) under the influence of alcohol;

4) under the effect of narcotics or psychotropic substances; 5) by the driver of a vehicle used for public transport of persons or goods.

For the application of points 3, 4 and 5 of this article, the court seized shall also order the temporary suspension of up to one (01) year or the cancellation of the driver's license.

##### Section 5: Threats

###### Art. 245

A threat is understood to be an act of intimidation consisting of a person inspiring, by any means whatsoever, in another person the fear of physical or moral violence against their person, family, property or interests.

###### Art. 246: Any person guilty of threat is punished

1) by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, when it is a death threat;

2) by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if this threat is made with an order or under condition to perform or allow to be performed an unlawful act or an act prejudicial to another.

###### Art. 247

The penalties are six (06) months to two (02) years of imprisonment or a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs if the threat was not accompanied by any order or condition.

#### Chapitre V

Offences relating to the production, trafficking and use of narcotics and psychotropic substances

###### Art. 248

The cultivation, production, manufacture, illicit trafficking of narcotics and psychotropic substances subject to regulation are prohibited on the national territory.

Any substance subject to regulation held or used is subject to seizure. Confiscation thereof is ordered by the competent judicial authority, in accordance with the provisions of the law on drug control relating to the preservation and destruction of seized plants and substances.

The judicial authority may resort to the opinion of an expert.

Without prejudice to prosecution, where applicable, for cultivation, production, manufacture or illicit trafficking, opposition, by any means whatsoever, to the exercise of the functions of pharmacy inspectors and more generally to the exercise of control and investigation functions is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 1

Repression of the production and illicit trafficking of controlled substances

###### Art. 249

The provisions of this chapter apply to all plants and substances listed by order of the minister responsible for public health relating to substances subject to regulation on the national territory and updated as of December 31 of each year.

###### Art. 250

For the application of the provisions of this chapter, a distinction is made between:

1) "high-risk substances"
2) "risk substances"
3) "precursors"

###### Art. 251

The classification of these substances in the categories listed in the preceding article is detailed in the order of the minister responsible for public health referred to in article 249.

###### Art. 252: In this chapter

1) the expressions "illicit use" and "use of substances" respectively designate the use of prohibited substances and the use without medical prescription of other substances subject to regulation on the national territory.

2) the term "drug addiction" designates a person in a state of physical and/or psychological dependence with regard to a substance subject to regulation on the national territory.

###### Paragraphe 1: High-risk substances

###### Art. 253

Those who have contravened the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the cultivation, production, manufacture, extraction, preparation or transformation of high-risk substances are punished by a term of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 254

Those who contravene the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the export, import and international transport of high-risk substances are punished by a term of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Those who contravene the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the offer, putting on sale, distribution, brokerage, sale, delivery on any basis whatsoever, sending, dispatch, transport, purchase, possession or use of high-risk substances are also punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 255

Those are punished by a term of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

1) those who facilitate for another by any means whatsoever, and in any place whatsoever, the illicit use of high-risk substances, for consideration or free of charge.

This is particularly the case for owners, managers, directors, operators, de facto or de jure, of any place open to the public or used by the public, who tolerate the use of high-risk substances in these places. Fraudulent intent is presumed in case of a second positive check by a police service;

2) those who issue pharmaceutical prescriptions not medically justified for high-risk substances; 3) those who, knowingly, upon presentation of a pharmaceutical prescription not medically justified that is made to them, deliver high-risk substances; 4) those who, by means of pharmaceutical prescriptions not medically justified, have delivered to themselves or attempt to have delivered to themselves high-risk substances;

5) those who introduce high-risk substances into food or beverages, without the knowledge of consumers.

###### Art. 256

Those who transfer or offer high-risk substances to a person for their personal consumption are punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraph 2: High-risk substances

###### Art. 257

Those who contravene legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the cultivation, production, manufacture, extraction, preparation, transformation, importation, exportation, offering, offering for sale, distribution, brokerage, sale, delivery under any title whatsoever, sending, dispatch, transport, purchase, possession or use of high-risk substances are punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years' imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

In the case of offering or transfer to a person for their personal consumption, the prison sentence is one (01) to five (05) year(s) and the fine is five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Paragraph 3: Precursors, equipment and materials

###### Art. 258

Those who produce, manufacture, import, export, transport, offer, sell, distribute, deliver under any title whatsoever, send, dispatch, purchase or possess precursors, equipment and materials, either with the aim of using them in or for the illicit cultivation, production or manufacture of high-risk substances or risk substances, or knowing that these precursors, equipment or materials are to be used for such purposes, are punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years' criminal imprisonment and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraph 4: Common provisions

###### Art. 259

Those are punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years' imprisonment and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to two hundred million (200,000,000) CFA francs:

1) those who provide their assistance in the conversion or transfer of resources or property derived from the offences provided for in articles 252 to 258 with the aim of concealing or disguising the illicit origin of said property or resources; 2) those who provide their assistance in the concealment or disguising of the nature, origin, replacement, disposition, movement or actual ownership of resources, property or related rights derived from one of the offences listed in point 1 above;

3) those who acquire, possess or use property and resources knowing that they came from one of the offences listed in point 1 above;

4) those who by any means, allow the false justification of the origin of property and income of the perpetrator of one of the offences provided for in articles 254 to 259 having procured for the latter a direct or indirect profit.

Those who provide their assistance to any person involved in the commission of the offences provided for in articles 253 to 259 with a view to shielding them from judicial prosecution are punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 260

Those who, by any means, incite, directly or indirectly, even if such incitement has not been followed by effect, to the illicit use of high-risk substances or substances presented as having the effects of high-risk substances, are punished by a sentence of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years' imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 261

Those who, by any means, incite directly or indirectly to the illicit use of risk substances or substances presented as having the effects of risk substances are punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years' criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 262

The attempt to commit one of the offences provided for in articles 253 to 260 is punished as the completed offence.

The same applies to association or conspiracy with a view to committing one of these offences.

###### Art. 263

Accomplices by providing, with knowledge of the facts, means, assistance, aid or advice for one of the offences referred to in articles 253 to 260 are punished by the same penalties as the perpetrator of this offence.

###### Art. 264

Financial operations intentionally carried out, relating to one of the offences provided for in articles 253 to 260, are punished as the offence itself.

###### Art. 265

The penalties provided for in articles 253 to 260 may be pronounced even if the various acts that constitute the elements of the offence were carried out in different countries.

##### Section 2: Use of narcotics and psychotropic substances

###### Art. 266

The use without medical prescription of substances subject to regulation is prohibited on the national territory. Any substance subject to regulation found thus possessed or used is seized. Confiscation is ordered by the competent judicial authority, even if the person is not subject to prosecution. The provisions of the law on drug control relating to the preservation and destruction of seized plants and substances are applicable.

The judicial authority may however request the opinion of an expert.

###### Art. 267

Notwithstanding the provisions of articles 254 and 255, those who have unlawfully possessed, purchased or cultivated plants or substances classified as narcotics or psychotropic substances, the small quantity of which allows them to be considered as intended for their personal consumption, are punished by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The person concerned may be exempted from the penalty or from its execution:

1) if they have not reached the age of criminal majority; 2) if they are not in a state of recidivism; and 3) if by solemn declaration made at the hearing, they undertake not to repeat the offence.

The court orders the confiscation of the seized substances or plants, utensils and materials used in the commission of the offence. Their destruction may possibly be ordered.

###### Art. 268

Any person who drives a land, sea or air motor vehicle, while they are, even in the absence of any external sign, under the influence of a high-risk substance which they use unlawfully, is punished by a sentence of one (01) to three (03) year(s) imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who refuses to submit to screening tests and verifications is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph.

When there is occasion to apply the provisions punishing homicide and involuntary injuries, the penalties provided for these offences are doubled.

A joint order of the ministers responsible for health and security determines the screening tests and verifications to which drivers may be subjected, as well as the conditions under which these operations are carried out.

##### Section 3

Supply to minors of plants or substances classified as narcotics or psychotropic substances

###### Art. 269

Those who, knowingly, have supplied to a minor plants or substances classified as narcotics or psychotropic substances appearing on the list established by an order of the minister responsible for health are punished by a sentence of criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 4: Circumstances aggravating penalties

###### Art. 270

The maximum penalties provided for in Articles 253 to 262 are doubled without this maximum exceeding fifty (50) years:

1) when the perpetrator of the offence belongs to an organized gang;

2) when the perpetrator of the offence participates in other illegal activities facilitated by the offence;

3) when the perpetrator of the offence has used violence or weapons;

4) when the perpetrator of the offence held public office or representative functions and the offence is committed in the exercise of his functions;

5) when the offence is committed by a health professional or a person responsible for combating the use or trafficking of regulated substances;

6) when the drug is delivered or offered or its use has been facilitated to a minor, a mentally disabled person or a person undergoing detoxification treatment;

7) when a minor or a mentally disabled person participates in the offence;

8) when the substances delivered cause death or seriously compromise the health of one or more persons;

9) when the offence is committed in a penitentiary establishment, a military establishment, an educational or training establishment, a hospital or care establishment, a social services centre or in other places where schoolchildren and students engage in educational, sporting or social activities or in the immediate vicinity of these establishments and places;

10) when the perpetrator of the offence adds to the regulated substances, substances which have aggravated their dangers;

11) when the perpetrator of the offence is in a state of recidivism, convictions pronounced abroad are taken into account to establish recidivism.

##### Section 5: Exemption and mitigation of penalties in favour of repentant offenders

###### Art. 271

Any person who has been guilty of participation in an association or agreement with a view to committing one of the offences provided for in Articles 253 to 261 is exempted from penalty, if, having reported this association or agreement to the administrative or judicial authority, he has made it possible to prevent the commission of the offence and to identify the other persons involved.

###### Art. 272

Apart from the cases provided for in the preceding article, the maximum penalty incurred by any person who is the perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offences listed in that article, who has, before any prosecution, enabled or facilitated the identification of the other guilty parties or, after the commencement of prosecution, enabled or facilitated their arrest, is reduced by half.

In addition, the said person is exempted from the fine as well as from the optional accessory and complementary penalties provided for in Article 277.

##### Section 6: Accessory or complementary penalties and measures

###### Paragraphe 1: Mandatory confiscations

###### Art. 273

In all cases provided for in Articles 253 to 260, the courts shall order the confiscation of the seized plants and substances. They may also order their destruction or their transfer to an authorized body for their use.

###### Art. 274

In all cases provided for in Articles 253 to 260, the courts shall order the confiscation and shall indicate the destination of the installations, materials, equipment and other movable property used or intended to be used for the commission of the offence to whomever they belong, unless the owners establish that they were unaware of their fraudulent use.

###### Art. 275

In all cases provided for in Articles 253 to 260, the courts shall order the confiscation of the proceeds derived from the offence, movable or immovable property into which these proceeds have been transformed or converted and up to the value of the said proceeds, property acquired legitimately with which the said proceeds have been mixed, as well as income and other advantages derived from these proceeds, property into which they have been transformed or invested or property with which they have been mixed to whomever these proceeds and property belong, unless the owners establish that they were unaware of their fraudulent origin.

###### Paragraphe 2: Optional penalties

###### Art. 276

In the cases provided for in Articles 253 to 262, the courts may pronounce:

1) permanent prohibition from the territory or prohibition of residence on the territory for a period of five (05) to ten (10) years, against any foreigner;

2) prohibition of civic rights for a period of one (01) to ten (10) year(s);

3) prohibition from leaving the national territory and withdrawal of passport for a period of one (01) to ten (10) year(s);

4) prohibition from driving motor vehicles, land, sea and air, and withdrawal of permits or licences for a period of one (01) to ten (10) year(s);

5) permanent prohibition or for a period of one (01) to ten (10) year(s) from exercising the profession in connection with which the offence was committed;

6) confiscation of all or part of the property of lawful origin of the convicted person, whatever its nature, movable or immovable; divided or undivided.

In the cases provided for in Article 255 point 1, the confiscation of utensils, materials and furniture with which the premises were furnished or decorated.

In the cases provided for in Articles 253, 254, 255 point 1; 256, 257, 261 and 262, the closure for a period of six (06) months to three (03) years of all places open to the public or used by the public where these offences were committed by the owner, manager, director, operator, in fact or in law, of these places, or with his complicity, may be pronounced, without prejudice to the penalties provided for by Article 54 of this code in case of conviction of a legal person.

The withdrawal of the licence to sell beverages or operate a restaurant may be pronounced for the same period.

###### Art. 277

Without prejudice, where applicable, to provisions providing for more severe penalties, anyone who contravenes one of the prohibitions listed in the preceding article or the closure of the establishment provided for in paragraph 3 of the same article, is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 3: Treatment measures

###### Art. 278

When the person prosecuted under the offences provided for in Articles 267 to 269 is a drug addict, the court may subject him, in replacement of the penalty, to a treatment measure appropriate to his condition.

When the person prosecuted under the offences provided for in this chapter, is at the same time a drug addict, the court may subject him, in addition to the penalty, to a treatment measure appropriate to his condition.

Anyone who evades these treatment measures is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

#### Chapitre VI: Offences against individual liberty

###### Art. 279

Any person holding public authority by his title or functions, acting in the exercise of his functions or using his title or functions, who, knowingly, orders or commits an action tending to unlawfully deprive another person of his liberty is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

When the unlawful deprivation of liberty consists of detention or retention for a period of more than seven days, the penalty is three (03) to five (05) years of imprisonment and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 280

Any person holding public authority, by virtue of their title or functions, who, having become aware, in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of their functions or mission, of an unlawful deprivation of liberty, voluntarily refrains either from putting an end to it if they have the power to do so, or, failing that, from requesting the intervention of a competent authority, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The same penalty applies when the person referred to in the preceding paragraph voluntarily refrains either from carrying out the necessary verifications if they have the power to do so, or, failing that, from transmitting the complaint to a competent authority when the deprivation of liberty, recognized as unlawful, has continued.

###### Art. 281

Any prison administration officer who receives or detains a person without a warrant, judgment or committal order established in accordance with the law, or who unduly extends the duration of a detention, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 282

The director of a psychiatric treatment center or an approved care center who unlawfully receives or detains a hospitalized person is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 283

Any person who arrests, detains or holds, without a decision of the competent authority and outside the cases provided for by law, or abducts or sequesters a person in any place whatsoever, is punished:

1) by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years if the detention, arrest or sequestration lasted more than one (01) month;

2) by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) if it lasts less than one (01) month.

###### Art. 284

Any person who knowingly lends or provides a place to carry out the detention or sequestration is punished by the same penalties as the perpetrator of that detention or sequestration.

###### Art. 285

The perpetrators of the acts provided for in articles 283 and 284 are punished by a term of five (05) to (10) years of criminal imprisonment if the victim is a child under eighteen (18) years of age.

###### Art. 286

The perpetrators of the acts provided for in articles 283 and 284 are punished by a term of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment:

1) if they engaged in acts of torture, barbarity or violence against the victim;

2) if the sequestration or detention was carried out to facilitate, prepare or commit an offense against property or to exercise blackmail or to obtain a ransom;

3) if the sequestration or detention was carried out for the purposes of early or forced marriage, for the purposes of mystical or ritual practices or for any other unlawful purpose.

###### Art. 287

The perpetrators of sequestration or detention resulting in the death of the victim are punished by the maximum term of criminal imprisonment.

The perpetrators of sequestration or detention carried out for the purpose of facilitating an organ removal are punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 288

The perpetrators or accomplices of sequestration or detention benefit from mitigating circumstances, as provided for in articles 36 and following, when they have, unconditionally, restored liberty to the victim safe and sound before the second day completed since that of the arrest, sequestration or detention.

#### Chapitre VII : Des atteintes à la dignité humaine

##### Section 1 : Des atteintes à l'honneur

###### Art. 289

An attack on honor constitutes any act directed against the mark of consideration, the regard due to rank, the testimony of esteem or the homage paid to the worth of a person.

###### Art. 290

Any person who, publicly, by any means of communication whatsoever, imputes to another a fact likely to harm their honor and reputation, commits an offense of defamation and is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 291

The direct publication, or by way of reproduction, of an allegation or imputation qualified as defamation, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 292

Defamation committed by one of the means set out in article 553, against the courts and tribunals, the armed forces and law enforcement forces, the constituted bodies, the public administrations, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 293

Defamation committed by the means set out in article 553, by reason of their functions or their capacity, against ministers of religion, dignitaries of national orders, civil servants, depositaries or agents of public authority, citizens entrusted with a public service or mandate, temporary or permanent, jurors or witnesses by reason of their testimony, is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

###### Art. 294

Defamation committed against private individuals, by one of the means set out in article 553, is punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 295

Whoever has sent an open correspondence containing defamation, either against private individuals or against the bodies or persons designated in articles 293 and 294, is punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 296

If the imputed fact is punishable by a penalty exceeding two (02) years of imprisonment, its perpetrator is further punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years.

In case of recidivism of one of the offenses provided for in articles 291 to 296, the maximum of the two penalties may be applied cumulatively.

###### Art. 297

An insult constitutes any outrageous expression, any term of contempt or any invective not containing the imputation of any fact.

###### Art. 298

Any person who, publicly or in writing, violently addresses an insult to another, is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

If the insult includes a term of contempt relating to the sex, gender, disability, racial, ethnic, religious or national affiliation, or HIV-positive status of the

victim, the perpetrator is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and the fine may be doubled.

###### Art. 299

Insult committed by the means set out in article 553 against the bodies or persons designated in article 293 is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 300

Insults or defamation directed against the memory of the dead are punished by the penalties provided for in article 294 above.

###### Art. 301

An offense is constituted by lack of respect towards the Head of State, the head of government, the President of the National Assembly, the President of the Senate, members of government, members of parliament and the presidents of the institutions of the Republic provided for by the Constitution.

An offense committed publicly against the Head of State, the head of government, the President of the National Assembly, the President of the Senate, members of government, members of parliament and the presidents of the institutions of the Republic provided for by the Constitution, is punished by a suspended sentence of one (01) to six (06) months' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

An offense committed publicly against foreign Heads of State, heads of government and members of government, is punished by the same penalty as in the preceding paragraph.

###### Art. 302

An insult committed publicly against heads of mission and other diplomatic agents accredited to the government of the Togolese Republic, is punished by a suspended sentence of one (01) to six (06) months' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 2: Discrimination

###### Art. 303

Any direct or indirect discrimination against a person or group of persons is prohibited.

###### Paragraphe 1: Discrimination in general

###### Art. 304

Discrimination constitutes any distinction, exclusion, restriction or preference based on sex, gender, disability, race, colour, ancestry or family, ethnic or regional origin, economic or social situation, political, religious, philosophical or other convictions, HIV seropositivity, which has the purpose or effect of destroying or compromising the recognition, enjoyment or exercise

of human rights and fundamental freedoms in the political, economic, social and cultural fields or in any other field of public life.

###### Art. 305

Any act of discrimination is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 306

Any person who, by any means of communication, incites discrimination, is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) year(s)' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 312

Any act of discrimination against women is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 313

Any person who prevents or prohibits a woman, by reason of her sex, from access to land and to factors of production and development, or hinders her freedom to come and go, to join and participate in associative meetings, is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 307

Discrimination in matters of employment and occupation constitutes any distinction, exclusion or preference based on sex, gender, race, colour, religion, ethnic affiliation, political or philosophical opinion, racial origin, legal status, nationality or national ancestry, health status or disability and which has the purpose or effect of reducing or altering equality of opportunity or treatment in matters of employment or occupation.

###### Art. 308

Any act of discrimination in matters of employment and occupation is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Excluded from the scope of application of the preceding article are all incentive measures in favour of persons living with a disability.

###### Art. 309

Discrimination in the field of education constitutes the act of undermining equal treatment in access to education, its level and quality as well as the conditions under which it is provided, or inciting abandonment of the school curriculum.

###### Art. 310

Any act of discrimination in the field of education is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 2: Discrimination against women

###### Art. 311

Discrimination against women constitutes any act based on the sexual identity of women, which has the purpose or effect of harming the recognition, enjoyment or exercise by women, regardless of their marital status, of human rights and fundamental freedoms in the political, economic, social, cultural and civil fields or in any other field.

###### Paragraphe 3: Discrimination against persons affected by HIV

###### Art. 314

Any act of discrimination or stigmatisation committed against a person infected or affected by HIV is prohibited.

An act of stigmatisation is considered to be the act of castigating, blaming, degrading or punishing a person by reason of their serological status with HIV and AIDS.

An act of discrimination constitutes any distinction, restriction, exclusion based on the serological status of persons infected by HIV and AIDS, without this being done in their legitimate interest.

###### Art. 315

Whoever is guilty of an act of discrimination or stigmatisation against a person infected or affected by HIV is punished by a sentence of six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 316

The offenses provided for by this section and any other legislation relating to acts of discrimination and stigmatisation against persons affected by HIV are punished by a sentence of one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs when they are committed by a civil servant, a public agent or any person acting in the capacity of public or private authority.

##### Section 3

Trafficking in persons and illicit smuggling of migrants by land, air and sea

###### Art. 317

Trafficking in persons is the act of recruiting, transporting, transferring, harbouring or receiving persons, by the threat of use or the use of force or other forms of coercion, by abduction, fraud, deception, abuse of authority or of a position of

vulnerability, or by the offer or acceptance of payments or benefits to obtain the consent of a person having authority over another for the purposes of exploitation.

Exploitation includes in particular:

1) sexual exploitation including the exploitation of the prostitution of others;

2) exploitation through labour including forced labour or services, slavery or servitude;

3) exploitation through forced or servile marriage;

4) exploitation in armed conflicts;

5) exploitation through begging;

6) exploitation of elements of the human body;

7) exploitation through the performance of illicit activities by others including the production and trafficking of drugs.

The consent of a victim of trafficking in persons to the intended exploitation, as defined in paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article, is irrelevant.

###### Art. 318

Any person who facilitates or participates, by any means, in the process of trafficking in persons as defined in the preceding article, without necessarily being a party to each of the acts in this process, is punished by a term of imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

The custodial sentence incurred by the perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offences provided for in this section is reduced by half if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, he or she has made it possible to stop the offence or to prevent the offence from resulting in death or permanent disability and to identify, where applicable, the other perpetrators or accomplices.

###### Art. 319

The penalty is twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs against any person who is the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence of trafficking in persons when it is committed in the following circumstances:

1) the offence is committed against a particularly vulnerable victim, in particular because of his or her minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency; 2) the offence is committed against several victims;

3) the offence has resulted in serious injury to the victim or a third party;

4) the offence is committed within the framework of the activities of a criminal group;

5) the victim of the offence was chosen by the perpetrator because of his or her nationality, ethnic origin, skin colour, gender, religion or beliefs, or political opinions;

6) narcotics or psychotropic substances, medicines or weapons are used for the commission of the offence;

7) the victim was sequestered or exposed in a public or private place;

8) the acts of trafficking caused the child physical, moral or mental incapacity or any other medically established sequelae;

9) the child was subjected to the worst forms of labour;

10) the purpose of the offence was the removal of one or more organs from the child;

11) the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence is in a state of recidivism;

12) the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence is a public official in the exercise of his or her duties;

13) the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence is the spouse or cohabitant of the victim;

14) the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence is in a position of authority, responsibility or trust in relation to the victim;

15) the victim was put in contact with the perpetrator or accomplice of the acts through the use, for the dissemination of messages intended for an undetermined public, of an electronic communication network.

Any person who organises the commission of a trafficking offence or gives instructions for this offence to be committed is punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 320

The recruitment, transportation, transfer, harbouring or receipt of a child for the purpose of exploitation are considered as trafficking in persons, even if they do not involve any of the means set out in paragraph 1 of Article 318 of this section, and are punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

In the case where the victim of the offence is a child, defined as a human being under the age of eighteen (18) years, the offence of trafficking in persons is constituted even in the absence of the means referred to in Article 317 above.

###### Art. 321

In addition to what is provided for in Article 320, the maximum term of imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs are applicable against any perpetrator or accomplice of the offence of trafficking in persons when it is committed in the following circumstances:

1) the victim is under the age of fifteen (15) years at the time of the commission of the acts;

2) the act was committed by violence, acts of torture or barbarity;

3) the trafficking is committed by an organised gang;

4) the trafficking offence resulted in the death of the victim or a third party including death by suicide, or the contraction by the victim of a fatal disease or HIV/AIDS; 5) in case of recidivism of the offence provided for in Article 320.

###### Art. 322

Any person who solicits or receives gifts, promises, advantages of any kind with a view to facilitating trafficking in persons is punished by criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The attempt is punished by the same penalties.

If the perpetrator is an agent of the public administration having acted in the exercise of his or her duties or on the occasion of the exercise thereof, the penalties are increased to twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 323

Any parent or guardian who knowingly facilitates the trafficking of his or her child or a child in his or her care is punished by a penalty of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

The attempt is punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 324

Any person of foreign nationality who is guilty of trafficking in persons, attempted or complicity in trafficking in persons is, in addition, prohibited from staying in the national territory for a period of at least five (05) years after having served his or her sentence.

###### Art. 325

The court orders the confiscation of all objects and materials used in the process of trafficking in persons.

Any person convicted under the provisions of this section relating to trafficking in persons is liable for all expenses incurred by the services for the care of the victim and covered by the State and any approved organisation.

###### Art. 326

A victim of trafficking in persons having acted under duress, as defined by Article 27, is not criminally liable:

1) for having committed offences when he or she was reduced to them by his or her condition as a victim of trafficking;

2) for having violated national legislation or regulations relating to immigration.
