The provisions of this article are without prejudice to the general defences that the victim may invoke under the law.

###### Art. 327

Smuggling of migrants is the act of ensuring, in order to obtain, directly or indirectly, a financial advantage or other material benefit, the illegal entry into Togolese territory or another State of a person who is neither a national nor a permanent resident thereof.

Illegal entry means the crossing of borders when the conditions necessary for legal entry into Togolese territory are not satisfied.

By fraudulent travel or identity document is meant any travel or identity document:

1) that has been counterfeited or substantially altered by anyone other than a person or authority legally authorised to establish or issue the travel or identity document on behalf of a State; or

2) that has been issued or obtained irregularly by means of false declaration, corruption or duress, or in any other illegal manner; or

3) that is used by a person other than the rightful holder.

###### Art. 328

Any person guilty of smuggling of migrants by land, air and sea is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 329

The penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs when:

1) the offence endangers or risks endangering the life or safety of the migrant who is the object of the trafficking;
2) the migrant who is the object of the trafficking is subjected to inhuman or degrading treatment or acts of exploitation of the human body;

3) the perpetrator of the offence abuses or takes advantage of the particular vulnerability or dependence of the migrant who is the object of the trafficking;
4) the perpetrator of the offence is in a state of recidivism;

5) the offence is committed within the framework of the activities of an organized criminal group;

6) the perpetrator of the offence used drugs, psychotropic substances, medicines or weapons to commit the offence;

7) the offence involved numerous migrants who are the object of the trafficking;

8) the perpetrator of the offence used a child as an accomplice or participant in the criminal act;

9) the perpetrator of the offence used or threatened to use any form of violence against the migrant who is the object of the trafficking or his family;

10) the perpetrator of the offence confiscated, destroyed or attempted to destroy the travel or identity documents of the migrant who is the object of the trafficking.

The penalty is twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of imprisonment and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs when the offence of illicit trafficking in migrants is accompanied by one of the following circumstances:

1) the offence resulted in the death of the victim including death by suicide or the contraction by the victim of a fatal disease including HIV AIDS;

2) the offence is committed by resorting to torture or acts of barbarity;

3) the offence is committed by an organized gang;

4) the offence involved victims under eighteen (18) years of age, pregnant women or persons with physical or mental disabilities;

5) one of the perpetrators or accomplices is an agent of the public administration acting in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions.

###### Art. 330

Any person who intentionally manufactures a fraudulent travel or identity document with a view to facilitating the illicit trafficking of migrants by land, air and sea, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 331

Any person who obtains, procures, destroys, conceals, causes to disappear, confiscates, retains, modifies, reproduces or possesses a travel or identity document of another person or facilitates its fraudulent use, with the intention of committing an offence referred to in this section, is liable to a penalty of one (01) to five (05) years of imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

By fraudulent travel or identity document is meant any travel or identity document:

1) that has been counterfeited or substantially altered by anyone other than a person or authority legally authorized to establish or issue the travel or identity document on behalf of a State; or

2) that has been issued or obtained irregularly by means of false declaration, corruption or by coercion, or in any other illegal manner; or

3) that is used by a person other than the legitimate holder.

###### Art. 332

Any transport company, its legal representatives or agents are responsible for any failure to verify that each passenger is in possession of the identity and/or travel documents required for entry into the country of destination and into any country of transit. Any violation of this obligation is punishable by a penalty of one (01) to six (06) months of imprisonment and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Any transport company, its legal representatives or agents who knowingly fail to report to the competent authorities that a person who attempted to travel or travelled using its services was a victim of trafficking, are liable to a penalty of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) or one of these two penalties.

Any culpable negligence by these same persons is punished by a penalty of six (06) months to two (02) years of imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

The carrier, if it is a common carrier, is not criminally liable if:

1) there are reasonable grounds to believe that the documents that the passenger had in his possession were the documents required to enter legally into the territory of the State;

2) the passenger was in possession of regular travel or identity documents when he boarded or the last time he boarded the means of transport bound for the State concerned.

###### Art. 333

Any person who, without being authorized to do so, discloses information that he obtained in the course of his official functions that allows the identification of a victim and/or witness of trafficking in persons or leads to his identification, is liable to a penalty of one (01) to five (05) year(s) of imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 334

Any exchange of information between the competent services regarding the identification of victims, the assistance offered to them and the judicial investigation is carried out in full respect of the privacy and security of victims and witnesses.

All information exchanged between a victim and a professional providing medical, psychological, legal or other assistance is confidential and may not be communicated to third parties without the victim's consent.

The results of any medical examinations undergone by a victim of trafficking in persons are considered confidential and are used only for the purposes of the investigation and criminal prosecution.

The name, address of a victim of trafficking in persons or any other information, including photos, allowing their identification are neither made public nor published in the media.

Any person who violates the principles provided for in the preceding paragraphs is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs. This penalty is increased to imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, if the disclosure of information has directly caused harm to the victim.

The additional penalty of confiscation of property is applied to natural and legal persons who are perpetrators of trafficking in persons and illicit trafficking in migrants.

The confiscation covers:

1) movable or immovable property of any nature whatsoever having been used to commit the offence or which were intended to commit it and of which the convicted person is the owner. Such property includes in particular, premises and equipment having been used in the context of the trafficking including vehicles and assets;

2) property which is the object or the direct or indirect proceeds of the offence with the exception of property capable of restitution to migrants who are the object of the trafficking;

3) movable or immovable property of any nature whatsoever belonging to the convicted person when he or she is unable to explain their origin.

Confiscation is mandatory for objects classified as dangerous or harmful by law or regulations or the possession of which is unlawful, whether or not such property is the property of the convicted person. It applies under the same conditions to all incorporeal rights.

The confiscated thing is, as a priority, allocated to the reparation of the harm suffered by the person who is a victim of trafficking in persons or the object of illicit trafficking in migrants. In the case where the value of the confiscated property is greater than the estimated harm, the remainder is transferred to the State.

The court seized may, in addition to the penalty pronounced, order any security measure provided for by this code.

##### Section 4 : Du trafic d'organes, des tissus, du sang humains et de ses dérivés

###### Art. 335

Trafficking in organs or human tissues constitutes the act of selling or buying, removing or possessing for any purpose not expressly provided for by law, all or part of the human body.

Any person guilty of trafficking in organs or human tissues, whether removed from his or her own body or from that of another, is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 336

Trafficking in human blood or its derivatives constitutes the act of selling or buying, removing or possessing for any purpose not expressly provided for by law, these elements of the human body.

Any person guilty of trafficking in human blood or its derivatives, whether removed from his or her own body or from that of another, is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 337

Also constitutes within the meaning of articles 336 and 337 trafficking in organs, tissues, human blood or its derivatives, the use of all or part of a human body for the purposes of any mystical or ritual practice whatsoever.

Any person guilty of trafficking in organs, tissues, human blood or its derivatives for the purposes of any mystical or ritual practice whatsoever is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 5 : De l'exploitation

###### Paragraphe 1 : De l'exploitation de la personne humaine

###### Art. 338

Any person, whatever the means used, who commits the offence of forced labour or services is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who practices, facilitates or derives a financial or material profit from the slavery of another or an analogous practice, as defined by article 150 of this code, is liable to a penalty of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 339

Any person who exploits another person, in a forced manner, through pornography is liable to a penalty of five (05) years to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who uses or exploits a particularly vulnerable person, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or psychological deficiency, for pornographic purposes, is punished by a term of imprisonment of ten (10) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

The penalty may be increased up to twenty (20) years of imprisonment if violence has been used.

###### Art. 340

Any person who uses or exploits another person, in a forced manner, for the purposes of sex tourism for the benefit of persons of foreign nationality who are temporarily staying in Togo is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who uses or exploits a particularly vulnerable person, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or psychological deficiency, for the purposes referred to in the preceding paragraph, is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

The penalty may be increased up to twenty (20) years of imprisonment if violence has been used.

###### Art. 341

Forced or servile marriage is any institution or practice under which:

1) an adult person, without his or her consent, or a child, is promised or given in marriage in return for financial or material consideration;

2) a person is transferred to a third party, for consideration or not, by his or her spouse or a member of his or her family or clan with a view to marriage;

3) A person is abducted or held against his or her will with a view to marriage.

Any person who subjects another to forced or servile marriage is liable to a penalty of three (03) years to five (05) years of imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 342

Prohibited and constituting the exploitation of a child in armed conflict is the act by any person of enlisting a child in the armed forces, incorporating them into an armed group or causing them to take part in hostilities.

The preceding paragraph applies both to child victims of international armed conflicts and to child victims of internal armed conflicts, internal tensions and civil disturbances. It is also applicable to children who, before the start of hostilities, are considered refugees within the meaning of relevant international law or legislative provisions in force.

Any person who engages in the exploitation of a child in armed conflict is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 343

Any person who derives financial or material profit from the one-time, continuous or repeated commission of offences provided for in this paragraph by a third party is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 344

The offences referred to in articles 339 to 343 are punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, if they are accompanied by any of the following circumstances:

1) the offence is committed against a particularly vulnerable victim, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental disability;

2) the offence is committed against several victims; 3) the offence has resulted in serious injury to the victim or a third party;

4) the offence is committed within the framework of the activities of a criminal group;

5) the victim of the offence was chosen by the perpetrator because of their nationality, ethnic origin, skin colour, gender, religion or beliefs, or political opinions;

6) narcotics or psychotropic substances, medicines or weapons are used for the commission of the offence;

7) the perpetrator of the offence is in a state of recidivism;

8) the perpetrator of the offence is a public official;

9) the perpetrator of the offence is the spouse or cohabitant of the victim;

10) the perpetrator of the offence is in a position of authority, responsibility or trust in relation to the victim;

11) the victim was put in contact with the perpetrator through the use, for the dissemination of messages to a wide audience, of an electronic communication network.

###### Art. 345

The offences referred to in this paragraph are punishable by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs, if they are accompanied by any of the following circumstances:

1) the victim is under fifteen (15) years of age at the time of the commission of the acts;

2) the act was committed with violence, acts of torture or barbarity;

3) the offence is committed by an organized gang;

4) in case of recidivism;

5) the offence has resulted in the death of the victim or a third party, including death by suicide, or the contraction by the victim of a fatal disease or HIV/AIDS.

###### Paragraphe 2 : De l'exploitation de la mendicité

###### Art. 346

The exploitation of begging is the act by any person in any manner whatsoever:

1) to organize the begging of another person with a view to deriving profit therefrom; 2) to derive profit from the begging of another person, to share the benefits thereof or to receive subsidies from a person habitually engaged in begging;

3) to hire, train or divert a person with a view to delivering them to begging, or to exert pressure on them to beg or continue to do so;

4) to hire, train or divert for purposes of personal enrichment a person with a view to delivering them to the performance of a service in exchange for a donation on the public highway.

###### Art. 347

Assimilated to the exploitation of begging is the act of being unable to justify resources corresponding to one's lifestyle while exercising a de facto influence, permanent or not, over one or more persons engaged in begging or being in habitual contact with the latter.

###### Art. 348

Any person guilty of exploiting the begging of another person is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 349

The penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, when:

1) the offence is committed against a particularly vulnerable victim, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental disability;

2) the offence is committed against either a national 3) the offence is committed against either a Togolese national or resident outside Togolese territory, or a Togolese national or resident outside Togolese territory, or a Togolese national or resident outside Togolese territory, or a Togolese national or resident outside Togolese territory, or

4) by a legitimate, natural or adoptive ascendant of the person who begs or by a person who has authority over them or abuses the authority conferred upon them by their functions; 5) with the use of coercion, violence or fraudulent manoeuvres against the person engaged in begging, their family or a person in habitual contact with them;

6) by several persons acting as perpetrators or accomplices, without constituting an organized gang.

###### Art. 350

The exploitation of the begging of another person committed by an organized gang, or in case of recidivism, is punished by a sentence of criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 6

Des conditions de travail et d'hébergement contraires à la dignité de la personne

###### Art. 351

Constitute working conditions contrary to the dignity of the person, the act of obtaining from a person, whose vulnerability or state of dependence is apparent or known to the perpetrator, the provision of services not remunerated or in exchange for remuneration manifestly disproportionate to the importance of the work accomplished.

###### Art. 352

Constitute accommodation conditions contrary to the dignity of the person the act of subjecting a person, whose vulnerability or state of dependence is apparent or known to the perpetrator, to accommodation conditions not meeting the requirements of decent housing.

###### Art. 353

Any person who subjects another person to working conditions or accommodation conditions contrary to the dignity of the person is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 354

Any legal person declared criminally liable for the offences defined in articles 352 and 353 is punished by the penalties provided for in article 54 of this code. The court seized may also, if it deems it necessary, suspend for a period of up to three (03) years the activity in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed.

##### Section 7 : De la profanation et des atteintes au corps des personnes décédées

###### Art. 355

Constitutes a desecration and an attack on the body of deceased persons, the act of:

1) burying a human body, without medical certification of death and without burial authorization issued by the competent authority;

2) by any means whatsoever, attacking the dignity or integrity of the body of a deceased person, and in particular concealing, mutilating, outraging or destroying it; 3) violating or desecrating a tomb, a burial place or a funerary monument;

4) removing, buying, selling, possessing or using, for mystical or ritual purposes, human bones;

5) degrading or desecrating a public monument to the memory of those who died for the homeland.

###### Art. 356

Any person who attacks the respect due to the dead is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) year to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1.000.000) to five million (5.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

#### Chapitre VIII : Des atteintes à la personnalité

##### Section 1 : De la violation du secret professionnel et de correspondance

###### Art. 357

Constitutes a violation of professional secrecy, the act by a person who is the custodian of information of a secret nature, either by status or by profession, or by reason of a temporary mission or function, of disclosing it outside the cases where the law requires or authorizes its disclosure.

###### Art. 358

Any person who violates professional secrecy is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1.000.000) to three million (3.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 359

Anyone who, either by reason of their profession, or by reason of a function or mission, is the custodian of confidential health information concerning a person infected with HIV, knowingly discloses it to a person not qualified to share the secret is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

The maximum fine is increased to twenty-five million (25.000.000) CFA francs when the offence is committed:

1) by the use of any means of mass communication;

2) by any health professional participating in the care of the person infected with HIV.

Prosecution for violation of professional secrecy may only be brought upon complaint by the victim, their legal representative or their successors in title without prejudice to the provisions provided for by the code of ethics of the profession concerned.

###### Art. 360

Constitutes a violation of the secrecy of correspondence the act by a person of ordering, committing or facilitating, outside the cases provided for by law, the destruction, opening, suppression, diversion, even momentarily, of things entrusted to the postal service and, by extension, subjecting them to other treatments such as fraudulent delays, abusive returns, intentionally erroneous deliveries.

Also constitutes a violation of the secrecy of correspondence the act by any person of revealing the content of such correspondence.

###### Art. 361

Any person who violates the secrecy of correspondence is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100.000) to five hundred thousand (500.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph do not apply to persons:

1) who have obtained express or tacit authorization, either from the author of the correspondence, or from the person to whom its author intends it, to open the correspondence and reveal its content;

2) who open correspondence and reveal its content following an authorization issued in the context of a judicial investigation by the public prosecutor or by an investigating judge.

###### Art. 362

Any violation of correspondence entrusted to the postal service, committed or facilitated by an agent or employee of the administration, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500.000) to two million (2.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, if it is established that the perpetrator acted by misappropriation or abuse of the powers attached to their functions.

##### Section 2 : De la dénonciation calomnieuse

###### Art. 363

Constitutes a false accusation the act of making false accusations against a specific person to an administrative or judicial police officer, a judicial official or a court, the hierarchical superiors or the employer of the person denounced or any other authority having the power to act upon it.

###### Art. 364

Any person who is the author of a false accusation is punished by the penalty applicable to the offence imputed to the defendant or the accused whom the false accusation aims to have wrongly convicted and a fine of five million (5.000.000) to twenty million (20.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The prohibition of rights provided for in articles 80 and following, in particular the prohibition from the profession in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed, may be pronounced as an additional penalty.

If the author, instigator, sponsor or accomplice of the false accusation acted by misappropriation or abuse of the powers attached to their functions, they are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of twenty million (20.000.000) to fifty million (50,000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties. If the fact denounced is liable to criminal or disciplinary sanction, prosecution may be initiated under this article either after judgment or decision of discharge or acquittal, or after order or decision of no case to answer, or after dismissal of the denunciation by the magistrate, official, superior authority or employer competent to give it the follow-up it is likely to have.

The court seized under this article must stay proceedings if prosecution concerning the fact denounced is pending.

##### Section 3 : Des atteintes à la vie privée

###### Paragraphe 1 : De la violation de domicile

###### Art. 365

Constitutes a violation of domicile the act, by a person, of entering a private place inhabited or used for habitation against the will or without the knowledge of the master of the premises, or, having entered, of remaining there.

Also constitutes a violation of domicile, the abuse of authority consisting, for a custodian of public authority, in the exercise of their functions, of entering the domicile of another against their will, outside the cases provided for by law.

###### Art. 366

Any person who is the author of violation of domicile is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500.000) to two million (2.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 367

The penalty is one (01) to three (03) year(s) of imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if the violation of domicile was committed:

1) in a group;

2) during the night;

3) with the aid of violence, threats, assault or coercion;

4) with the carrying of a visible or concealed weapon;

5) with the use of a false name, a false title or a false order of legitimate authority.

Prosecution may only be exercised upon complaint by the victim.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Violation of personal privacy

###### Art. 368 : Constitutes a violation of a person's privacy, the act

1) of publishing or disseminating private papers or recordings, a drawing, a photograph, a film or any other medium reproducing the image of that person without their consent or that of their successors in title;

2) of organizing, by any means whatsoever, the interception, listening to or recording of private communications, oral, optical, magnetic or other exchanges received in a private place, without the knowledge or consent of the persons in communication or of the owner of the premises.

###### Art. 369

Any natural or legal person who violates the privacy of another person is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 370

If the perpetrator of the violation acted by misappropriation or abuse of the powers attached to their functions, they are punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 371

Any natural or legal person admitted to participate in the execution of a telecommunications or electronic communications service who intercepts a private communication, or who, except in cases provided for by law, discloses, publishes or uses the content of said correspondence is punished in accordance with the provisions of the preceding article.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph do not apply to persons:

1) who have obtained the express or tacit consent, either of the author of the private communication or of the person to whom its author intends it, to the interception of the private communication and to the disclosure of its content; 2) who intercept a private communication in accordance with an authorization issued in the context of a judicial investigation by the public prosecutor or by an investigating judge;

3) who provide the public with a telecommunications or electronic communications service and who intercept a private communication when such interception is necessary to protect their rights or property directly related to the provision of the telecommunications or electronic communications service;

4) members of the staff of the regulatory authority responsible for the management and control of the radio frequency spectrum for a private communication that they have intercepted in order to identify, isolate or prevent the unauthorized use of a frequency or transmission.

###### Art. 372

Any natural or legal person who, participating in the cases provided for by law in the execution of an interception decision, reveals the existence or content of the judicial or security interception is punished by the penalties provided for in article 371.

###### Art. 373

Any legal person operating a telecommunications or electronic communications network, or providing a telecommunications or electronic communications service, cryptology and hosting services and failing to comply with requisitions emanating from the authorized authorities provided for by law is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six (06) months to (02) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to two hundred million (200,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 374

In case of recidivism, the penalties provided for in the preceding articles are doubled.

#### Chapitre IX : Offences against the family

###### Art. 375

Constitutes an offence against the family any act directed against the restricted group of father and mother and their children.

##### Section 1 : Family abandonment

###### Art. 376 : Constitutes family abandonment the act for

1) the father or mother of a family to abandon, without serious reason, for more than six (06) months, the family home or to evade all or part of their moral or material obligations, despite a formal notice notified, either by bailiff, or by letter with acknowledgment of receipt to their last known residence;

2) the spouse, without serious reason, to abandon for more than three (03) months their spouse, knowing them to be emeritus; 3) the father or mother to refuse, without serious reason, to pay the amount of expenses necessitated by the maintenance and education of a child entrusted to a third party.

###### Art. 377

Any person guilty of the offence of family abandonment is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 378

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years or a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, any parent who, through their negligence, seriously compromises the health of their children or of those living in their home.

The judge may, in addition, order a treatment or detoxification measure for the alcoholic parent guilty of the offence provided for by this article.

###### Art. 379

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, any parent who, in a spirit of profit, undertakes verbally or in writing the commitment to entrust to another their child born or to be born.

##### Section 2 : Refusal to pay maintenance

###### Art. 380

Constitutes a refusal to pay maintenance the act for any person, without serious reason, to remain more than one (01) month without paying the sum of money to be paid as maintenance to a spouse or to a spouse, to an ascendant or to a descendant in need, in execution of a maintenance obligation or an obligation of support to which they are bound in execution of a court decision or an authentic act.

###### Art. 381

Any person guilty of refusal to pay maintenance is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 3

Protection against non-presentation and offences against the civil status of the child

###### Art. 382

When a decision is made on the custody of a child by enforceable court decision, the father, mother or any other person having authority over them or having their custody who, in defiance of this decision, refuses to present the child, abducts or diverts them, has them abducted or diverted from the hands of those who have received custody, is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The persons referred to in the above paragraph may benefit from a mitigating excuse, under the conditions provided for in article 34 of this code.

If the child is presented before the judgment is rendered, the judge pronounces only a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 383

When the acts referred to in the preceding article are committed by any other person, the penalty of imprisonment is increased from three (03) to five (05) years.

###### Art. 384

The constituent elements of the child's identity must be protected.

The father and mother or any other person who, being legally required to do so, neglects to register a birth or the death of a child with the civil registry, are punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

Whoever knowingly makes to the civil registrar inaccurate declarations likely to alter the personal and family status of a child, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, the civil registrar or civil registry officer who, knowingly, registers inaccurate declarations or deliberately alters, falsifies or destroys a register, an act or a civil registry document.

###### Art. 385

Is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs, the attending physician or midwife who, being legally required to do so, neglects to record the birth of the child in the birth declaration register.

Is punished by the same penalties any relative or any person authorized by one of the parents having attended the delivery who neglects to declare the birth of the child to the civil registry within forty-five (45) days.

When the persons referred to in paragraph 2 of this article knowingly make to the civil registrar inaccurate declarations likely to alter the personal and family status of the child, the penalty is one (01) to three (03) year(s) of imprisonment and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 386

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two (02) to five (05) years and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties whoever abducts, conceals, substitutes a child with the aim of depriving him of his personal and family status.

#### Chapitre X : Des attentats contre les mœurs

###### Art. 387

Constitutes an offense against public morals any act of immorality aimed at either the personal satisfaction of the person acting, or the stimulation of the passions of others.

##### Section 1 : De l'attentat à la pudeur

###### Art. 388

Constitutes an indecent assault any voluntary touching performed against the will on the body of a specific person of either sex, with or without violence, constraint, threat or surprise, with the aim of arousing the senses.

###### Art. 389

Any person guilty of indecent assault is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Indecent assault committed on a child is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

If the indecent assault was committed with violence or threat on the person of a child, the penalties are five (05) to ten (10) years of imprisonment and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 2 : De l'outrage public à la pudeur

###### Art. 390

Constitutes a public indecency offense the sexual exhibition consisting of publicly causing scandal through obscene scenes or gestures.

###### Art. 391

Any person guilty of public indecency offense is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 3 : Des outrages aux bonnes mœurs

###### Art. 392

Constitutes an offense against public morals any indecent or unnatural act committed with an individual of one's own sex.

Also constitutes an offense against public morals any attack on public morality by words, writings, images or by any other means.

###### Art. 393

Any person who commits an offense against public morals is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 394

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties any person who:

1) publicly displays, manufactures or sells with a view to public display objects, images, films, sound or audiovisual recordings, contrary to decency;

2) distributes or causes to be distributed on the public highway or by post, or from door to door or by electronic means any books, brochures, catalogues, leaflets, images, films, sound or audiovisual recordings contrary to decency, without the prior consent of the recipients;

3) broadcasts or causes to be publicly broadcast incitements to practices contrary to public morals by words, writings or any other means of communication.

###### Art. 395

The objects, images, films, books, brochures, catalogues, leaflets, sound or audiovisual recordings referred to in the preceding article are in all cases seized and confiscated with a view to their destruction ordered by a court decision.

In addition, the guilty parties may be deprived for a period of up to five (05) years of the right to publish, sell or reproduce printed matter, recordings, films or images.

###### Art. 396

Whoever broadcasts for children, in a projection venue open to the public, audiovisual recordings of a pornographic nature using any medium whatsoever, is punished by a term of imprisonment of two (02) to five (05) years.

##### Section 4 : Du racolage

###### Art. 397

Constitutes soliciting the act for any person, by any means including by a passive attitude, of publicly soliciting another person with a view to inciting them to sexual relations in exchange for remuneration or a promise of remuneration.

###### Art. 398

Any person who publicly engages in soliciting with a view to prostitution, is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to three hundred thousand (300,000) CFA francs. In case of recidivism within a period of one (01) year, the guilty party is punished by a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs and ten (10) to thirty (30) days of community service.

##### Section 5 : Du harcèlement sexuel

###### Article 399

Constitutes sexual harassment, the act for a person of using orders, threats, constraints, words, gestures, writings or any other means with the aim of obtaining from another person, against their will, favors of a sexual nature.

###### Art. 400

Any person guilty of sexual harassment is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The penalties are increased to three (03) to five (05) years of imprisonment and three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs fine when the sexual harassment is committed:

1) on a particularly vulnerable person, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or mental deficiency;

2) by a person who has abused the authority conferred upon them by their functions, social or professional position in relation to the victim.

Double the penalty provided for in the preceding paragraph is imposed if the harassment was committed on a child under fifteen (15) years of age.

##### Section 6: Procuring and Related Offences

###### Art. 401

Procuring is the act, by any person in any manner whatsoever:

1) of aiding, assisting or protecting the prostitution of another; 2) of profiting from the prostitution of another, sharing the proceeds thereof or receiving subsidies from a person habitually engaged in prostitution;

3) of recruiting, enticing or diverting a person for the purpose of prostitution or exerting pressure on that person to engage in prostitution or continue to do so.

###### Art. 402

Any person guilty of procuring is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

That person is, in addition, deprived of all or part of their civil, civic or professional rights.

The court may order the closure of the establishment where the offence was committed, in application of articles 120 and following of this code relating to the closure of establishments, without prejudice to the penalties provided for by article 54 of this code in the event of conviction of a legal person.

All movable property having been used in the commission of the offence must be seized and confiscated.

The client of the person engaged in prostitution is punished under the same conditions as the procurer.

###### Art. 403

The following act by any person acting directly or indirectly is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs:

1) owning, managing, operating, directing, running, financing or contributing to the financing of an establishment of prostitution; 2) owning, managing, operating, directing, running, financing or contributing to the financing of any establishment open to the public or used by the public, accepting or habitually tolerating that one or more persons engage in prostitution inside the establishment or its annexes or seek clients there for the purpose of prostitution;

3) selling or making available to one or more persons premises or locations not used by the public, knowing that they engage in prostitution there;

4) selling, renting or making available, in any manner whatsoever, to one or more persons, vehicles of any nature knowing that they engage in prostitution therein.

###### Art. 404

The following act by any person, in any manner whatsoever, is assimilated to procuring and punished by the penalties provided for by the preceding article:

1) acting as an intermediary between two persons, one of whom engages in prostitution and the other exploits or remunerates the prostitution of another;

2) facilitating for a procurer the justification of fictitious resources;

3) being unable to justify resources corresponding to one's lifestyle while living with a person who habitually engages in prostitution or while being in habitual relations with one or more persons engaging in prostitution;

4) obstructing the action of prevention, control, assistance or re-education undertaken by qualified organisations with regard to persons in danger of prostitution or engaging in prostitution.

###### Art. 405

Procuring is punished by a sentence of five (05) years to ten (10) years of imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs when it is committed:

1) against a person whose particular vulnerability, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency, is apparent or known to its perpetrator;

2) against several persons;

3) against a person who has been incited to engage in prostitution either outside the territory of the Republic or upon arrival on the territory of the Republic;

4) by a legitimate, natural or adoptive ascendant of the person who engages in prostitution or by a person who has authority over that person or abuses the authority conferred by their functions;

5) by a person called upon to participate, by virtue of their functions, in the fight against prostitution, the protection of health or the maintenance of public order;

6) by a person carrying a weapon;

7) with the use of constraint, violence or fraudulent manoeuvres;

8) by several persons acting as perpetrators or accomplices, without constituting an organised gang; 9) through the use, for the dissemination of messages intended for an undetermined public, of a telecommunications network.

###### Art. 406

The procuring provided for in the preceding article is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs when it is committed:

1) by an organised gang; 2) with violence.

###### Art. 407

Procuring committed by resorting to torture or acts of barbarity is punished by the maximum sentence of criminal imprisonment for a fixed term and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 408

Any legal person declared criminally responsible for the offences defined in this section is punished by the penalties provided for by article 54 of this code. The court seized may in addition, if it deems it necessary, prohibit the activity in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed.

###### Art. 409

For the establishment of recidivism, in accordance with the rules set out in article 45 of this code, convictions pronounced abroad for procuring may be taken into account.

Any person convicted of procuring, by a national court or a foreign court, is deprived of all or part of the rights listed in article 80.

###### Art. 410

Persons having engaged in prostitution may be subjected to health examinations and treatment measures if they are affected by sexually transmitted infections and/or HIV and AIDS.

The costs of examination and treatment are borne by the procurers having exploited the activity of these persons.

### TITLE III: OFFENCES AGAINST PROPERTY

#### Chapter I: Theft

###### Art. 411: Theft is the fraudulent misappropriation of the property of another

###### Art. 412: The following are notably considered as theft within the meaning of the preceding article

1) the fraudulent misappropriation of a vehicle or a boat even for temporary use; 2) fraudulent connections, modifications or alterations of water, gas, electricity or telephone distribution installations intended to exempt the user in whole or in part from payment of charges; 3) the misappropriation or interception of information; 4) the fraudulent misappropriation of standing crops; 5) the refusal to return to the rightful owner or to declare to the competent authority, within a short period, a thing which one has improperly come into possession of by mistake or following a fortuitous event.

###### Art. 413

Any person who fraudulently:

1) uses telecommunications or electronic communications facilities or obtains a telecommunications or electronic communications service; 2) uses for personal or other purposes a public telecommunications or electronic communications network or connects by any means to a private line; 3) uses services obtained by means of the offences referred to in points 1 and 2 above.

is punished by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties only, without prejudice to damages.

Any person who fraudulently misappropriates telecommunications or electronic communications infrastructure is punished by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties, without prejudice to damages.

##### Section 1: Simple theft

###### Art. 414

Theft is simple when it has been committed without any aggravating circumstance.

###### Art. 415

Any person guilty of simple theft is punished by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 2: Aggravated theft

###### Art. 416: Theft is aggravated

1) when it has been committed at night;

2) when it has been committed jointly by several perpetrators or accomplices;

3) when it has been committed by an organized gang;

4) when it has been committed with the use of false keys;

5) when it has been committed by breaking in, climbing, external or internal breaking and entering;

6) when it has been committed in an inhabited place or a place used for habitation, for the storage of funds, valuables, goods or materials;

7) when it has been committed with the carrying of a weapon;

8) when it has been committed with hostage-taking;

9) when it is preceded, accompanied or followed by an act of destruction, degradation or deterioration;

10) when it has been committed with violence or threats against persons;

11) when it is facilitated by the condition of a person whose particular vulnerability, due in particular to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency, is apparent or known to its perpetrator;

12) when it has been committed with the use of a motor vehicle; 13) when it has been committed by a person who improperly assumes the capacity of a person holding public authority or entrusted with a public service mission;

14) when it has been committed with the use of an insignia or uniform that may be confused with those of public authority agents;

15) when it has been committed with the use of a false order from public authority;

16) when it has been committed by a carrier, hotelier or depositary or their agents with regard to things deposited under their responsibility;

17) when it is committed by a person holding public authority or entrusted with a public service mission, in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of their functions or mission;

18) when it is committed by reason of the membership or non-membership, real or supposed, of the victim in a particular ethnic group, nation, race, or religion.

###### Art. 417

Any person guilty of theft committed with a single one of the aggravating circumstances referred to in the preceding article is punished by imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years.

###### Art. 418

Any person guilty of theft committed with at least two of the aggravating circumstances referred to in article 417 is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 419

If the theft was committed with the use of weapons or with violence having caused injuries or incapacity for work exceeding two (02) weeks, or if it was committed with at least three of the aggravating circumstances referred to in article 417, the offender is punished by a penalty of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 420

Any person guilty of theft committed with violence having caused mutilation or serious disability of the victim is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years.

If the violence caused death, even without intent to cause it, or if the theft was committed with hostage-taking, the maximum term of imprisonment is applied.

###### Art. 421

"Breaking and entering" means any forcing, breaking, degradation, demolition, removal of walls, fences, roofs, floors, doors, windows, locks, padlocks or other utensils or instruments used to close or prevent passage.

External breaking and entering is that by means of which one can enter houses, courtyards, outbuildings, apartments or private dwellings. Entry through an underground opening other than that which has been established to serve as an entrance is also external breaking and entering.

Internal breaking and entering is that which, after entry into the places mentioned in the preceding paragraph, is done to interior doors or enclosures, as well as to cupboards or other closed furniture, including crates, boxes, bales under canvas and rope that contain any effects.

###### Art. 422

"Climbing" means any entry into houses, buildings, courtyards, edifices of any kind, gardens, parks and enclosures, executed over walls, doors, roofs or other enclosures.

###### Art. 423

"Inhabited house" means any building, dwelling, lodge, cabin, even mobile, which, without being currently inhabited, is intended for habitation and everything that depends on it, such as courtyards, barns, stables, buildings enclosed therein, whatever their use, and even if they have a particular enclosure within the general enclosure or perimeter.

###### Art. 424

"False keys" means all picks, all imitated, counterfeited or altered keys, all instruments whatsoever that have not been intended for the opening of locks, padlocks or other closures for which they have been used.

###### Art. 425

The manufacture of false keys is punished by imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s).

###### Art. 426

The court seized may possibly deprive those guilty of the different cases of theft provided for in this chapter of the rights listed in article 79 of this code. It may also prohibit them from appearing in certain places in accordance with the provisions of article 129 of this code if it deems it necessary for the protection of public order.

The court seized may, with regard to foreigners, decide to substitute the prohibition from appearing in certain places with the prohibition from national territory provided for in articles 131 and 132 of this code.

##### Section 3: Family immunities

###### Art. 427

Thefts committed by the husband to the prejudice of his wife, by the wife to the prejudice of her husband, by a widower or widow with regard to things that had belonged to the deceased spouse may only give rise to civil reparations.

###### Art. 428

Thefts committed may only give rise to civil reparations and only at the request of the victim:

1) by children or other descendants to the prejudice of their father or mother or other ascendants, by father or mother or other ascendants to the prejudice of their children or other descendants;

2) by relatives by marriage of the same degree, provided that the misappropriations are committed during the duration of the marriage and outside a period during which the spouses are authorized to live separately.

#### Chapter II: Misappropriations

##### Section 1: Breach of trust

###### Art. 429

Breach of trust is the act by a person of misappropriating to the detriment of another funds, securities or any property which have been entrusted to them and which they have accepted on the condition of returning them, producing them or making a specified use of them.

###### Art. 430: Also commits breach of trust, any person who

1) having obtained credit from a public or private credit institution, shall have made use of it other than that declared or shall not have been able to justify the conformity of the use with the use initially agreed upon;

2) having offered movable or immovable property as security; a) shall have assigned it as security for another property;

b) shall have misappropriated it by sale, donation, destruction or by any other means;

c) shall have concealed the proceedings and seizures initiated by another creditor on that property.

Also commits breach of trust any person who is the author of the acts enumerated in the preceding paragraph, in the name of, on behalf of, or under the cover of a legal person.

###### Art. 431

The burden of proof of the entrustment lies with the person who claims to have been the victim of the misappropriation.

Once proof of the entrustment of the object or security has been provided, the person who received it is presumed to have misappropriated, dissipated or destroyed it if they cannot return it, produce it or justify that they made the intended use or employment of it.

To rebut the presumption, it is incumbent upon them to establish that the impossibility in which they find themselves to return or produce the object or security received or to justify that they made the intended use or employment of it, does not have a fraudulent origin or, if that origin is fraudulent, that it is not attributable to them.

###### Art. 432

Any person guilty of breach of trust is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 433

If the breach of trust is committed by a professional in the exercise of their profession, they are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

The guilty party may, in addition, be deprived of the right to exercise their professional activity for a period of up to five (05) years.

###### Art. 434

If the breach of trust is committed by a public or ministerial officer, a trustee in collective judicial reorganization or liquidation proceedings, a company liquidator, a sequestrator, a commercial agent or by any person who manages the affairs of another, in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of their functions or profession, the person concerned is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

###### Art. 435

If the breach of trust is committed by a person making an appeal to the public in order to obtain either on their own account, or as director, administrator or manager of a company or a commercial or industrial enterprise, the entrustment of funds or securities by way of deposit, mandate or pledge, the person concerned is also punished by the penalties provided for in article 434 above.

###### Art. 436

The provisions of articles 428 and 429 of this code relating to family immunities are applicable to breach of trust.

##### Section 2: Abuse of blank signature

###### Art. 437: Constitutes a blank signature

1) the title signed in blank, a document to be completed that the signatory entrusts to a person so that the latter fills in the blanks themselves by determining the elements that are missing;

2) the signature affixed in advance at the bottom of a document on which the signatory has intentionally left a blank intended to be filled in subsequently.

###### Art. 438

Abuse of blank signature consists on the part of a person to whom a paper bearing a blank signature has been entrusted, in fraudulently inscribing, above that signature, an obligation or a receipt.

###### Art. 439

Any person guilty of abuse of blank signature is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 440

The provisions of articles 428 and 429 of this code relating to family immunities are applicable to abuse of blank signature.

##### Section 3: Misappropriation of pledge and seized object

###### Paragraph 1: Misappropriation of pledge

###### Art. 441: Misappropriation of pledge is the act

1) by a debtor, a borrower or a third party pledgor or holder of a pledge, of destroying, altering, failing to return, appropriating or obstructing the rights of the creditor over the object constituted as a pledge;

2) by a debtor, a holder of tools or equipment that is the object of a pledge, of destroying, altering, failing to return, appropriating or obstructing the rights of the creditor over the object constituted as a pledge;

3) by a creditor beneficiary of the pledge, of destroying, altering, failing to return, appropriating or obstructing the rights of the debtor over the pledged object.

###### Art. 442

Any person guilty of misappropriation of pledge is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraph 2: Misappropriation of seized object

###### Art. 443

Misappropriation of seized object is the act, by the person whose property is seized, of destroying, appropriating, failing to return or obstructing the rights of another over an object seized in their hands as security for the rights of a creditor and entrusted to their custody or to that of a third party.

###### Art. 444

Any person guilty of misappropriation of seized object is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 4: Fraudulent organization of insolvency

###### Art. 445

Constitutes fraudulent organization of insolvency the act by a debtor, of seeking, even before their debt is judicially recognized, to evade the execution of a criminal, tortious or maintenance conviction, either by increasing the liabilities or by decreasing the assets of their patrimony, or by concealing all or part of their property or income.

###### Art. 446

Any person who fraudulently organizes their insolvency is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The court seized may decide that the person convicted as an accomplice to the fraudulent organization of insolvency is held jointly and severally liable within the limit of the funds or the market value of the property received gratuitously or for consideration, for the pecuniary obligations resulting from the conviction the execution of which the perpetrator of the offense sought to evade.

The limitation period for the public prosecution runs only from the conviction the execution of which the debtor sought to evade. However, it runs only from the last act intended to organize the debtor's insolvency when the last act is subsequent to that conviction.

###### Art. 447

In the different cases provided for in this chapter, the court seized may possibly deprive the guilty parties of the rights enumerated in article 79 of this code.

If the guilty parties are foreigners, the court seized may pronounce against them the prohibition from the national territory provided for in articles 131 and 132 of this code.

#### Chapter III: Fraud and Related Offences

##### Section 1: Fraud

###### Art. 448

Fraud is the act, either by the use of a false name or a false capacity, or by the abuse of a true capacity, or by the use of fraudulent schemes, of deceiving a natural or legal person and thereby inducing them to their prejudice or to the prejudice of a third party, to hand over funds, securities or any property, to provide a service or to consent to an act creating an obligation or discharge.

###### Art. 449

A false name or false capacity means those which a person has no right to claim. A false name means a false surname, a false first name if it creates a risk of confusion, a false pseudonym under the same conditions.

A false capacity is the act by a person of:

1) falsely assuming a title issued or controlled by the authority holding an academic degree, member of a regulated profession, holder of a decoration, a title of nobility, a public or elected office;
2) attributing to oneself a false profession or activity or a false civil status.

###### Art. 450

The abuse of a true capacity is the act by a person of using a capacity which they actually possess to give force and credence to their allegations through the trust they inspire, the use of the capacity being such as to impart the appearance of sincerity.

###### Art. 451

Fraudulent schemes constitute external material acts performed with a view to giving force and credence to a false assertion, in particular the production of writings, the intervention of third parties or the insertion into a staged scenario.

###### Art. 452

Any person guilty of fraud is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 453

Assimilated to fraud and punished by the penalties provided for in this section is the fraudulent exploitation of the state of ignorance or the situation of weakness of a person infected or affected by HIV and other diseases, either to propose a fallacious treatment with extortion of funds, or to make that person consent to an act that is manifestly prejudicial to them.

If the administration of substances harmful to health causes serious disability, its perpetrator is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 454

In the different cases provided for in this chapter, the court seized may possibly deprive the guilty parties of the rights listed in Article 80 of this code.

##### Section 2: Offences Assimilated to Fraud

###### Paragraph 1: Deception

###### Art. 455

Deception is the act by a merchant or manufacturer of putting on sale, delivering or offering in bad faith goods, objects, instruments or substances not having loyal and merchantable quality, or not having the weight or measure indicated.

###### Art. 456

Any person who commits deception is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraph 2: False Advertising

###### Art. 457

False advertising means the dissemination, by any means whatsoever, of an announcement made in the context of a commercial, industrial, artisanal, liberal activity with the aim of promoting, selling, inciting any person to purchase goods and services, by extolling their imaginary qualities or by giving inaccurate information about them likely to mislead the person who sees it.

###### Art. 458

Any person who commits false advertising is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 459

Any perpetrator or accomplice of any false advertising relating to medicines and other care products, the treatment of AIDS or the prevention of HIV is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 460

Consumer associations are authorized to prosecute any perpetrator of false advertising and to constitute themselves as civil parties, in particular to obtain its rectification by the same media.

###### Paragraph 3: Petty Fraud

###### Art. 461

Petty fraud is in particular the act by a person who knows they are in the absolute impossibility of paying or who is determined not to pay:

1) to have served and to consume beverages or food in an establishment serving beverages or food for a fee;

2) to have allocated and to effectively occupy one or more rooms in an establishment renting rooms;

3) to have served fuels or lubricants with which they have all or part of the tanks of a vehicle filled by distribution professionals;

4) to have themselves transported by taxi or to rent a car; 5) to have served a telephone communication or an internet connection.

###### Art. 462

Any person guilty of petty fraud is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraph 4: Extortion

###### Art. 463

Extortion is the act of obtaining by violence, threat of violence, physical or moral, or coercion:

1) a signature or the handing over of a writing, an act or any document containing an obligation, disposition or discharge; 2) a commitment or a renunciation; 3) the revelation or non-revelation of a secret;

4) the handing over of funds, securities or any property; or 5) any undue advantage.

###### Art. 464

Any person guilty of extortion is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 465: The penalty is increased to five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment

1) if the threat has received a commencement of execution; 2) if the guilty party habitually engages in such activity; 3) if they abuse, in order to engage in it, the information or the position that their profession or function provides them;

4) if the guilty party engages in their criminal activity to the detriment of a particularly vulnerable person, in particular due to their minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or mental deficiency;

5) if the guilty party leads their victim, by these methods or their repetition, to ruin and/or suicide.

###### Paragraph 5: Blackmail

###### Art. 466

Blackmail is the act of obtaining by threatening to reveal or impute facts likely to harm honour or reputation:

1) a signature or the handing over of a writing, an act or any document containing an obligation, disposition or discharge;

2) a commitment or a renunciation;

3) the revelation or non-revelation of a secret; 4) the handing over of funds, securities or any property; or

5) any undue advantage.

###### Art. 467

Any person guilty of blackmail is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 468 : The penalty is one (01) to five (05) year(s) of imprisonment

1) if the threat has begun to be carried out; 2) if the offender habitually engages in such activity;

3) if he abuses, in order to engage in it, information or the position provided to him by his profession or function;

4) if the offender engages in his unlawful activity against a particularly vulnerable person, due in particular to his minority, advanced age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or mental deficiency;

5) if the offender leads his victim, by these means or their repetition, to ruin and/or suicide.

The penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment in case of blackmail accompanied by physical or moral violence.

#### Chapter IV: Receiving stolen goods and related offences

##### Section 1: Receiving stolen goods

###### Article 469

Receiving stolen goods is the act of concealing, holding or transmitting a thing, by any means whatsoever, or acting as an intermediary in order to transmit it, knowing that this thing comes from a crime or an offence.

Also constitutes receiving stolen goods the act, knowingly, of benefiting, by any means whatsoever, from the proceeds of a crime or an offence.

###### Art. 470

Any person found guilty of receiving stolen goods is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties. Receiving stolen goods is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs:

1) when it is committed habitually or by using the facilities provided by the exercise of a professional activity;

2) when it is committed by an organized gang.

If the receiver had knowledge at the time of receiving of aggravating circumstances giving the principal offence a criminal classification, he is punished by the penalty applicable to that crime, unless he has reported the crime and returned the things received.

With regard to recidivism, receiving stolen goods is assimilated to the offence from which the received property comes.

##### Section 2: Offences assimilated to receiving stolen goods

###### Art.471

Is assimilated to receiving stolen goods, the act, by a person, of being unable to justify resources corresponding to his lifestyle or being unable to justify the origin of a property held, while being in habitual contact with one or more persons who engage in the commission of crimes and offences.

###### Art. 472

Any person who is the perpetrator of the offence referred to in the preceding article is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In case of recidivism, the fine may be increased beyond three million (3,000,000) CFA francs up to half the value of the received property.

#### Chapter V: Offences against information systems

###### Art. 473 : For the application of this chapter

1) the term "information system" means an isolated device or a set of interconnected or related devices, which ensures or of which one or more elements ensure, in execution of a program, an automated processing of computer data, as well as the computer data processed, stored, retrieved or transmitted by this device or this set of devices for the operation, use, protection or maintenance thereof;

2) the term "computer data" means a representation of facts, information or concepts in a form suitable for computer processing, including a program capable of causing an information system to perform this function;

The term "with or without right" means the existence or absence of a right of access, total or partial, to the information system on the part of its owner, or the authorization which may or may not be given by Togolese law.

###### Art. 474

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, any person who, with or without right:

1) introduces, deletes or modifies the computer data of an information system;

2) destroys, damages, alters, renders inaccessible or damages this data;

3) misappropriates this data for his personal use or to transfer it to a third party, for consideration or free of charge;

4) destroys, obstructs, falsifies, disrupts, interrupts the operation of an information system.

When the offence defined in this article is committed to the detriment of the Togolese State, its perpetrator is punished by a penalty of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to one hundred and fifty million (150,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 475

The production, marketing, supply or maintenance of tools designed or intended to commit the offences and crimes referred to in this chapter, while being unable to be unaware of the unlawful or criminal use that may be made of them by nature or by purpose, is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) years) and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

When the offence defined in this article has been committed to the detriment of the Togolese State, the penalties incurred are increased to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of two hundred million (200,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 476
