The commission by an organized gang of the offences referred to in this chapter is punished by doubling the maximum penalties incurred for these offences.

When the Togolese State has been a victim of these offences committed by an organized gang, the penalty of criminal imprisonment is increased to thirty (30) years and the fines to five times the maximum incurred.

###### Art. 477

The attempt of the offences and crimes referred to in this chapter is punished by the same penalties as these offences.

###### Art. 478

Incitement, in whatever form, to commit the offences or crimes referred to in this chapter is punished by the same penalties as these offences.

###### Art. 479

In case of recidivism relating to an offence or a crime referred to in this chapter, the maximum fines and custodial penalties are increased to triple without the maximum custodial penalty exceeding fifty (50) years.

Any prior conviction pronounced in a foreign State relating to an offence or a crime referred to in this chapter must be taken into consideration to establish recidivism.

###### Art. 480

The provisions of this chapter are applicable when the information system is located in Togo or when the perpetrator of the offences and crimes referred to in this chapter was physically present in Togo at the time of the commission of the offence, or when the computer data that are the subject of these offences and crimes belong to Togolese natural or legal persons, whether or not these offences and crimes are punished by foreign law.

###### Art. 481

By way of derogation from articles 39 and 40 of this code, when the commission of the offences referred to in this chapter is followed by the commission of an offence provided for by this code, the penalties incurred for each of these offences are cumulated, whether or not they are the subject of several prosecutions.

###### Art. 482

Any person who, with authorization, has accessed or remained in an information system in violation of a security or authorization measure is punished by imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph are doubled when serious disruption or interruption of the information system has resulted.

When the offense under this article is committed to the detriment of the Togolese State, the penalties incurred are tripled from the maximum incurred.

#### Chapitre VI

Violations of literary, artistic and industrial property rights and related rights

###### Art. 483

Any infringement by publication, alteration, reproduction, representation or dissemination by any means whatsoever, or the importation and dissemination free of charge or for consideration, on Togolese territory, of a protected work in violation of legislative provisions in force is prohibited and constitutes the offense of counterfeiting, punishable by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs. When the offense has been committed by an organized gang, the penalty is two (02) to five (05) years' imprisonment and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 484

The penalty is three (03) years to five (05) years' imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, if it is established that the offender has habitually engaged in the acts referred to in the preceding article.

In case of recidivism, after conviction pronounced pursuant to the preceding paragraph, the temporary or permanent closure of establishments operated by the habitual counterfeiter as a natural person or his accomplices may be ordered.

When the offense has been committed by an organized gang, the penalty is five (05) to ten (10) years' imprisonment and a fine of fifteen million (15,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 485

The offenders are, in addition, sentenced to the confiscation of sums equal to the amount of shares of receipts produced by the unlawful reproduction, representation or dissemination, as well as to the confiscation of all equipment specially installed for the purpose of unlawful reproduction and all counterfeit copies or objects.

###### Art. 486

The equipment and counterfeit copies as well as the receipts or share of receipts having given rise to confiscation are handed over to the author or his successors in title to compensate them accordingly for the damage suffered.

The remainder of the compensation due is settled by ordinary means before the competent courts.

###### Art. 487

Any person who issues authorizations in the name of performing artists or performers without being duly authorized to do so, or any person who knowingly acts under cover of such unlawful authorization, is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 488

Independently of the means of recourse for compensation for damages suffered provided for by legislative provisions in force, anyone who voluntarily provokes, in particular through the design, manufacture and marketing of objects enabling the commission of the offenses referred to in the preceding articles, the violation of literary, artistic and industrial property rights and related rights as protected by law, is liable to a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, to a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs and imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s).

###### Art. 489

Any person holding access to communication and marketing services to the public online must ensure that the marketing of counterfeit objects or the dissemination to the public of works or objects protected by copyright without the authorization of the holders of that right when authorization is required is prevented.

Failure to comply with this obligation is punished by a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and double the fine.

### TITRE IV : DES INFRACTIONS CONTRE L'ETAT

#### Chapitre I : Des infractions contre l'autorité de l'Etat

##### Section 1 : Des troubles à l'ordre public

###### Paragraphe 1

Insults to representatives of public authority, symbols and emblems of the State

###### Art. 490

Insults to representatives of public authority consist of the act by words, writing, gesture, images, objects or recorded message not made public of insulting or outraging in the exercise of his functions or on the occasion of that exercise a magistrate, a public official or any other citizen charged with a ministry of public service.

Insults to the flag or the national anthem consist of all acts, words or gestures of a nature to undermine the respect and honor due to them.

###### Art. 491

Any person found guilty of insults to representatives of public authority is punished by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Any person found guilty of insults to the flag and the national anthem is punished by a fine of fifty thousand (50,000) to two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 492

The fine penalty provided for in the preceding article may be doubled if the insult or outrage was uttered publicly or was the subject of public dissemination by its author.

When the insult to the flag or the national anthem is committed in a gathering, the penalty incurred is one (01) to six (06) months' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Des menaces et violences envers des représentants de l'autorité publique

###### Art. 493

Any person who utters threats, as defined in this code, against a magistrate, a public official or a citizen charged with a ministry of public service, a traditional chief in the exercise of his functions or on the occasion of that exercise, to harm his person, his reputation or those of his relatives is punished by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the threat is made with an order or condition to do or refrain from doing an act of his function, these penalties are doubled.

###### Art. 494

Any person who voluntarily commits violence or assault, as defined in this code, against a magistrate, a public official or a citizen charged with a public service ministry, a traditional chief or any person holding public authority in the exercise of their functions or on the occasion of such exercise is punished:

1) by six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs if the violence did not result in the victim being incapacitated from performing their service;

2) by one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment if the violence committed resulted in the victim being incapacitated from performing their service for a period of less than two (02) weeks;

3) by three (03) to five (05) years' imprisonment if the violence committed resulted in the victim being incapacitated from performing their service for a period of two (02) weeks to three (03) months;

4) by five (05) to ten (10) years' criminal imprisonment if the violence committed resulted in mutilation or serious disability or an interruption of service exceeding three (03) months; 5) by ten (10) to twenty (20) years' criminal imprisonment if the violence committed without intent to cause death nevertheless resulted in death;

6) by the maximum term of imprisonment if the violence was committed with homicidal intent.

When the violence and assault were committed in a group, the correctional penalties provided above are respectively:

a) one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment in the case provided for in point 1;

b) five (05) to ten (10) years' criminal imprisonment in the case provided for in point 3.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Des troubles aggravés à l'ordre public

###### Art. 495

Those who, in a movement of revolt, are punished by a penalty of one (01) to five (05) year(s)' imprisonment:

1) participate in the erection of barricades or other works to hinder the action of the public force;

2) invade public buildings and structures, sowing discord or pillage therein;

3) incite other persons to revolt through promises, threats, orders or rallying signs;

4) engage in pillage to the detriment of others.

###### Art. 496 : The organizers of the movement of revolt are punished

1) by ten (10) to twenty (20) years' criminal imprisonment if the movement resulted in the death of an agent of the public force or a person unconnected with the movement;

2) by five (05) to ten (10) years' criminal imprisonment if the movement resulted in injuries to an agent of the public force or to a person unconnected with the movement causing incapacity for work exceeding three (03) months.

3) by six (06) months to three (03) years' imprisonment if the movement resulted in injuries to an agent of the public force or to a person unconnected with the movement causing incapacity for work of between ten (10) days and three (03) months.

###### Art. 497

The publication, dissemination or reproduction, by any means whatsoever, of false news, fabricated, falsified or falsely attributed documents to third parties when, done in bad faith, it disturbs the public peace, or is likely to disturb it, is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The author of the false news referred to in the preceding paragraph is punished by a penalty of one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

When the publication, dissemination or reproduction done in bad faith is of a nature to undermine the discipline or morale of the armed forces or to hinder the Nation's war effort, the penalty is one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 2 : De la rébellion

###### Art. 498

Any violent resistance to the lawful action of agents of the public force constitutes rebellion.

Any person guilty of rebellion is punished by two (02) to three (03) months' imprisonment or ten (10) to one hundred (100) days of community service work or a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs when it was committed individually and did not cause the victim agent of the public force to be incapacitated from performing their service.

###### Art. 499

The penalty is six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment if the rebellion was committed jointly by several individuals without resulting in the victim agent of the public force being incapacitated from performing their service, or if it was committed with the carrying of a visible or concealed weapon.

###### Art. 500

The penalties of article 494 are applicable to perpetrators of rebellion according to whether they caused the agent of the public force to be incapacitated from performing their service, mutilation, disability or death.

##### Section 3 : Du refus d'obtempérer

###### Art. 501

Failure to comply constitutes the deliberate failure to observe a lawful order given on the public highway or in a public place by a representative of the public authority, acting in the exercise of their functions.

###### Art. 502

Any person guilty of failure to comply is punished by a fine of ten thousand (10,000) to fifty thousand (50,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 503

When failure to comply is accompanied by violence, of any nature whatsoever, against the person of the representative of the public authority, the penalty is:

1) six (06) months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of fifty thousand (50,000) to one hundred thousand (100,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties when such violence was committed jointly with several individuals or with the carrying of a visible or concealed weapon, without resulting in the victim representative of the public authority being incapacitated from performing their service;

2) one (01) to three (03) year(s)' imprisonment and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if the violence resulted in the victim representative of the public authority suffering temporary incapacity for work of less than two (02) weeks;

3) three (03) to five (05) years' imprisonment and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if the violence resulted in the victim representative of the public authority suffering temporary incapacity for work of between two (02) weeks and three (03) months;

4) five (05) to ten (10) years' criminal imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs if the violence resulted in the victim representative of the public authority suffering mutilation, serious disability or temporary incapacity for work of more than three (03) months;

5) ten (10) to twenty (20) years' criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs if the violence committed without intent to cause death nevertheless resulted in death; 6) the maximum term of imprisonment if the violence was committed with homicidal intent.

##### Section 4 : Des forfaitures

###### Art. 504

Any crime or any offence committed in the exercise of their functions or on the occasion of such exercise by a magistrate or an official holding public authority constitutes malfeasance.

In addition to the penalties incurred for the crime or offence committed, the guilty party is removed from the magistracy or the public service.

###### Art. 505

Any infringement of liberty, any violence against persons, any offence against morals, any offence against the property of private individuals committed in the exercise of their functions or by abuse of power by a magistrate or official holding a portion of public authority is punishable by double the penalties provided against ordinary private individuals.

When the crime committed constitutes a malfeasance, the maximum term of criminal imprisonment is applied.

##### Section 5 : Des abus de pouvoir

###### Art. 506

Political, administrative, parliamentary, judicial, military authorities and magistrates or any officials holding a portion of public authority who have conspired to influence, divert or oppose the execution of laws are by that fact alone punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100 000) to two hundred thousand (200 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the conspiracy took place between civil authorities and military authorities, the perpetrators or instigators shall be punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of two hundred thousand (200 000) to five hundred thousand (500 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties without prejudice to the penalties applicable in case of complicity against the security of the State.

###### Art. 507 : Sont punis de la déchéance de leurs fonctions

1) magistrates of the judicial order as well as administrative judges who have interfered in the exercise of legislative or regulatory power by unlawfully stopping or suspending the execution of laws and regulations or by enacting regulations;

2) ministers, parliamentarians, officials and agents holding a portion of administrative authority who have interfered in judicial functions by pronouncing criminal or civil convictions or by giving injunctions or prohibitions to courts and tribunals.

###### Art. 508

Any agent holding public authority who orders or requires the use of public force outside the cases provided for by law or to thwart the execution of the law, a decision or a warrant of justice, is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (1 00 000) to two hundred thousand (200 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 509

Any commander of a public force unit who refuses or neglects to deploy his unit after having been duly requested by the judicial or administrative authority, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (1 00 000) to two hundred thousand (200 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties and removed from his functions.

###### Art. 510

Any person who unduly claims to exercise public authority by making use either of false orders of that authority, or of false insignia or usurped uniforms is by that fact alone punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100 000) to two hundred thousand (200 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 511

The penalties provided for in this section are not applicable to officials or public agents who have acted only on orders from their hierarchical superiors given within the limits of their competence.

##### Section 6 : Des entraves à la justice

###### Sous-section 1 : Des entraves à la saisine de la justice

###### Art. 512

The act, for anyone having knowledge of a crime of which it is still possible to prevent or limit the effects, or of which the perpetrators are likely to commit new crimes which could be prevented, of not informing the judicial or administrative authorities is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1.000.000) to three million (3.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Excepted from the preceding provisions, except with regard to crimes committed against persons under eighteen (18) years of age:

1) relatives in direct line and their spouses, as well as brothers and sisters and their spouses, of the perpetrator or accomplice of the crime;

2) the spouse of the perpetrator or accomplice of the crime, or the person who notoriously lives in a marital situation with him.

Also excepted from the provisions of the first paragraph, persons holding information of a confidential nature, either by status or by profession, or by reason of a temporary mission or function.

###### Paragraphe 1 : Du bris de scellés

###### Art. 516

Any custodian of seals affixed by order of justice or administration who breaks or allows said seals to be broken is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1.000.000) to three million (3.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 513

When the crime referred to in the first paragraph of the preceding article constitutes an attack on national defense and State security provided for by Chapter VIII of this title or an act of terrorism provided for by Title V, the penalty is increased to five (05) years of imprisonment and up to twenty million (20.000.000) CFA francs fine.

If the seal has been deliberately broken by a person other than the custodian; the offender is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of five hundred thousand. (500 000) to one million (1 000 000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 514

The act, for anyone having had knowledge of deprivations, ill-treatment or sexual assaults inflicted on a person under eighteen (18) years of age or on a person who is not able to protect himself by reason of his age, an illness, an infirmity, a physical or mental deficiency or a state of pregnancy, of not informing the judicial or administrative authorities is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1 000 000) to three million (3.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 2

De la destruction, de la dégradation, de la soustraction de registres, d'éléments de preuve, d'actes ou autres documents publics.

###### Art. 517

Any person who destroys, degrades or removes registers, acts or other public documents contained and preserved in services or by ministerial officers as well as any element of evidence in a judicial proceeding is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1.000.000) to five million (5.000.000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Du refus de témoigner

Except when the law provides otherwise, excepted from the preceding provisions are persons holding information of a confidential nature, either by status or by profession, or by reason of a temporary mission or function.

###### Art. 518

Any person who, except in the case where he is bound by professional secrecy, refuses to provide his testimony in court, is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100.000) to one million (1.000.000) CFA francs.

###### Sous-section 2 : Des entraves au bon fonctionnement de la justice

###### Art. 515 : Constituent une entrave au bon fonctionnement de la justice

Si le refus de témoigner fait suite à une corruption du témoin ou s'il a pour effet de dissimuler un fait justificatif ou une excuse dans un procès pénal, le coupable est puni d'une peine d'emprisonnement d'un (01) à trois (03) ans et d'une amende d'un million (1.000.000) à trois millions (3.000.000) de francs CFA ou de l'une de ces deux peines.

1) breaking of seals;

2) destruction, degradation, removal of registers, evidence, acts or other public documents;

3) refusal to testify;

4) perjury;

5) false oath;

6) subornation of a witness;

7) wilful alteration by an interpreter of statements made in court;

8) wilful alteration by an expert of results or observations submitted in court.

###### Paragraphe 4 : Du faux témoignage

###### Art. 519

Perjury consists of the wilful alteration of a fact reported by a witness before any court or before a judicial police officer acting in execution of a letters rogatory.

###### Art. 520

Any perjurer who has acted through corruption or with the aim of causing a party to the proceedings to be unduly convicted is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the perjury is aimed at causing a defendant or an accused to be falsely convicted, the perjurer is punished by the penalty applicable to the offence imputed to that defendant or that accused.

The perjurer is exempt from penalty if he or she has spontaneously retracted his or her testimony before the decision of the trial or investigating court ending the proceedings.

###### Paragraphe 5 : Du faux serment

###### Art. 521

Any party to proceedings to whom an oath has been deferred or referred in civil matters and who takes a false oath is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The court seized may prohibit him or her from exercising all or part of the rights referred to in article 79 of this code for up to five (05) years.

###### Paragraphe 6 : De la subornation de témoin

###### Art. 522

Subornation of a witness consists of exercising various actions on a person, during proceedings or with a view to a claim or defence in court, with a view to inducing him or her either to make or deliver a false deposition, statement or attestation, or to refrain from such activity.

"Various actions" means in particular threats, abuse of authority, machination, promises, gifts, assault or seduction.

###### Art. 523

Any person who suborns or attempts to suborn a witness in judicial or disciplinary proceedings is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s), and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, even if the subornation has failed to achieve its effect.

###### Paragraphe 7

De l'altération volontaire par un interprète de déclarations faites en justice

###### Art. 524

Any interpreter who, in the execution of his or her mission, wilfully alters the statement he or she translates is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Paragraphe 8

De l'altération volontaire par un expert de résultats d'observations apportés en justice

###### Art. 525

Any expert who wilfully alters the results of his or her observations with the aim of distorting the course of justice is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Sous-section 3 : Dès autres entraves au bon fonctionnement de la justice

###### Art. 526 : Constitue aussi une entrave au bon fonctionnement de la justice

1) the use of physical force, threats or intimidation, or the promise, offer or granting of an undue advantage to obtain false testimony or prevent testimony or the presentation of evidence in proceedings in connection with the offence committed;

2) the use of physical force, threats or intimidation to prevent an officer of justice or an officer of the detection and law enforcement services from exercising the duties of their office in connection with the offence committed;

3) the act of slowing down or causing to be slowed down judicial proceedings in order to delay judicial proceedings brought against oneself or another person in exchange for any advantage promised or offered by that person or another person;

4) the failure to comply with a judicial or administrative injunction not to leave Togolese territory, whether such departure from the territory is legal or illegal;

5) the act of leaving, legally or not, Togolese territory in order to evade criminal or administrative proceedings.

###### Art. 527

Any person found guilty of obstruction of justice is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 528

Also constitutes an obstruction to the proper functioning of justice the act of continuing or causing to be continued, in defiance of a court decision ordering the suspension of works on an immovable property, the execution of said works, the transfer or exploitation of the immovable property.

###### Art. 529

Any person who, in defiance of a court decision ordering the suspension of works on an immovable property, continues or causes to be continued the execution of said works is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The court seized may order, if it deems it necessary, the demolition of works illegally undertaken.

##### Section 7 : Des entraves à la liberté des enchères ou soumissions

###### Art. 530

An obstruction to the freedom of auctions or tenders consists of any disturbance caused by various means, in particular by threats, violence, assault or fraudulent means, to auction or tender operations.

###### Art. 531

Any person who obstructs the freedom of auctions or tenders is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph are pronounced against those who, by gifts, promises or fraudulent agreements, have excluded bidders, limited bids or tenders, as well as against those who have received these gifts or accepted these promises.

##### Section 8 : De la fraude aux examens et concours publics

###### Art. 532 : Constitue une fraude à un examen ou à un concours le fait

- de livrer à un tiers ou de communiquer sciemment à une quelconque des parties intéressées le test, le sujet, l'épreuve ;

- d'accepter d'un tiers le test, le sujet, l'épreuve ;

- de faire usage de fausses pièces telles que diplômes, certificats, extraits de naissance ou autres ;

- de se faire substituer ou de substituer une tierce personne ; - de déclarer faussement admis un candidat ou un non candidat.

###### Art. 533

Any person guilty of fraud in an examination or competition is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) month to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The penalty is doubled if the offender acted with the aim of enrichment.

##### Section 9 : Offences against public tranquility

###### Paragraphe 1 : Abusive or nocturnal disturbance and public drunkenness

###### Art. 534

Abusive disturbance constitutes any personal and voluntary act producing offensive noises.

###### Art. 535

Nocturnal disturbance constitutes any personal and voluntary act producing or causing noises that disturb the tranquility of inhabitants during the night.

###### Art. 536

Any person who, outside of public celebrations and customs, causes an abusive or nocturnal disturbance, is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 537

In case of recidivism within a period of six (06) months, the offender is punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs without prejudice to the closure of the establishment and seizure of equipment contributing to the disturbance for a period not exceeding three (03) months and the penalties provided for by article 54 in case of conviction of a legal person.

###### Art. 538

Any person who is in a state of public and manifest drunkenness is punished by a fine of ten thousand (10,000) to fifty thousand (50,000) CFA francs.

In case of recidivism within twelve (12) months, the offender is punished by two (02) to three (03) months of imprisonment or ten (10) to thirty (30) days of community service.

The court seized may, in addition, order that the person be subjected at their own expense to detoxification treatment.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Demonstrations and gatherings

###### Art. 539

Any person who participates on the public highway in a procession, gathering or other demonstration obstructing public traffic, without having observed the prior administrative formalities, is punished by a fine of fifty thousand (50,000) to one hundred and fifty thousand (150,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 540

Any organizer of a demonstration not in conformity with legal requirements on the public highway, even if they did not personally participate, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 541

When, as a result of a concerted action carried out by force by a group, violence or assault has been committed against persons, or destruction or damage has been caused to the property of residents or to vehicles parked or in circulation, the instigators and organizers of this action as well as those who voluntarily participated in it are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) without prejudice to the more severe penalties provided for by law.

###### Art. 542

When, as a result of an unlawful gathering or one legally prohibited by the administrative authority, violence, assault, destruction or damage qualified as crimes or offences has been committed, the following are punished by a term of imprisonment of:

1) six (06) months to two (02) years, the instigators and organizers of this gathering who did not give the order to disperse as soon as they became aware of such violence, assault, destruction and damage; 2) one (01) to six (06) months, those who continued to actively participate in this gathering after the commencement and with knowledge of the violence, assault, destruction or damage already committed.

Those guilty of the offences defined in this article are liable for bodily or material damage caused to persons and property.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Begging and vagrancy

###### Article 543

Begging is the act of asking for alms in one's personal interest on the public highway.

###### Article 544

Any person guilty of begging is punished by a term of imprisonment of fifteen (15) to thirty (30) days or eight (08) to fifteen (15) days of community service.

###### Art. 545

Vagrancy is the situation of any individual who has neither a fixed domicile nor means of subsistence and who does not habitually exercise either a trade or profession.

Any person who engages in vagrancy is punished by a term of imprisonment of fifteen (15) to thirty (30) days or eight (08) to fifteen (15) days of community service.

The court may, in addition, order their placement in a reception, guidance or care facility for a period of three (03) months, which may be extended with the aim of facilitating their socio-professional reintegration.

###### Article 546

Foreigners found in a state of vagrancy may be, at the end of their sentence, expelled from the national territory.

They are exempted from penalty if their repatriation is carried out without delay by the diplomatic or consular authorities to which they belong.

###### Art. 547

Any beggar or vagrant who is found carrying a weapon, or equipped with a skeleton key, hook or other instrument suitable either for committing thefts or other offences, or for providing them with the means to enter houses, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years.

##### Section 10 : Criminal groups

###### Art. 548

A criminal group constitutes any association formed or any agreement established, whatever its duration and the number of its members, with a view to preparing one or more crimes or offences.

###### Art. 549

Any person who joins or participates in a criminal group is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s).

The organizers or leaders of criminal groups are by this fact alone punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 550

Any person who knowingly habitually provides a meeting or retreat place for members of the group or stores weapons or instruments that have been used or are to be used for their criminal activities, is punished by the penalties of the first paragraph of the preceding article.

###### Art. 551

Any person who, before any participation in one of the crimes or offences prepared by the group, reports the existence of said group to the authorities is exempted from the penalties provided for in this section.

##### Section 11: Incitement to and glorification of crimes and offences

###### Art. 552

Any person who, by speeches, cries or threats uttered in public places or meetings, or by writings, printed matter, drawings, engravings, paintings, emblems, images or any other medium of writing, speech or image, sold or distributed, offered for sale or displayed in public places or meetings, or by placards or posters displayed to public view, or by any means of communication to the public by electronic means, has glorified or directly incited the commission of one of the following offences, is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs:

1) intentional attacks on life, intentional attacks on the physical integrity of the person, including sexual assaults, defined by Title II of this code;

2) thefts, extortions and destructions, damage and intentional deterioration dangerous to persons, defined by this code.

Any person who, by the means set out in the first paragraph, has glorified or directly incited, is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs:

1) one of the offences against national defence and State security, defined by Chapter VIII of this title;

2) acts of terrorism provided for by Title V of this code.

Any person who, by the means set out in the first paragraph, has glorified the crimes referred to in the first paragraph, war crimes, crimes against humanity or crimes of apartheid, is punished by the same penalties.

All seditious cries and songs uttered in public places or meetings are punished by one (01) to two (02) months' imprisonment or ten (10) days to sixty (60) days of community service and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 553

Any person who, by the means set out in the first paragraph of the preceding article, has directly incited discrimination, hatred or violence against a person or group of persons on the basis of their origin or their membership or non-membership of a particular ethnic group, nation, race or religion, or on the basis of their sex, gender or disability, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In the event of conviction for one of the acts provided for in the preceding paragraph, the court may also order:

1) deprivation of the rights provided for by articles 80 and following of this code, except in cases where it is excluded by law; 2) the posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced at the expense of the convicted person.

##### Section 12: Illegal entry into and exit from the territory

###### Art. 554

Illegal entry and exit means the crossing of borders when the legal or regulatory conditions for entry into or exit from Togolese territory are not satisfied.

Any person who contravenes these conditions is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

#### Chapitre II

Illicit manufacture and trafficking of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition

###### Art. 555: For the purposes of this article

1) the term "firearms" means any portable barrelled weapon that propels, is designed to propel or may be readily converted to propel shot, a bullet or a projectile by the action of an explosive, excluding antique firearms or their replicas;

2) the term "parts and components" means any element or replacement element specifically designed for a firearm and essential to its operation, including the barrel, frame or receiver, slide or cylinder, bolt or breech block, as well as any device designed or adapted to diminish the sound caused by a firearm shot;

3) the term "ammunition" means the complete round or its components, including cartridge cases, primers, propellant powder, bullets or projectiles, used in a firearm, provided that such components are subject to legal authorization.

###### Art. 556

The illicit manufacture of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition means the manufacture or assembly of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition:

1) without marking of firearms at the time of their manufacture;

2) from parts or components that have been the subject of illicit trafficking;

3) without a licence or authorization from the competent authority.

The illicit manufacture of firearms, their parts and components or ammunition, when committed intentionally, is punished by a term of five (05) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs with confiscation of the products and their accessories or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 557

The illicit trafficking of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition means the import, export, acquisition, sale, delivery, transport or transfer of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition from the territory of the Togolese State or through the latter to the territory of another State, either without legal authorization or without marking of the firearms.

The illicit trafficking of firearms, their parts, components and ammunition is punished by the same penalties as those provided for in the preceding article.

###### Art. 558

Any person who intentionally falsifies, erases, removes or unlawfully alters the mark or marks that a firearm must bear is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 559

The act of attempting, being an accomplice to, organizing, directing, facilitating, encouraging or favouring by means of aid or advice the commission of an offence established in accordance with this section is punished by the same penalties as the case may be.

#### Chapitre III

Employment, manufacture and storage of non-conventional weapons Section 1: Bacteriological weapons

###### Art. 560

"Bacteriological weapons" means microbiological or other biological agents, toxins whatever their origin or method of production, of types and in quantities that are not intended for peaceful purposes.

###### Art. 561

Any person who transfers, directly or indirectly, any of the agents, toxins, weapons, equipment or vectors referred to in the preceding articles, is punished by a term of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

The same penalty applies to any person who aids, encourages or incites in any manner whatsoever a State, group of States or international organization to manufacture or otherwise acquire any of the agents, toxins, weapons, equipment or vectors referred to in the preceding articles.

###### Art. 562

Any person who engages in the development, manufacture, stockpiling, acquisition or retention of:

1) microbial or other biological agents, as well as toxins whatever their origin or method of production, of types and in quantities that are not

intended for prophylactic, protective or other peaceful purposes;

2) weapons, equipment or means of delivery designed to use such agents or toxins for hostile purposes or in armed conflicts.

is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 2: Chemical weapons

###### Art. 563

"Chemical weapons" means the following, taken together or separately:

1) toxic chemicals and their precursors, except where intended for purposes not prohibited by the 1993 Convention on Chemical Weapons, as long as the types and quantities involved are consistent with such purposes;

2) munitions and devices specifically designed to cause death or other harm through the toxic action of the toxic chemicals defined in point 1, which would be released as a result of the employment of such munitions and devices;

3) any equipment specifically designed for use in direct connection with the employment of munitions and devices defined in point 2.

"Toxic chemicals" means any chemical which through its chemical action on biological processes can cause death, temporary incapacitation or permanent harm to humans or animals. This includes all such chemicals, whatever their origin or method of manufacture, whether they are produced in facilities, in munitions or elsewhere.

"Purposes not prohibited by the convention" means:

1) industrial, agricultural, research, medical, pharmaceutical or other peaceful purposes;

2) protective purposes, namely, purposes directly related to protection against toxic chemicals and protection against chemical weapons;

3) military purposes not connected with the use of chemical weapons and not dependent on the use of the toxic properties of chemicals as a method of warfare;

4) law enforcement purposes including domestic riot control.

The use, development, manufacture, acquisition, stockpiling or retention of chemical weapons by riot control agents is a prerogative of the State for the purposes of maintaining public order. These activities are therefore prohibited to all other persons.

###### Art. 564

Any person who without prior authorization:

1) develops, manufactures, acquires, stockpiles or retains chemical weapons, or transfers, directly or indirectly, chemical weapons to anyone;

2) uses chemical weapons;

3) undertakes any military preparations whatsoever for the use of chemical weapons;

4) uses riot control agents as a method of warfare;

5) possesses a chemical weapons production facility, constructs a new chemical weapons facility or modifies an existing facility to convert it into a chemical weapons production facility;

6) manufactures, acquires, retains, uses or transfers on the territory of a State not party to the Convention a Schedule 1 chemical;

7) manufactures, acquires, retains, uses or transfers on Togolese territory a Schedule 1 chemical; unless such chemicals are for medical, pharmaceutical or protective purposes and their type and quantity are strictly limited to what can be justified for such purposes;

8) exports a Schedule 1 chemical, previously imported into Togo, to a third State;

9) unlawfully exports to, or unlawfully imports from a State not party to the Convention a Schedule 1 or 2 chemical;

10) unlawfully exports a Schedule 3 chemical to a State not party to the Convention;

11) assists, encourages or induces another person, in any manner whatsoever, to undertake the activities mentioned in the preceding points.

is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 565

The criminal offences mentioned in the preceding article committed outside Togolese territory are deemed to be committed on Togolese territory. Togolese courts have jurisdiction to try them if they are committed by a Togolese national or in any place under the control of the Togolese State.

For the purposes of this article, "Schedule 1, 2 and 3" means the Schedules appearing in the Annex to the Convention of 13 January 1993 on the Prohibition of the Development, Production, Stockpiling and Use of Chemical Weapons and on their Destruction.

##### Section 3: Conventional weapons

###### Art. 566

Any person who uses a weapon the primary effect of which is to injure by fragments which are not detectable by X-rays in the human body, is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 567

Without prejudice to the implementing law of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Use, Stockpiling, Production and Transfer of Anti-Personnel Mines adopted in Ottawa on 4 December 1997, any person who uses or directs mines, booby-traps or other devices:

1) which are designed to cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering, or are of a nature to cause such injury or such suffering;

2) which are equipped with a mechanism or device specifically designed to detonate in the absence of contact, under the influence of a magnetic field or other influence generated by the presence of a commonly available mine detector, normally used for detection operations;

3) against the civilian population as such or against individual civilians, or against civilian objects, whether in offence, defence or by way of reprisals;

4) indiscriminately.

is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

"Indiscriminate use" means any placement of such weapons:

1) other than on a military objective, or such that these weapons are not directed against such an objective; or

2) which employs a method or means of delivery on the objective such that these weapons cannot be directed against a specific military objective; or

3) which may be expected to cause incidental loss of civilian life, injury to civilians, damage to civilian objects, or a combination of these losses and damage, which would be excessive in relation to the concrete and direct military advantage anticipated.

###### Art. 568

Any person who employs mines other than remotely delivered mines, booby-traps or other devices in any city, town, village or other area containing a similar concentration of civilians in which combat between ground forces is not taking place or does not appear to be imminent, is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, unless:

1) they are placed on or in the close vicinity of a military objective belonging to or under the control of an adverse party; or 2) measures such as the posting of sentries, the issuing of warnings or the erecting of fences are taken to protect the civilian population against the effects of such weapons.

###### Art. 569

Any person who employs remotely delivered mines is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, unless:

1) they are recorded in accordance with the provisions of Protocol II to the Convention on Prohibitions or Restrictions on the Use of Certain Conventional Weapons of 10 October 1980, as amended on 3 May 1996; 2) to the extent feasible, they are equipped with an effective self-destruction or self-neutralization mechanism and have a back-up self-deactivation feature designed so that the mines will no longer function as such when they no longer serve the military purpose for which they were placed.

###### Art. 570

Any person who employs mines that are self-deactivating, or that are equipped with an anti-handling device designed to remain capable of functioning after the mines have ceased to do so, is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 571

Without prejudice to the rules of international law applicable in armed conflict relating to treachery and perfidy, any person who employs booby-traps or other devices which have the appearance of harmless portable objects but which are in fact specifically designed and constructed to contain explosive material, or booby-traps and other devices which are in any way attached to or associated with:

1) internationally recognized protective emblems, signs or signals;

2) sick, wounded or dead persons;

3) burial or cremation sites, or graves;

4) medical facilities, equipment, supplies or transports; children's toys or other portable objects or products specially designed for the feeding, health, hygiene, clothing or education of children;

5) food or drink;

6) kitchen utensils or appliances, except in military establishments, military locations and military supply depots;

7) objects clearly of a religious nature; 8) historic monuments, works of art or places of worship which constitute the cultural or spiritual heritage of peoples;

9) animals or animal carcasses.

is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 572

"Incendiary weapon" means any weapon or munition which is primarily designed to set fire to objects or to cause burn injury to persons through the action of flame, heat, or a combination thereof, produced by a chemical reaction of a substance delivered on the target.

Incendiary weapons can be in the form of, for example, flame throwers, fougasses, shells, rockets, grenades, mines, bombs and other containers of incendiary substances.

Incendiary weapons do not include:

1) munitions which may have incidental incendiary effects, such as illuminants, tracers, smoke or signalling systems;

2) munitions designed to combine penetration, blast or fragmentation effects with an incendiary effect, such as armour-piercing projectiles, fragmentation shells, explosive bombs and similar combined-effects munitions in which the incendiary effect is not specifically designed to cause burn injury to persons, but to be used against military objectives, such as armoured vehicles, aircraft and installations or facilities.

"Concentration of civilians" means any concentration of civilians, be it permanent or temporary, such as in inhabited parts of cities, or inhabited towns or villages, or as in camps or columns of refugees or evacuees, or groups of nomads.

"Military objective" means, so far as objects are concerned, any object which by its nature, location, purpose or use makes an effective contribution to military action and whose total or partial destruction, capture or neutralization, in the circumstances ruling at the time, offers a definite military advantage.

"Civilian objects" means all objects which are not military objectives.

"Feasible precautions" means those precautions which are practicable or practically possible taking into account all circumstances ruling at the time, including humanitarian and military considerations.

###### Art. 573

Any person who:

1) makes the civilian population as such, individual civilians or civilian objects the object of attack by incendiary weapons;

2) makes any military objective located within a concentration of civilians the object of attack by air-delivered incendiary weapons;

3) makes any military objective located within a concentration of civilians the object of attack by means of incendiary weapons other than air-delivered incendiary weapons, except when such military objective is clearly separated from the concentration of civilians and all feasible precautions are taken to limit the incendiary effects to the military objective and to avoid, and in any event to minimize, incidental loss of civilian life, injury to civilians and damage to civilian objects;

4) makes forests or other kinds of plant cover the object of attack by incendiary weapons except when such natural elements are used to cover, conceal or camouflage combatants or other military objectives, or are themselves military objectives.

is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 574

"Laser weapons" means any weapon specifically designed, the function of which or one of the combat functions of which is to cause permanent blindness to unenhanced vision, that is to the naked eye or to the eye with corrective eyesight devices.

###### Art. 575

Any person who employs a laser weapon is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

##### Section 4: Cluster munitions

###### Art. 576: Is not a cluster munition

1) a munition or submunition designed to dispense flares, smoke, pyrotechnics or decoys, or a munition designed exclusively for air defence purposes;

2) a munition or submunition designed to produce electrical or electronic effects;

3) a munition which, in order to avoid indiscriminate area effects and the risks posed by unexploded submunitions, has all of the following characteristics:

a) each munition contains fewer than ten (10) explosive submunitions;

b) each explosive submunition weighs more than four (04) kilograms;

c) each explosive submunition is designed to detect and engage a single target object;

d) each explosive submunition is equipped with an electronic self-destruction mechanism;

e) each explosive submunition is equipped with an electronic self-deactivating feature.

###### Art. 577

Any person who commits any of the following acts is punished by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs:

1) uses cluster munitions;

2) develops, produces, otherwise acquires, stockpiles, retains, or transfers to any other person, directly or indirectly, cluster munitions; 3) assists, encourages or induces any other person to engage in any activity prohibited in points 1 and 2 of this paragraph.

"Cluster munition" means a conventional munition that is designed to disperse or release explosive submunitions each weighing less than twenty (20) kilograms, and includes those explosive submunitions.

###### Art. 578

The provisions of article 576 apply, mutatis mutandis, to explosive bomblets which are specifically designed to be dispersed or released from dispensers affixed to aircraft.

###### Art. 579

The prohibitions and restrictions provided for in this section apply both in cases of international armed conflicts and armed conflicts not of an international character occurring on national territory.

#### Chapter IV: Escapes

###### Art. 580

An escape is constituted by the act of a person escaping from the place where he or she was held in detention as a convicted person, accused person or in police custody, which is punishable if the detainee evades the custody to which he or she is subject by violence, breaking or corruption with or without the connivance of a third party.

###### Art. 581

Any agent of the public force assigned to guard a detainee who through negligence facilitates the escape of that detainee is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months.

###### Art. 582

Any guard or agent assigned to guard a detainee who knowingly procures his or her escape or attempts to procure it is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s). He or she is furthermore stripped of his or her public employment.

###### Art. 583

Any person who voluntarily facilitates the escape of a detainee by providing him or her with information, instruments, clothing, a vehicle or any other means of flight or hiding, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years.

The sentence is doubled if by reason of his or her status or function the offender had the possibility of communicating freely with the detainee.

###### Art. 584

Any person who delivers or causes to be delivered correspondence, a package, a sum of money, or any object whatsoever to a detainee outside the channels provided for by the regulations or in defiance of a solitary confinement order, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties. If the offender has abused the facilities offered by his or her profession for this delivery, he or she is furthermore stripped of the right to exercise that profession for up to three (03) years and the penalties are doubled.

###### Art. 585

Any detainee who, by any means whatsoever, escapes either from a penitentiary establishment, or from a police custody facility, or from a hospital or health establishment where he or she was receiving treatment, or while employed outside a penitentiary establishment, or during a transfer or who does not return to the penitentiary establishment at the end of a leave of absence, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years.

#### Chapter V: Breaches of the duty of probity

###### Art. 586: For the purposes of this chapter, the following terms mean

1) public agent:

- any person who holds an elective, executive, administrative or judicial mandate, whether appointed or elected on a permanent or temporary basis, whether remunerated or not and whatever his or her hierarchical level;

- any person who exercises a public function or is vested with a public service mission, including for a public body or public enterprise, or who provides a service, as these terms are defined in Togolese law;

- the agents of any private law person charged with the execution of a public service or a contract whatever the modalities by which the mission is entrusted to it;

- any other person defined as such in Togolese law.

2) foreign public agent: any person who holds a legislative, executive, administrative or judicial mandate of a foreign country, whether appointed or elected, and any person who exercises a public function for a foreign country, including for a public body or public enterprise;

3) official of a public international organization: an international official or any person authorized by such an organization to act on its behalf.

##### Section 1: Misappropriation and embezzlement of public funds and property

###### Subsection 1: Misappropriation and embezzlement of public funds

###### Art. 586

bis: Any agent or employee of the State, of a secondary territorial authority, of a public establishment, of a company in which the State or another public authority has taken a stake and more generally any agent or employee of a legal person under public law, who fraudulently misappropriates, suppresses, diverts or dissipates public funds or effects in lieu thereof, or documents, titles, deeds, movable effects which were in his or her hands by reason of or on the occasion of the exercise of his or her functions, is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

When the amount of the thing diverted or dissipated is greater than five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs and less than ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, the sentence is criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

When the amount of the thing diverted or dissipated is equal to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs and less than one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, the sentence is criminal imprisonment of ten (10) to twenty (20) years and a fine of at least ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs without the said fine being able to exceed one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

When the amount of the thing diverted or dissipated is equal to or greater than one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, the sentence is the maximum term of imprisonment and a fine of at least one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

If the offender was exercising the functions of public accountant at the time of the commission of the offence, the sentence is doubled.

###### Art. 587

Any natural or legal person, whether a trader or not, any manager, who by common agreement with a public agent has overvalued the value or sale price, rental price of a good, service or supply in relation to the commonly practiced price, is guilty of the crime of misappropriation of public funds and is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine at least equal to triple the value of the sums dissipated.

###### Art. 588

Receivers of misappropriated and embezzled goods or assets are punished by the penalties applied to the principal perpetrators and their accomplices.

###### Art. 589

The perpetrators of the offences provided for and punished by this subsection may not in any case benefit from the mitigating circumstances and suspended sentences provided for by articles 36 to 38 and 97 to 98 of this code. The administrative act establishing the sum owed to the public treasury by these same persons does not prejudice the exercise of the public prosecution, nor the judgment on the offences committed.

Soùs-sécfion 2 : Des détournements de biens publics

###### Art. 590

Any agent or employee of the State or of a legal person under public law who fraudulently uses vehicles, service equipment or furniture assigned to the public service outside regulated conditions is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 591

Any agent or employee of the State or of a legal person under public law who, through negligence, lack of care or caution has caused the disabling or premature wear of a vehicle, machine or any other service equipment assigned to his employment is punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

The offender is exempt from prosecution if he ensures at his own expense the replacement or repair of the damaged equipment.

##### Section 2 : De la concussion

###### Art. 592

Constitutes extortion, the act, by a person holding public authority or charged with a public service mission, of receiving, demanding or ordering the collection as duties or contributions, taxes or public levies, of a sum which he knows is not due, or exceeds what is due.

###### Art. 593

Any person guilty of extortion is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

##### Section 3 : De la corruption et des infractions assimilées

###### Sous-section 1 : De la corruption

###### Paragraphe 1 : De la corruption des agents publics nationaux

###### Art. 594 : Constitue une corruption des agents publics nationaux, le fait par

1) toute personne dépositaire de l'autorité publique ou chargée d'une mission de service public ou investie d'un mandat électif public ou tout agent de l'Etat de solliciter ou d'agréer, sans droit, directement ou indirectement, des offres, des promesses, des dons, des présents ou des avantages quelconques pour lui-même, pour autrui ou une entité afin d'accomplir ou de s'abstenir d'accomplir un acte de sa fonction, de sa mission ou de son mandat ;

2) tout magistrat, juré ou toute autre personne siégeant dans une formation juridictionnelle, tout fonctionnaire au greffe d'une juridiction, tout arbitre ou tout expert nommé soit par une juridiction, soit par les parties ou toute personne chargée par l'autorité judiciaire d'une mission de conciliation ou de médiation, de solliciter ou d'agréer, sans droit, à tout moment, directement ou indirectement, des offres, des promesses, des dons, des présents ou des avantages quelconques, pour lui-même ou pour autrui ou une entité pour l'accomplissement ou l'abstention d'un acte de sa fonction ;

3) toute personne de proposer à tout moment des offres, des promesses, des dons, des présents ou des avantages quelconques, pour elle-même, pour autrui ou une entité afin d'obtenir d'une des personnes citées aux points 1 et 2 ci-dessus, l'accomplissement ou l'abstention d'un acte de sa fonction, ou de céder aux sollicitations de ces personnes.

###### Art. 595

Any person guilty of corruption within the meaning of the preceding article is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to double the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without the said fine being less than two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 596

When the offence defined in article 595 of this code is committed for the benefit or to the detriment of a person subject to criminal proceedings, the perpetrator incurs a term of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to five times the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without the said fine being less than two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

The court seized shall pronounce, where applicable, in the cases provided for by this article the following additional penalties:

1) civic disqualification;
2) confiscation of sums or objects irregularly received by the perpetrator of the offence, with the exception of objects capable of restitution. If the thing has not been seized or cannot be produced, confiscation is ordered by value;
3) posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced.

3) posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced.

###### Paragraphe 2

De la corruption des agents publics étrangers et des fonctionnaires internationaux

###### Art. 597

Constitutes corruption of foreign public agents and international officials, the act for:

1) any foreign public agent or international official of soliciting or accepting without right and at any time, directly or indirectly, offers or promises, receiving gifts or presents or other undue advantages for himself or for another person or entity, to perform or refrain from performing an act of his official functions or employment, or with a view to granting, obtaining, causing to be obtained, retaining or causing to be retained a contract or other undue advantage in international trade; 2) any person of offering or granting, without right and at any time, directly or indirectly to a foreign public agent or to an international official, promises, gifts or presents or other undue advantages, for himself or for another person or entity, so that he performs or refrains from performing an act of his official functions or employment, or with a view to granting, obtaining, causing to be obtained, retaining or causing to be retained a contract or other undue advantage in international trade.

###### Art. 598

Any person guilty of corruption within the meaning of the preceding article is punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to five times the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without the said fine being less than two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Article 599

The court seized shall pronounce, where applicable, the following additional sanctions against foreign public agents and international officials:

1) confiscation of sums, objects or assets irregularly acquired or held. If the thing has not been seized or cannot be produced, confiscation is ordered by value;

2) posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced at the expense of the convicted person;

3) prohibition from residing in Togo for a period not exceeding ten (10) years after execution of the sentence.

###### Paragraphe 3 : Corruption in the private sector

###### Art. 600 : Constitutes corruption of private sector agents, the act by

1) any person of promising, offering or granting, without right and at any time, directly or indirectly, an undue advantage to any other person who directs a private sector entity or works for such an entity, in any capacity whatsoever, for themselves or for another person so that, in violation of their duties, they perform or refrain from performing an act;

2) any person who directs a private sector entity or works for such an entity, in any capacity whatsoever, of soliciting or accepting, without right and at any time, directly or indirectly, an undue advantage for themselves or for another person, in order to perform or refrain from performing an act in violation of their duties.

###### Art. 601

Any person guilty of corruption within the meaning of the preceding article is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to five times the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without said fine being less than two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 602

The court seized shall pronounce, where applicable, against the convicted person the following complementary penalties:

1) the confiscation of sums, objects or assets irregularly acquired or held. If the thing has not been seized or cannot be produced, confiscation is ordered in value;

2) the posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced at the expense of the convicted person;

3) the prohibition from residing in Togo for a period not exceeding ten (10) years after the execution of the sentence; if the convicted person is a foreigner.

###### Art. 603

Any agreement is prohibited whereby a pharmacist provides to a physician, a dental surgeon, a veterinarian, a paramedic, a benefit of any nature whatsoever, on the sale of pharmaceutical medicinal, cosmetic or hygienic products that they may prescribe.

###### Art. 604

The act is prohibited for anyone who exercises the profession of physician, dental surgeon, veterinarian and paramedic of receiving, in any form whatsoever, directly or indirectly, interests or rebates proportionate or not to the number of units prescribed, whether they are medicines or products falling under the pharmaceutical monopoly.

###### Art. 605

The establishment and operation of companies whose manifest purpose is the pursuit of interests or rebates defined above and accruing to the individuals themselves or to the group formed for this purpose, as well as the exercise, for the same purpose, of the profession of pharmacist and that of physician, dental surgeon, veterinarian and paramedic, are prohibited.

###### Art. 606

The offences referred to in articles 603 to 605 are punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs.

The co-perpetrators of the offence are liable to the same penalties.

In case of recidivism, the prohibition from exercising the profession for a period of five (05) to ten (10) years may be pronounced by the courts in addition to the principal penalty.

###### Sous-section 2 : Offences assimilated to corruption

###### Art. 607

The offences assimilated to corruption are: trading in influence, abuse of function, illegal taking of interest, illicit enrichment.

###### Paragraphe 1 : Trading in influence

###### Art. 608 : Constitutes trading in influence the act for

1) any person, of proposing, without right, at any time, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts, presents or any advantages whatsoever to a person holding public authority, charged with a public service mission or invested with an elective public mandate, for themselves or for others, so that they abuse their real or supposed influence in order to obtain from an authority or a public administration distinctions, jobs, contracts or any other favourable decision;

2) a person holding public authority, charged with a public service mission, or invested with an elective public mandate, of soliciting or accepting, without right, at any time, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts, presents or any advantages whatsoever for themselves or for others to abuse their real or supposed influence in order to obtain from an authority or a public administration distinctions, jobs, contracts or any other favourable decision;

3) any person, of soliciting or accepting, at any time, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts, presents or any advantages whatsoever, for themselves or for others, in order to abuse their real or supposed influence in order to obtain any decision or any favourable opinion from a person referred to in article 595 paragraph 2, when they exercise their functions within or before a court or when they are appointed by such a court;

4) any person, of proposing, without right, at any time, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts, presents or any advantages whatsoever to a person, for themselves or for others, so that they abuse their real or supposed influence in order to obtain any decision or favourable opinion from a person referred to in article 601 paragraph 2, when they exercise their functions within or before a court or when they are appointed by such a court;

5) any person, of yielding to another person who solicits from them, at any time, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts or presents or any advantages whatsoever, for themselves or for others, in order to abuse their real or supposed influence in order to obtain from a person referred to in article 601 paragraph 2, any decision or any favourable opinion.

###### Art. 609

Any person guilty of trading in influence is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine equal to twice the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without said fine being less than one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 610

The court seized may pronounce, where applicable, the complementary penalties provided for in article 599 above against the convicted person.

###### Paragraphe 2 : Abuse of functions

###### Art. 611

Constitutes abuse of functions, the act for a public agent in the exercise of their functions, of performing or refraining from performing intentionally, an act in violation of laws or regulations in order to obtain an undue advantage for themselves, for another person or an entity.

###### Art. 612

Any person guilty of abuse of functions is punished by a sentence of six. (06) months to two. (02) years of imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand. (500,000) to two million. (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.
