Are punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs:

1) any person who:

a) contravenes the prohibition provided for by article 150 of the labour code, subject to the provisions relating to apprenticeship, of the employment of children under fifteen (15) years of age or the performance of any type of work by children under fifteen (15) years of age, except for derogations provided for by order of the minister responsible for labour, issued after consultation with the national labour council taking into account local circumstances and the tasks that may be required.

b) maintains a disabled person or a child in employment recognized as beyond their capacities and does not assign them to suitable employment or, if this is not possible, does not terminate the contract with payment of notice compensation and possibly severance pay provided for by article 155 paragraph 2 of the labour code;

c) contravenes the provisions of article 156 of the labour code establishing a mandatory weekly rest of a minimum duration of twenty-four (24) consecutive hours; d) contravenes the obligation of any enterprise or establishment, of whatever nature, to provide an occupational safety and health service to its workers, as provided for by article 175 paragraph 1 of the labour code;

e) contravenes the obligation of the employer, provided for by article 180 of the labour code, in case of illness of a worker or a member of their family housed with them at the expense of the enterprise, to provide them with free care and medicines within the limits of the means defined by the labour code, and to provide free food to any sick worker treated at the workplace;

f) contravenes the obligation of the employer, provided for by article 181 of the labour code, relating to the transport of injured and sick persons.

2) The perpetrators of offences against the provisions of the orders provided for in articles 150, 156 and 175 of the labour code.

In cases of offences against the provisions of the orders provided for by articles 150 and 175 paragraph 1 of the labour code, recidivism may, in addition, be punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years.

If there is double recidivism, the term of imprisonment is mandatory.

###### Art. 882

Are punished by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs and/or one of these two penalties:

1) any person who contravenes:

a) the prohibition on the employer, provided for by article 90 of the labour code, from inflicting fines on a worker or imposing a double sanction for the same offence;

b) the regime of public holidays and the obligation to post the list and regime of public holidays at the workplace, as provided for by article 157 of the labour code;

c) the provisions of article 158 of the labour code relating to the right of workers to paid leave;

d) the provisions of article 160 of the labour code relating to the leave allowance paid by the employer to the worker before departure on leave and travel time;

e) the provisions of article 166 of the labour code relating to the right of a worker who has ceased service to assert against their former employer rights relating to leave, travel, in case of actual displacement of the worker, and transport within a period of five (05) years from the day of cessation of work with said employer;

f) the provisions of article 167 of the labour code relating to the right of the worker to demand payment in cash of the amount of repatriation costs to be borne by the employer within the limits of the security deposit that they prove to have paid;

g) the terms of the formal notice sent by the labour and social legislation inspector to the employer to remedy working conditions dangerous to the safety or health of workers, not covered by the texts provided for in article 169 of the labour code, within a specified period, as provided for by article 171 of the labour code;

2) any person who contravenes the provisions of:

a) article 35 of the labour code relating to prohibited or limited hirings established on an exceptional basis and for reasons of economic and social order decided by order of the minister responsible for labour, after consultation with the national labour council;

b) article 147 of the labour code relating to work prohibited for pregnant women, the nature of which is determined by order of the minister responsible for labour issued after consultation with the national labour council;

c) article 185 of the labour code relating to the prohibition for labour and social legislation inspectors from holding any interest whatsoever, direct or indirect, in enterprises placed under their control.

Likewise, the perpetrators of offences against the provisions of article 150 paragraph 4 of the labour code relating to the subjection of children to the worst forms of labour are punished by five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these penalties, without prejudice to the application of special legal provisions relating to the trafficking or trade of children.

In case of recidivism, the sanctions are doubled.

###### Art. 883

Are punished by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties:

1) the perpetrators of offences against the provisions of:

a) article 83 of the labour code relating to the obligation of the head of enterprise who receives from a worker a security deposit in cash or in securities to issue a receipt for it and record it in detail in the employer's register;

b) article 84 of the labour code relating to the procedures for depositing the security deposit given by the worker to the employer;

c) articles 136 and 137 of the labour code setting out the procedures for deductions from wages;

d) article 148 relating to the suspension by a pregnant woman of her work;

e) article 154 setting the minimum daily rest for children at twelve (12) consecutive hours.

2) the perpetrators of offences against the provisions of the orders provided for by articles 108 and 121 of the labour code, relating respectively to working conditions for a specific profession and to inter-occupational minimum wages;

3) any person who employs a worker of foreign nationality holding a worker's booklet or work card or holding a booklet issued for a profession other than that of the employment actually performed;

4) any person who hires a foreign worker whose contract with a previous employer has not either expired or been terminated by judicial decision.

###### Art. 884

Are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) francs or one of these two penalties:

1) persons who knowingly make a false declaration of a work accident or occupational disease; 2) any person who, by using a fictitious contract or a work record containing inaccurate information, gets hired or voluntarily substitutes himself for another worker;

3) any employer, authorized representative or agent, who knowingly enters false attestations on the work record, employer register or any other document relating to the duration and conditions of work performed by the worker, as well as any worker who knowingly makes use of these attestations;

4) any employer, authorized representative or agent, who knowingly hires, attempts to hire or retain in his service a worker still bound to another employer by an employment contract, an apprentice still bound by an apprenticeship contract or a trainee undergoing vocational training, independently of the right to damages that may be recognized to the injured party;

5) any person who demands or accepts from the worker any remuneration as an intermediary in the settlement or payment of wages, compensation, allowances and expenses of any nature.

In case of recidivism, the term of imprisonment is six (06) months to two (02) years and the fine is one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 885

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, anyone who infringes or attempts to infringe either the free designation of staff delegates or the regular exercise of their functions.

In case of recidivism within one (01) year, the term of imprisonment is mandatory.

The infractions are recorded by the labor and social legislation inspector.

###### Art. 886

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who, by violence, threat, fraud or promise, compels or attempts to compel a worker to hire himself against his will, or who, by the same means, attempts to prevent or prevents him from hiring himself or from fulfilling the obligations imposed by his contract.

###### Art. 887

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of fifteen (15) days to thirty (30) days of community service and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who opposes or attempts to oppose the execution of obligations or the exercise of powers incumbent upon labor and social legislation inspectors and controllers.

In case of recidivism, the penalty is increased to two (02) months of imprisonment.

The provisions of this code which provide for and punish acts of resistance, insults and violence against representatives of public authority are, in addition, applicable to those who are guilty of acts of the same nature towards labor and social legislation inspectors and their deputies.

###### Art. 888

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years at most and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) francs, any employer who withholds or uses for his personal interest or for the needs of his enterprise, sums or securities remitted as security deposit.

###### Art. 899

Are punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, the perpetrators of infractions to the provisions of:

1) article 39 of the labor code relating to unjustified grounds for sanction or dismissal of workers, and withdrawal of a person from a recruitment procedure;

2) article 40 of the labor code relating to the prohibition of sanctioning or dismissing a worker for having suffered, refused to suffer, reported or testified to acts of sexual harassment, as defined in article 392 of the labor code, by an employer, his representative or any other employee of the employer.

In case of recidivism, the penalty is doubled.

###### Art. 890

Persons guilty of having usurped the functions of labor and social legislation inspector, labor medical inspector or labor and social legislation controller, are punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

#### Chapitre II : Des infractions relatives à la sécurité sociale

###### Art. 891

The employer who has contravened the prescriptions of the social security code and its implementing texts or who opposes the registration of his worker is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs without prejudice to the conviction by the same judgment to payment of contributions and surcharges the payment of which was incumbent upon him.

The fine is applied as many times as there are persons employed in conditions contrary to the prescriptions of the social security code.

There is recidivism when, in the twelve (12) months prior to the expiry date of the fifteen-day period granted by the formal notice, the offender has already been the subject of a conviction for an identical infraction.

###### Art. 892

The employer who has unduly withheld the contribution of an employee deducted from the wages of the latter under the superior pensions scheme, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) days to three (03) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In case of recidivism within three (03) years, he is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 893

Anyone who is guilty of fraud or false declarations to obtain or attempt to have obtained benefits that are not due, is liable to a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, without prejudice to penalties resulting from the application of other laws.

He is further required to reimburse the social security fund for benefits unduly paid.

Is punished by the same penalties anyone who is guilty of fraud or false declarations to reduce or attempt to reduce the remuneration on which social contributions are based in order to reduce the contributions to be paid, without prejudice to penalties resulting from the application of other laws.

He is further required to pay back to the fund the reduced contributions, including penalties and late payment surcharges. The maximum of both penalties is always applied to the offender in case of recidivism within one (01) year.

###### Art. 894

For all cases provided for in the articles of this chapter, the court may order that the judgment be published in the press and posted in the places it indicates, at the expense of the offender.

### TITLE IX: ECONOMIC, BANKING AND FINANCIAL OFFENCES

#### Chapter I: Money Laundering

##### Section 1: General Provisions

###### Paragraph 1: Simple Money Laundering

###### Art. 895

Money laundering is the act of enabling, by any means, the false justification of the origin of property and income of the perpetrator of a crime or offence that has procured for that person a direct or indirect profit.

Money laundering is also the act of:

1) participating in an operation enabling the concealment or conversion of the direct or indirect proceeds of a crime or offence;

2) acquiring, holding or using property and resources knowing that they come, directly or indirectly, from a crime or offence.

Simple money laundering is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Paragraph 2: Aggravated Money Laundering

###### Art. 896

Punishable by double the term of imprisonment provided for in article 895 and a fine that cannot be less than the higher of the sums between double the fine provided for in article 895 and one-third of the value of the property or funds that were the subject of the money laundering operations, is money laundering committed habitually or by using the means provided by the exercise of a professional activity of whatever nature.

##### Section 2: Specific Provisions Relating to Capital Laundering

###### Art. 897

Capital laundering is defined as the offence constituted by one or more of the acts listed below, committed intentionally:

1) the conversion, transfer or manipulation of property, which the perpetrator knows comes from a crime or offence or from participation in that crime or offence, for the purpose of concealing or disguising the illicit origin of said property or of helping any person involved in the commission of that crime or offence to escape the judicial consequences of their acts;

2) the concealment, disguising of the nature, origin, location, disposition, movement or actual ownership of property or rights relating thereto which the perpetrator knows come from a crime or offence, as defined by the national legislations of the member States of the West African Economic and Monetary Union (WAEMU) or from participation in that crime or offence;

3) the acquisition, holding or use of property which the perpetrator knows, at the time of receiving said property, comes from a crime or offence or from participation in that crime or offence.

There is capital laundering even if the facts that are at the origin of the acquisition, holding and transfer of the property to be laundered are committed on the territory of another WAEMU member State or on that of a third State.

###### Art. 898

Natural persons guilty of a capital laundering offence are punished by a term of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to three times the value of the property or funds that were the subject of the money laundering operations.

Attempted money laundering is punished by the same penalties.

###### Art. 899

Conspiracy or participation in an association with a view to committing an act constituting capital laundering, association to commit said act, aiding, inciting or advising a natural or legal person, with a view to executing it or facilitating its execution are punished by the same penalties provided for in the preceding article.

###### Art. 900: The penalties provided for in the preceding article are doubled

1) when the capital laundering offence is committed habitually or by using the facilities provided by the exercise of a professional activity, of whatever nature;

2) when the perpetrator of the offence is in a state of recidivism, in which case, convictions pronounced abroad are taken into account to establish recidivism;

3) when the money laundering offence is committed by an organized gang.

When the crime or offence from which the property or sums of money that were the subject of the laundering comes is punished by a custodial sentence of a duration greater than that of the imprisonment incurred under the preceding article, the money laundering is punished by the penalties attached to the original offence of which its perpetrator had knowledge and, if that offence is accompanied by aggravating circumstances, by the penalties attached only to the circumstances of which they had knowledge.

###### Art. 901

Persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons who, in the course of their profession, carry out, control or advise on transactions involving deposits, exchanges, investments, conversions or any other movements of capital or any other property, are punished by imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, when they have intentionally:

1) made disclosures to the owner of the sums or to the author of the transactions listed in paragraph 1 of this article, concerning the declaration they are required to make or the follow-up action taken;

2) destroyed or removed documents or records relating to the identification obligations provided for by legislative provisions relating to the fight against money laundering;

3) carried out or attempted to carry out, under a false identity, one of the following transactions:

a) transactions involving deposits, exchanges, investments, conversions or any other movements of capital or any other property;

b) foreign exchange transactions, capital movements and settlements of any kind;

c) opening of an account and custody of securities, stocks or bonds, allocation of a safe deposit box and establishment of any other business relationships;

d) transactions involving a sum in cash equal to or greater than five million (5,000,000) CFA francs;

e) transactions carried out on behalf of another person; f) payments in cash or by bearer instrument of a sum equal to or greater than fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs and any transaction involving a sum equal to or greater than ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs when it is carried out under unusual conditions of complexity or does not appear to have any economic justification or lawful purpose;

g) manual foreign exchange transactions subject to no regulatory limit, likely to be carried out for the purposes of money laundering, when their amount reaches five million (5,000,000) CFA francs;

h) contribution or exchange of gaming chips or plaques, in a casino or any other gaming establishment, for a sum greater than or equal to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or whose equivalent value is greater than or equal to that sum;

4) informed by any means the person or persons subject to an investigation established for money laundering offences of which they became aware, by reason of their profession or their functions;

5) communicated to the judicial authorities or to the officials competent to establish the original and subsequent offences, acts or documents provided for by law, which they know to be falsified or erroneous;

6) communicated information or documents to persons other than those provided for by legislative provisions relating to identification obligations in the fight against money laundering;

7) failed to make the suspicious transaction report as provided for by law, when the circumstances lead to the conclusion that the sums of money may originate from a money laundering offence.

Persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons referred to in paragraph 1 of this article are punished by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, when they, unintentionally:

1) fail to make the suspicious transaction report provided for by law;

2) contravene legislative provisions relating to foreign exchange regulations, identification measures, the retention and communication of documents, certain transactions in casinos and gaming establishments, and the obligation to report suspicious transactions in the fight against money laundering.

###### Art. 902

Natural persons guilty of the offences defined in articles 898 to 901 above may also incur the following additional penalties:

1) permanent prohibition from national territory or for a period of one (01) to five (05) year(s) against any convicted foreigner;

2) prohibition from residing for a period of one (01) to three (03) year(s) in certain prefectures of the country;

3) prohibition from leaving national territory and withdrawal of passport for a period of one (01) to three (03) year(s);

4) prohibition of civil and political rights for a period of one (01) to three (03) year(s);

5) prohibition from driving motorized land, sea and air vehicles and withdrawal of permits or licenses for a period of three (03) to five (05) years;

6) permanent prohibition or for a period of five (05) to ten (10) years from exercising the profession or activity in connection with which the offence was committed and prohibition from exercising a public function;

7) prohibition from issuing cheques, other than those which allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those which are certified, and from using payment cards for five (05) to ten (10) years;

8) prohibition from holding or carrying a weapon subject to authorization for five (05) to ten (10) years;

9) confiscation of all or part of the property of lawful origin of the convicted person;

10) confiscation of the property or thing which was used or intended to commit the offence or the thing which is the proceeds thereof; with the exception of objects capable of restitution.

###### Art. 903

Legal persons other than the State, on behalf of or for the benefit of which a money laundering offence or one of the offences provided for in this section has been committed by one of its organs or representatives, are punished by one of the penalties provided for in article 54 of this code, without prejudice to the conviction of natural persons as perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts.

Legal persons, other than the State, may, in addition, be sentenced to one or more of the following penalties:

1) those provided for in article 54 of this code; 2) placement under judicial supervision for a period of not more than five (05) years;

3) prohibition, permanently, or for a period of not more than five (05) to ten (10) years, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more professional or social activities in connection with which the offence was committed;

4) posting of the decision rendered or dissemination thereof by the written press or by any audiovisual means of communication, at the expense of the convicted legal person, in accordance with the provisions of article 142 of this code.

The sanctions provided for in points 2, 3, 4 of the preceding paragraph, the permanent closure of an establishment and the dissolution of the legal person are not applicable to financial institutions subject to a supervisory authority having disciplinary power.

The competent supervisory authority, referred to by the public prosecutor of any prosecution initiated against a financial institution, may take appropriate sanctions, in accordance with specific legislative and regulatory texts in force.

###### Art. 904

Any person guilty, on the one hand, of participation in an association or agreement, with a view to committing one of the offences provided for in articles 898 to 902 above and, on the other hand, of aiding, inciting or advising a natural or legal person, with a view to executing them or facilitating their execution, is exempted from criminal sanctions if, having disclosed the existence of this agreement, association, aid or advice to the judicial authority, they thereby allow, on the one hand, the identification of the other persons involved and, on the other hand, the prevention of the commission of the offence.

###### Art. 905

The penalties incurred by any person, perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offences listed in articles 898 to 902 above who, before any prosecution, permits or facilitates the identification of other offenders or after the commencement of prosecution, permits or facilitates the arrest

of the latter, are reduced by half. In addition, such person is exempted from the fine and, where applicable, from accessory measures and optional complementary penalties.

###### Art. 906

In all cases of conviction for money laundering offences or attempts, the courts shall order the confiscation for the benefit of the public treasury, of the proceeds derived from the offence, of movable or immovable property into which these proceeds are transformed or converted and, up to their value, of legitimately acquired property with which the said proceeds are mixed, as well as income and other advantages derived from these proceeds, from property into which they are transformed or invested or from property with which they are mixed, to whomever these proceeds and property belong, unless their owner establishes that he was unaware of their fraudulent origin at the time of the facts.

###### Art. 907

Individuals prosecuted for the offences referred to in this chapter are subject to extradition regardless of the duration of the penalty incurred on national territory; individuals who, for offences referred to in this chapter, are definitively convicted by the courts of the requesting State without it being necessary to take into account the penalty imposed.

The common law rules of extradition are not derogated from, in particular those relating to dual criminality.

#### Chapitre II : Des violations des règles de la concurrence

###### Art. 908

The following offences are subject to the provisions of this chapter:

1) offences classified as anti-competitive practices; 2) offences against the rules of market transparency and restrictive competition practices; 3) offences against provisions ancillary to the organization of competition.

##### Section 1 : Des pratiques anticoncurrentielles

###### Art. 909

An anti-competitive practice is constituted by the act of contravening the rules relating to agreements and abuses of dominant position as defined by articles 910 and 911 below.

###### Art. 910

All agreements between undertakings, all decisions by associations of undertakings and all concerted practices which have as their object or may have the effect of preventing, restricting or distorting the play of competition in a market, are prohibited, in particular when they tend to:

1) limit access to the market;

2) obstruct, directly or indirectly, the free setting of purchase or sale prices or other transaction conditions;

3) limit or control production, outlets, investments or technical progress;

4) share markets, customers or sources of supply;

5) apply unequal conditions to equivalent services in relation to trading partners, thereby placing them at a competitive disadvantage;

6) make the conclusion of contracts subject to acceptance, by the partners, of supplementary services which, by their nature or according to commercial usage, have no connection with the subject of these contracts.

###### Art. 911

Prohibited under the same conditions as those referred to in the preceding article is the abusive exploitation by an undertaking or a group of undertakings of a dominant position or of the state of economic dependence in which a client undertaking or a supplier finds itself in relation to it in the national market or a substantial part thereof, when it tends in particular to:

1) limit access to the market;

2) impose directly or indirectly unfair purchase or sale prices or other transaction conditions;

3) limit or control production, outlets, investments or technical progress;

4) apply unequal conditions to equivalent services in relation to trading partners, thereby placing them at a competitive disadvantage;

5) make the conclusion of contracts subject to acceptance, by the partners, of supplementary services which, by their nature or according to commercial usage, have no connection with the subject of these contracts.

These abuses may consist in particular of refusals to sell, tied sales, discriminatory practices or imposed price practices as well as in the abrupt, total or partial termination of established commercial relations. Abrupt termination means termination without reasonable notice taking into account in particular the nature, duration and possible economic dependence of the undertaking victim of such termination.

###### Art. 912

The provisions of articles 910 and 911 above do not apply to practices:

1) which result from the application of a legislative text, subject to prior consultation and a positive opinion from the national competition and consumer commission;

2) the perpetrators of which can demonstrate that they have the effect of ensuring economic progress, including through the creation or maintenance of employment, and that they reserve for users an equitable share of the resulting profit, without giving the undertakings concerned the possibility of eliminating competition for a substantial part of the products in question. These practices, which may consist of organizing, for agricultural products or products of agricultural origin, under the same brand or sign, the volumes and quality of production as well as commercial policy, including by agreeing on a common transfer price, must impose restrictions on competition only to the extent that they are indispensable to achieve this objective of progress.

###### Art. 913

Any commitment, agreement or contractual clause relating to a practice prohibited by articles 910 and 911 of this code is null and void by operation of law.

###### Art. 914

Any natural person who fraudulently takes a personal and decisive part in the conception, organization or implementation of practices referred to in articles 910 and 911 is liable to a penalty of imprisonment from two (02) months to two (02) years and a fine from two million (2,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

The court may jointly and severally condemn legal persons to payment of fines imposed on their managers for their participation in practices prohibited by articles 910 and 911 of this code, in accordance with article 53 paragraph 6 of this code.

###### Art. 915

Fines imposed on legal persons are calculated on the basis of a proportion of the value of sales made by each company or entity concerned, of products or services in relation to the offence or, where applicable, the offences concerned during the last year of participation in the offence. This proportion may represent up to fifteen percent (15%) of the value of sales made for the least serious practices, such as simple exchanges of information, and from fifteen percent (15%) to thirty percent (30%) of the value of sales made for the most serious practices, such as a price cartel. This base amount is multiplied by the number of years of participation in the offence, it being understood that a period greater than or equal to six (06) months counts as one year. The fines are proportionate to the seriousness of the facts alleged, to the extent of the damage caused to

the economy, to the contributory capacity of the entity or company sanctioned or of the group to which the company belongs and to any repeat offence of practices prohibited by articles 910 and 911 of this code.

Where the practices referred to in articles 910 and 911 of this code are implemented by a legal person (company or association of companies) under private or public law, the maximum amount of the fine incurred by that legal person is 5% of the highest worldwide turnover excluding tax achieved during one of the financial years closed since the financial year preceding that during which the practices were implemented. If the accounts of the company concerned have been consolidated or combined pursuant to the texts applicable to its corporate form, the turnover taken into account is that appearing in the consolidated or combined accounts of the consolidating or combining company.

The fines are determined individually for each legal person sanctioned and in a reasoned manner for each sanction.

Where a legal person does not contest the reality of the grievances alleged against it in respect of the practices prohibited by articles 910 and 911 of this code, the court may impose a fine taking into account the absence of contestation. In that case, the maximum amount of the sanction incurred is reduced by half.

A total or partial exemption from fines may be granted to a legal person which has, together with others, implemented a practice prohibited by the provisions of articles 935 and 936 of this code, if it has contributed to establishing the reality of the prohibited practice and identifying its perpetrators, by providing elements of information which the court did not previously have, after opinion of the national competition and consumer commission.

###### Art. 916

Repeat offence constitutes an aggravating circumstance. Natural or legal persons who, within a period of two (02) years, have been found guilty of an offence of the same nature as that for which they have been definitively convicted are deemed to be in a state of repeat offence.

###### Art. 917

Notwithstanding the penalties provided for in articles 914 and 915 of this code, the competent court may order at the expense of the convicted person the full or partial publication of its decision in one or more newspapers which it designates and the posting in the places which it indicates. In addition, it may prescribe the insertion of the full text of its decision in the report drawn up on the operations of the financial year by the manager or the board of directors.

##### Section 2

Offences against the rules of market transparency and restrictive competition practices

###### Art. 918

For the purposes of this section, the following are considered as offences against the rules of market transparency and as restrictive practices:

1) practices of imposed price and resale at a loss;

2) non-compliance with invoicing rules;

3) non-communication of price scales and conditions of sale;

4) refusal to sell and subordination of sale with regard to the consumer;

5) discriminatory practices between professionals;

6) wild sales and para-commercialism;

7) non-compliance with the rules relating to consumer information;

8) misleading or deceptive advertising;

9) non-compliance with the regulations relating to direct sales to consumers.

###### Art. 919

The offences provided for in article 918 above, with the exception of points 2 and 8, are punishable by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In addition, the court may order at the expense of the convicted person the publication of its decision in the newspapers which it designates. Likewise, the reseller who requests from his supplier or obtains from him any advantages contrary to the rules of competition is liable to the same penalty.

Without prejudice to the penalties provided for in the first paragraph above, the minister responsible for trade may, in conjunction with the relevant supervising minister, order the immediate cessation of the exercise of the profession on the occasion of which the offence was committed or the evacuation of public property irregularly occupied for commercial purposes.

###### Art. 920

Any professional who sells or resells products, goods or offers services without issuing an invoice is liable to a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Any professional who, holding goods or products for the needs of his activity, cannot justify the holding thereof by the presentation of an invoice or any other document in lieu thereof upon first requisition is punished by the same penalty.

The same applies where:

1) the invoice issued contains false information on one or more of the particulars required by law;

2) the invoice is false or falsified;

3) the invoice does not contain one or more of the particulars provided for by law.

The non-issuance of an invoice, receipt or expense note at the request of the consumer and the non-retention of invoices for the legal period of five (05) years from the date of the transaction are also punished by the same penalty.

###### Art. 921

The offences provided for in point 8 of article 918 above are liable to a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In addition, the court may order the publication of a corrective notice at the expense of the convicted person.

In all cases, the competent administration may, as a precautionary measure, order the withdrawal of the disputed advertising at the expense of the defendant.

The advertiser, on whose behalf the advertising is disseminated, is primarily liable for the offence committed.

##### Section 3

Offences against the ancillary provisions on the organization of competition

###### Art. 922

The act of contravening the legislative provisions relating to the fight against fraud, to warranty and after-sales service; to deception and falsification and to consumer safety is considered as an offence against the ancillary provisions on the organization of competition.

###### Art. 923

The following are punishable by imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, without prejudice to duties and taxes due:

1) any form of transfer of import or export title;

2) any import or export carried out in violation of the regulations on pre-shipment inspection of goods;

3) any import or export without title or without customs declaration of goods, products and merchandise subject to this regime or their determination;

4) any use of false documents for import or export purposes;

5) any transfer of installation authorization cards and the non-possession of the installation authorization card after six (06) months of activity;

6) any commercial practice without a sign affixed to the front of the building in which the activities take place and any affixing of a commercial sign whose inscriptions are unrelated to the business name shown on the installation authorization card.

###### Art. 924

Violations of legislative provisions in force relating to warranty and after-sales service are punishable by imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs and or one of these two penalties. In addition, the obligation to perform after-sales service may be ordered by the judge.

###### Art. 925

Violations of legislative provisions in force relating to deception, adulteration and consumer safety are punishable by imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs and or one of these two penalties.

These penalties are applicable even in cases where the harmful adulteration is known to the buyer or consumer.

###### Art. 926

The penalties provided for in Article 925 are doubled if an adulterated, counterfeit, corrupted or toxic substance harmful to the health of humans or animals is involved in the commission of the offense.

###### Art. 927

Notwithstanding the provisions of Articles 925 and 926 above, goods, objects or devices whose sale, use or possession constitute offenses within the meaning of legislative provisions relating to deception and adulteration may be confiscated.

If the goods, objects or devices have been recognized as dangerous for humans or animals, the authority competent for seizure shall proceed with their destruction or give them appropriate use.

The court may order in all cases that the judgment of conviction be published in full or in extracts in the newspapers it designates and posted in the places it indicates. These measures are carried out at the expense of the convicted person.

###### Art. 928

Anyone who, in defiance of the provisions of an order issued pursuant to legislative provisions in force relating to wild sales and para-commercialism, is punishable by the penalties provided for in Article 924 above:

1) manufactures, imports, exports, places on the market free of charge or for consideration a product or service that has been subject to a provisional suspension measure;

2) fails to disseminate the warnings or precautions for use ordered;

3) does not exchange, modify or reimburse, under the prescribed conditions of place and time, totally or partially the product or service;

4) does not proceed with the withdrawal or destruction of a product; 5) does not comply with the emergency measures prescribed to stop the serious or imminent danger presented by the product or service;

6) does not comply with the consignment measure decided for products likely to present a serious or imminent danger;

7) does not observe the measure of suspension of service provision:

###### Art. 929

The court that pronounces a conviction for an offense under texts adopted pursuant to legislative provisions relating to deception and adulteration may order at the expense of the convicted person:

1) the publication of the conviction decision and the dissemination of one or more messages informing the public of this decision;

2) the withdrawal or destruction of the products on which the offense was committed and the prohibition of service provision; 3) the confiscation of the proceeds from the sale of products or the provision of services on which the offense was committed.

###### Art. 930

The competent court may, as soon as it is seized of prosecutions for the offenses referred to in the preceding article, order the suspension of the sale of the product or the provision of the incriminated service.

##### Section 4: Miscellaneous sanctions

###### Art. 931

Anyone who opposes in any way whatsoever the exercise of the functions with which State officials and agents specially commissioned to draw up official reports are charged is punishable by fifteen (15) days to one (01) month imprisonment and/or a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or eight (08) to fifteen (15) days of community service.

In case of recidivism, the prison sentence is increased to two (02) months.

###### Art. 932

For offenses found in matters of fraud, deception and adulteration, misleading advertising and failure to comply with consumer safety rules, the minister in charge of commerce may order the administrative closure of retail stores and shops for a maximum period of three (03) months.

###### Art. 933

Recidivism constitutes an aggravating circumstance for the offenses referred to in Sections 2 and 3 of this chapter. Those who, within a period of two (02) years, have been found guilty of an offense of the same nature as that for which they were definitively convicted are deemed to be in a state of recidivism.

In case of recidivism for the offenses listed in Article 932 above, the judge may order the temporary or permanent cessation of all commercial activity throughout the national territory.

###### Art. 934

Accomplices convicted of offenses referred to in Sections 2 and 3 of this chapter are punished with the same penalties as the principal perpetrators.

#### Chapter III: Offenses relating to regulated professions and sectors

##### Section 1: Illegal practice of regulated professions

###### Art. 935

Any practice or attempted practice of a regulated profession in violation of the conditions defined by the legislation in force is considered illegal and subject to the sanctions provided for in Article 937.

###### Art. 936

The act of a person usurping a title, function, quality and sign whose use is subject to legal or regulatory restrictions, either by preceding or following their name with this title, or by using, for their name or on behalf of a legal entity, terms likely to maintain in the public an erroneous belief as to the profession practiced by that person or the legal entity, is prohibited and subject to the sanctions provided for in Article 937.

###### Art. 937

Anyone who practices or attempts to practice illegally a regulated profession, usurps it or attempts to usurp it is punishable by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

In case of recidivism, the prison and fine penalties may be doubled.


This excerpt sits under: JOURNAL OFFICIEL > TITRE IX : DES INFRACTIONS ÉCONOMIQUES, BANCAIRES ET FINANCIERES > Chapitre II : Des violations des règles de la concurrence > Section 3

##### Section 2: Offences relating to regulated sectors

###### Sous-section 1: Offences in the craft sector

###### Art. 938

Without prejudice to the provisions of this code, failure to comply with regulations relating to crafts exposes offenders to the following sanctions and penalties:

1) refusal to submit to identification leads to exclusion from enjoyment of the privileges granted to craftsmen and craft enterprises;

2) refusal to submit to registration or enrolment exposes the offender to prohibition from carrying on the activity;

3) false declarations at the time of registration or enrolment lead to withdrawal of the professional card and a fine ranging from one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs depending on the duration of use of the falsified document.

###### Sous-section 2: Offences in the electricity and drinking water sectors

###### Art. 939

Any person who engages in activities for the supply of electrical energy or drinking water in a situation where the legislation in force requires the prior conclusion of a concession or delegation agreement without having validly concluded such a concession or delegation agreement is punished by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

The penalty referred to in the preceding point is incurred by any person operating electrical or drinking water installations, with the objective of supplying electrical energy or drinking water and by virtue of rights acquired under the previous legislation, without having obtained or filed an application for recognition of said rights within the required time limits.

Any person having operated electrical or drinking water installations intended to supply electrical energy or drinking water without having previously obtained an operating authorization for that purpose is punished by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person having operated electrical or drinking water installations not intended to supply electrical energy or drinking water without having previously obtained an operating authorization for that purpose is punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (500,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 940

Any candidate for the grant of any concession or delegation who knowingly provides information that he knew to be false for the purpose of being granted said concession or management delegation is punished by a fine of five million (5,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 941

Any concessionaire or delegatee of public service holding an operating authorization who knowingly obstructs, or attempts to obstruct, by any means whatsoever the exercise by the regulatory authority and the minister responsible for energy or water, of their powers of inspection of electrical or drinking water installations determined by the legislation in force is punished by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 942

Any concessionaire or delegatee of public service who, without justification, refuses to supply electrical energy or drinking water to any consumer having filed an application to that effect in the territory covered by the concession or delegation agreement to which he is a party is punished by a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Any concessionaire or delegatee who, without justification, refuses any other concessionaire or delegatee or any consumer the right to transit electrical energy or drinking water through his electrical or drinking water installations is punished by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

For the purposes of applying paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the existence of a justification for the refusal to supply or transit is assessed with regard to:

1) the legal obligation of the concessionaire or delegatee to supply electrical energy or drinking water in accordance with the conditions stipulated in the concession or delegation agreement;

2) the legal obligation of the transporter concessionaire to allow another concessionaire to transit electrical energy or drinking water through his electrical or drinking water installations, except in case of technical impossibility or lack of capacity to transport or transit electrical energy or drinking water.

###### Art. 943

Any concessionaire or any delegatee or any operator who knowingly invoices any consumer or any other concessionaire or delegatee or operator, any service related to the supply of electrical energy or drinking water at prices higher than those set in the relevant concession or delegation agreement or in the tariff regulations is punished by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 944

The following is considered to constitute an offence of corruption or influence peddling as defined in articles 594 and 608 of this code:

1) any member of the regulatory authority soliciting, accepting or facilitating through his functions, directly or indirectly, offers, promises, gifts or any advantages whatsoever with a view to favouring the grant of any concession or the issuance or renewal of any operating authorization, or the insertion in any concession agreement of more favourable stipulations, for the benefit of the author of the offers, promises, gifts or any advantages whatsoever in question, either to perform or refrain from performing an act inherent in any of his mandates, or to abuse his real or supposed influence;

2) any candidate for the grant of any concession or for the issuance or renewal of any operating authorization making or offering, or attempting to make or offer, to any member of the regulatory authority, offers, promises, gifts or any advantages whatsoever for the purpose of obtaining from the member or members in question one of the advantages mentioned in point 1 above.

The offences provided for in this article are punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine equal to twice the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, provided that said fine may not be less than five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 945

The following constitutes illegal taking of interest within the meaning of article 614 of this code: any person holding public authority or any member of the regulatory authority who, subsequent to the conclusion of any concession agreement, knowingly takes, receives or retains, directly or indirectly, any interest whatsoever in an enterprise or operation for which said concession agreement was concluded.

The offence provided for in this article is punished by a sentence of one (01) to three (03) year(s) of imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 946

Any concessionaire or delegatee who knowingly, alone or in cooperation with other concessionaires, implements or carries out or attempts to implement or carry out any practice having the object or effect of operating unjustified discrimination against a person or category of persons, is punished by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

For the application of the preceding paragraph, disparities of treatment between several persons or categories of persons are considered justified by imperatives arising from technical reasons or reasons related to the capacity of the electrical or drinking water installations of the concessionaire or delegatee concerned or any other reasons that can objectively justify such disparities.

###### Sous-section 3 : Offences relating to the communication and media sector

###### Art. 947

Constitutes the offence of omission and is punished by a fine of ten thousand (10,000) to one hundred thousand (100,000) CFA francs, any failure to comply with the requirements relating to declaration, printing and legal deposit with regard to the written press, to the recording and preservation of broadcast or televised programmes. In case of recidivism, the maximum penalty may be applied.

###### Art. 948

Any contravention of the prohibition on posting private notices in places designated by the prefect or the mayor as being exclusively intended to receive notices and acts emanating from the public authority, is punished by a fine of five thousand (5,000) to one hundred thousand (100,000) CFA francs and, in case of recidivism, double that penalty.

###### Art. 949

Whoever removes, tears, covers or alters by any process whatsoever, so as to distort or render illegible, notices posted by order of the administration in places reserved for it, is punished by a fine of five thousand (5,000) to one hundred thousand (100,000) CFA francs.

If the offence provided for in the preceding paragraph was committed by an official or agent of the public authority, the penalty is a fine of ten thousand (10,000) to two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 950

Any person alone or in a group found guilty of having posted notices or, by any process whatsoever, made inscriptions, drawn signs or drawings on movable or immovable property of the domains

of the State, public establishments, or property assigned to the execution of a public service, as well as anyone who, without being the owner, usufructuary or tenant of an immovable property or without being authorized by one of these persons, posts notices thereon or, by any process whatsoever, makes inscriptions, draws signs or drawings is punished by a fine of ten thousand (10,000) to two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 951

In case of refusal to insert or broadcast the reply despite the summary order, the director of the publication or broadcasting organ in question is liable to a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

A suspension of publication or broadcasting of fifteen (15) days to three (03) months may be pronounced against the organ in question without prejudice to the fine penalty provided for in the preceding paragraph.

###### Art. 952

Is considered as refusal to insert and punished by the same penalty, the act of deleting part of the reply that the publication was required to reproduce.

###### Art. 953

Any owner of a press organ, any director of a publication or one of his collaborators who receives or has promised to him a sum of money or any other advantage for the purpose of disguising advertising as information, is liable to a fine penalty of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 954

Any operation of a radio or television station before obtaining authorization under the conditions provided by law is liable to a fine penalty of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 955

The broadcasting or publication of information contrary to reality with the manifest aim of manipulating consciences or distorting information or facts, is liable to a fine penalty of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, without prejudice to other penalties provided by legislative provisions in force.

In case of recidivism, the maximum penalty may be applied.

###### Art. 956

The director of the publication has the obligation to require all necessary information on the authors of articles published or broadcast.

###### Art. 957

Any person convicted of having lent in any manner whatsoever his name to the owner or sponsor of a publication referred to in article 9 of the press and communication code is punished by three (03)

months to two (02) years' imprisonment and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 958

The sale, distribution or reproduction of prohibited works, the publication or broadcasting under a different title of a prohibited work, when done with knowledge of the prohibition of the work, are punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 959

Constitutes an offence of obstruction of freedom of the press and communication any act likely to prevent the printing or free circulation of the press or to prevent the journalist from freely exercising his activity.

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months with suspension or a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, whoever is guilty of acts of this nature.

In case of recidivism, the maximum may be applied cumulatively for both penalties.

###### Art. 960

When obstruction of freedom of the press and communication is exercised with violence against the journalist, the communication technician or against the press organization, the provisions of this code relating to voluntary violence, destruction and damage are applicable.

###### Sous-section 4

Offences relating to the telecommunications and electronic communications sector

###### Art. 961

Is punishable by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties:

1) establishing, causing to be established, operating or causing to be operated a telecommunications network, without the authorization provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization;

2) providing or causing to be provided to the public the telecommunications service, without the authorization provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization;

3) establishing, causing to be established, operating or causing to be operated a free network or service that does not use the radio spectrum in violation of the obligations of establishment conditions

and the declaration obligation provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or discontinue;

4) establishing, causing to be established, operating or causing to be operated radioelectric networks, installations or terminal equipment without the authorization provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force or in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization;

5) using a radioelectric frequency, without the authorization provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force or in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization.

###### Art. 962

Is punishable by a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, the act of providing a free telecommunications service that does not use the radio spectrum in violation of the obligations of establishment conditions and the declaration obligation provided for by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or discontinue.

###### Art. 963

Is punishable by imprisonment of two (02) months to two (02) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties, anyone who transmits, without authorization, information or correspondence from one place to another, either by means of telecommunications apparatus, or by any other means defined by the legislative provisions in force.

###### Art. 964

Is punishable by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to eight million (8,000,000) CFA francs the act of manufacturing for the domestic market, importing for release for consumption, holding for sale, distributing free of charge or for consideration, connecting to a network open to the public or advertising terminal equipment and telecommunications installations without the approval provided for by law.

###### Art. 965

In case of conviction for one of the offenses provided for in the preceding articles and articles 371 and 415 of this code, the court may, in addition:

1) order the confiscation of unapproved terminal equipment and telecommunications installations, materials and installations constituting the network or enabling the provision of the service, equipment that was used or intended to commit the offense;

2) order their destruction at the expense of the convicted person;

3) order the prohibition from applying for a period of up to two years for an authorization or approval pursuant to the legislative and regulatory provisions in force.

###### Art. 966

The net proceeds of fines provided for by this paragraph and resulting from cases pursued at the request of the regulatory authority are shared equally between the public treasury and the regulatory authority.

###### Art. 967

Is punishable by imprisonment of six (06) months to three (03) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties anyone who, by breaking cables, by damaging apparatus or by any other means, voluntarily causes the interruption of telecommunications services or voluntarily obstructs the operation of telecommunications installations and services.

Any contractual provision between two or more operators, contrary to the above provision is deemed unwritten.

In case of a voluntary interruption or one committed through negligence, the operator victim of these acts may immediately take all provisional measures, in agreement with the regulatory authority, in order to stop said acts. It may claim damages fixed by agreement of the parties or by the courts after expert opinion.

###### Art. 968

Is punishable by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs any person who voluntarily disrupts, by using a frequency, a radioelectric installation or by any other means, another radioelectric service.

###### Art. 969

Is punishable by imprisonment of two (02) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who, knowingly, transmits or circulates by means of telecommunications, false or misleading distress information or calls.

###### Art. 970

Is punishable by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties:

1) establishing, causing to be established, operating or causing to be operated an electronic communications network without the license or authorization provided for by law, or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such license;

2) providing or causing to be provided to the public the electronic communications service, without the license or authorization or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such license or authorization;

3) establishing, causing to be established, operating or causing to be operated radioelectric networks, installations or terminal equipment, without the authorization provided for by law or in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization;

4) using a radioelectric frequency, without the authorization provided for by law or in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw such authorization.

Is punishable by a fine of five million (6,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, the act of providing or causing to be provided electronic communications services subject to a declaration regime to the regulatory authority in violation of the rules defined by the regulatory authority or maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or discontinue.

###### Art. 971

Is punishable by imprisonment of two (02) months to two (02) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties, anyone who transmits, without prior license or authorization, information or correspondence from one place to another, by means of any electronic communications equipment or installation.

###### Art. 972

Is punishable by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to eight million (8,000,000) CFA francs the act of manufacturing for the domestic market, importing for release for consumption, holding for sale, distributing free of charge or for consideration, connecting to a network open to the public or advertising terminal equipment and electronic communications installations without the approval provided for by law.

###### Art. 973

Is punishable by a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs anyone who fails to inform the regulatory authority of modifications made to the information stated in an application for a licence or authorisation.

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) months and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, anyone who refuses to provide the regulatory authority with the information required for the proper execution of its missions or voluntarily provides it with erroneous information.

###### Art. 974

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to three (03) years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties anyone who, by breaking cables, by damaging equipment or by any other means, voluntarily causes the interruption of electronic communications services or voluntarily obstructs the operation of electronic communications installations and services.

Any contractual provision between two or more operators, contrary to the above provision is deemed unwritten.

In the event of a voluntary interruption or one committed through negligence, the operator victim of these acts may immediately take all provisional measures, in agreement with the regulatory authority, with a view to stopping said acts. It may claim damages fixed by agreement of the parties or by the courts after expert opinion.

###### Art. 975

In the event of conviction for one of the offences provided for in articles 969 to 974 above, the court may in addition:

1) order the confiscation of terminal equipment and non-approved electronic communications installations, materials and installations constituting the network or enabling the provision of the service, equipment which was used or intended to commit the offence; 2) order their destruction at the expense of the convicted person;

3) order the prohibition from applying for a period of up to two (02) years for a licence, an authorisation or an approval in application of the legislative provisions in force.

###### Art. 976

Is punishable by a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs any person who voluntarily disrupts, by using a frequency, a radio installation or by any other means, another radio service.

###### Art. 977

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of two (02) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who, knowingly, transmits or puts into circulation by means of electronic communications, false or misleading distress information or calls.

###### Art. 978

Is punishable by a fine at most equal to ten (10) times the usage fee, any person who uses prefixes or numbers or blocks of numbers, without prior allocation.

###### Art. 979

The refusal to implement, to provide or to have provided the conventions, secrets of means of presentation, of cryptology, as provided for by the legislative provisions in force, is punishable by a fine of two million (2,000,000) to eight million (8,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 980

Is punishable by penalties identical to those of the perpetrator, any natural or legal person found guilty of complicity in the commission of any one of the offences provided for in this paragraph.

###### Art. 981

In the event of recidivism, the penalties provided for in this sub-section are doubled.

###### Sous-section 5: Des infractions relatives aux services postaux

###### Art. 982

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of six (08) months to two (02) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties the act:

1) of providing or having provided to the public a postal service, without the authorisation provided for by law or of maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw this authorisation;

2) of creating, manufacturing or issuing postage stamps, without the authorisation provided for by law or of producing them in violation of a decision to suspend or withdraw this authorisation;

3) of providing or having provided to the public a free postal service in violation of the general conditions and the declaration obligation provided for by law, or of maintaining it in violation of a decision to suspend or stop.

###### Art. 983

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties any person who:

1) fraudulently uses, affixes, knowingly removes, mutilates or counterfeits a postage stamp or part of a postage stamp;

2) conceals a counterfeit postage stamp or a mutilated postage stamp.

###### Art. 984

In the event of conviction for one of the offences provided for in articles 982 and 983, the court orders the confiscation and destruction, at the expense of the accused, of the objects of fraud and objects which were used or intended to commit the offence.

###### Art. 985

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties the act:

1) of abandoning, retaining or voluntarily diverting a means of mail transmission, of hindering or delaying its operation or of obstructing or delaying the routing of a postal item;

2) of stopping a mail transport with the intention of stealing it or searching it;

3) of refusing or delaying the routing of mail or the movement of means of mail transmission, routes or modes of transport to which access depends on him.

###### Art. 986

Any person who, fraudulently, takes a personal and decisive part in the design, organisation or implementation of agreements or abuse of dominant position is punishable by the penalties provided for defined in chapter II of title IX.

###### Art. 987

In the event of recidivism, the penalties provided for in articles 982, 983, 985 and 986 above are doubled.

###### Art. 988

The net proceeds of fines provided for by this sub-section and resulting from cases prosecuted at the request of the regulatory authority are divided equally between the public treasury and the regulatory authority.

###### Sous-section 6: Des infractions dans le domaine de l'aviation civile

###### Art. 989

Is punishable by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, anyone who:

1) puts into or leaves in service an aircraft, drone or any remotely controlled device which has not obtained a certificate of registration, certificate of airworthiness or exceptional permit; 2) puts into or leaves in service an aircraft, drone or any remotely controlled device without the identification marks provided for by law;

3) operates or allows to operate an aircraft, drone or any remotely controlled device whose certificate of airworthiness or exceptional permit has ceased to be valid;

4) operates or allows to operate an aircraft, drone or any remotely controlled device in conditions other than those determined by the certificate of airworthiness and associated documents or the exceptional permit;

5) operates or allows to operate an aircraft, drone or any remotely controlled device in conditions of use not in conformity with the safety rules dictated by the legislative and regulatory provisions in force.

###### Art. 990

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties anyone who:

1) operates or participates in the operation of an aircraft without the certificates, in a valid state, required by the regulations in force;

2) destroys or removes a logbook or any on-board document required by air regulations or enters inaccurate information in this book or one of these documents;

3) operates an aircraft or participates in its operation under the conditions provided for in article 989 above;

4) contravenes the provisions relating to the use of regularly established aerodromes;

5) operates an aircraft or participates in its operation under the influence of alcohol, narcotics or drugs.

###### Art. 991

The penalties provided for in articles 989 and 990 above are doubled if the offences provided for under points 1, 3 and 4 of article 989 and point 1 of article 990 were committed after the refusal to issue or the withdrawal of the certificate of registration, the certificate of airworthiness or the exceptional pass, the certificates required of crew members by the regulations in force.

###### Art. 992

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to two (02) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties:

1) anyone who is on board an aircraft in flight without being able to justify their presence by a regular transport ticket or by the consent of the operator or the pilot in command;

2) anyone who does not comply with or refuses to comply with the instructions of the pilot in command for the safety of the aircraft or that of the persons transported.

###### Article 993

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to one (01) year and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties anyone who:

1) transports by aircraft, without special authorization, explosives, weapons and war munitions, carrier pigeons;

2) transports or uses photographic equipment whose transport and use are prohibited;

3) uses, on board aircraft, objects or equipment whose transport is prohibited;

4) uses photographic or cinematographic equipment over prohibited zones without special authorization.

###### Art. 994

Anyone who does not comply with the requirements relating to restrictions on overflight of certain zones is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to six (06) months and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 995

Anyone who affixes or causes to be affixed on an aircraft registration marks not conforming to those of the certificate of registration or who removes or causes to be removed, renders or causes to be rendered illegible the marks correctly affixed, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 996

Anyone who, having been convicted of one of the offences provided for in the preceding articles, commits an offence falling under this sub-section or the same offence within a period of five (05) years after expiry of the term of imprisonment or payment of the fine or prescription of these two penalties, is sentenced to the maximum terms of imprisonment and fine and these penalties may be doubled.

###### Art. 997

Are punished by a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs and may be punished in addition, depending on the circumstances, by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to two (02) months:
